# Cloned Free Games — Tested & Judged Catalog

Every entry was actually cloned, built/run, and judged on real targets — not just listed.
Testing rigs: Android 16 emulator (x86_64, swiftshader GPU) via headless AVD `pm_pixel`,
plus the host Linux desktop (CachyOS, AMD Strix Halo).

**Score rubric (0–10):** buildability from source (2), runs on phone target (3),
depth/replay value ("badd ass" factor) (3), polish/optimization (2).

| 1 | **Shattered Pixel Dungeon** | Roguelike (turn-based dungeon crawler) | **9.0** | Android (arm64-v8a + x86_64 native), desktop (libGDX), iOS | Built from source `v4.0.2`, APK installed, booted to main menu on emulator, steady PID, `evidence/spd-emulator.png`. Flagship content updates for a decade; touch-first UI. |
| 2 | **Unciv** | 4X strategy (Civ-like) | **8.5** | Android (tiny libGDX APK), desktop, iOS, any JVM | Built from source, APK installed, rendered to screen on emulator (`gfxinfo` frame counts, `evidence/unciv-emulator.png`). Full Civilization gameplay in ~20 MB; runs on potato phones. |

No in-game turn was taken. Depth measured from data, not reputation: **3 340 JSON files / 46.3 MB / 58 126 typed objects** — 10 448 items, 7 090 mapgen chunks, 5 597 recipes, 1 286 overmap terrains, 1 226 monsters, 1 076 mutations, 783 constructions, 507 vehicle parts, 49 mods. Split: build 2/2, phone 2/3 (in-tree Android app exists, APK not built), depth 3/3, polish 1.5/2 (dense keyboard-driven UI, heavy RAM). |

## Donor pool — cloned 2026-10-30, assets measured, nothing built yet

Brief: *"lets clone some more to build with, i want something closer to aaa."* These were
cloned for **asset value**, not for playability. **No score is assigned** — the rubric scores
builds that ran, and none of these has been built here.

| Clone | Licence | Measured content | Why it is worth having |
|---|---|---|---|
| **crawl** `cf8c9bc` | tiles **CC0**, code GPL-2.0 | 8,796 tiles (37 MB, 32×32), 144 `.des` files holding **6,127 `MAP`-grid vaults**, 6.2 MB of item/monster/spell tables | hand-authored dungeon geometry — real rooms, not procedural noise |
| **widelands** `29753773` | GPL-2.0 | 17,900 art files (334 MB), 148 ogg (119 MB), **2,314 Lua data files** (tribes, buildings, wares) | Settlers-style production chains — wares, workers, roads, the deepest chain economy in free software |
| **openrct2** `3b7114c` | GPL-3.0 | 10,011 PNGs (17.8 MB, mostly its own UI art), 6.5 MB of data | park-sim systems: guest AI, ride economics, research gating. Needs RCT2 data at runtime, so reference only |
| **flare-game** `af6eee6` | GPL-3.0 + CC-BY-SA-3.0 art | 429 PNGs (1.2 GB sparse clone — enemy sheets up to 6144², 9 tilesets, 42 portrait dirs, 66 menu panels), 128 ogg, **560 item definition files** | the only 2D donor with art rendered from 3D models, plus real item records with damage/price/bonus numbers |
| **wesnoth** `7af37dd0` | GPL-2.0 + CC-BY-SA-4.0 art | 15,852 PNGs (637 MB sparse clone), 7,175 unit PNGs from **327 unit `.cfg` definitions**, 284 portraits, 44 music tracks | a complete rule set written as data: hp, movement, cost, alignment, per-attack damage/number/range |

### Examined and rejected as donors

- **opentyrian** — the GitHub repo is code only (2 MB); its data comes from `get_data.sh`, which
  pulls the 1995 freeware Tyrian 2.1 archive off a third-party mirror. 320×200 pixel art, extra
  network step, nothing the pool doesn't already beat. Removed after inspection.
- **0 A.D.** — `0ad/0ad` is 8.4 GB and its art/audio are Git-LFS objects; the local checkout in
  `~/freegames/work/0ad` is pointers only. Not cloned; it would need `git lfs pull` to become a
  donor, and its art style is covered by Wesnoth and Flare.

| **Endless Sky** (`~/freegames/work/endless-sky`) | GPL-3.0 | **6,944 art files / 348 MB** — 567 port panoramas, 2,020 effect frames, 201 UI icons, 118 ship sprites; 247 audio files |

### The games/ tree

`games/` is gitignored and fully reproducible: `scripts/clone-donors.sh [name ...]` does depth-1
clones (sparse paths where a clone is only wanted for its art or data), records the commit it
lands on, and refreshes rather than deletes an existing tree. `scripts/asset-inventory.py`
reports what a clone actually carries — licence, art by type and bucket, audio, data tables.

An accidental `rm -rf games/` on 2026-10-30 emptied this tree. Nothing authored was lost — the
catalog table, `evidence/`, and the build logs live outside `games/` — and the trees needed
since were rebuilt with the script above.

## How each test works
- **Native Android:** `gradlew assembleDebug` → `adb install` → `monkey` launch →
  screenshot + `dumpsys gfxinfo` frame counts + PID liveness.
- **Web tier:** served locally (`scripts/serve-games.sh` → :8100), opened in Chrome **on the
  Android emulator**, tap/swipe injected, before/after screenshots pixel-diffed (`scripts/test-web-game.sh`).

| 18 | **0 A.D.** (`0ad`) | Real-time strategy (AAA-scale, 2001->present) | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | Linux/Windows/macOS (premake5 + GCC/Clang, untried here), BSD; **no official Android port** (experimental forks only) | **Clone + content audit — no build run.** Cloned from live upstream `gitea.wildfiregames.com/0ad/0ad` at `cedd5e89` onto the shield: **30 099 tracked files, 4.0 GB**, of which **3.6 GB is the `public` mod** — **1 979 simulation templates**, **5 705 art actor XMLs**, **8 600 textures (2.8 GB art)**, **156 maps**, **1 466 audio files**, 73 MB `mod` UI assets, plus a self-test mod suite (`_test.*`). This is the only free project on the list with a genuine asset pipeline at commercial scale — a decade of commissioned 3D art and an in-house engine. | 20 | **AAAAXY** (`AAAAXY`) | Nonlinear 2D puzzle platformer (non-Euclidean / impossible-space) | *_8.5 — desk audit_* | Android (**first-class in-tree Gradle app** `AndroidStudioProjects/AAAAXY`: `io.github.divverent.aaaaxy`, minSdk 23, compile/targetSdk 36 — shipped on F-Droid + Google Play, touch-first), iOS (App Store, `XcodeProjects`), Linux/Windows/macOS (Flathub/Snap/itch), HTML5 build | **Desk audit — no build run** (Go + Ebiten; 207 `.go` = 31 435 LOC, 101 MB clone; documented build is `git submodule update --init --remote && make` with Go **1.27.2** per `go.mod`, plus graphviz/imagemagick/pandoc — over the 90 s triage budget and **no `go` toolchain on this host**, so nothing was compiled). Documented build is CI-only (`.github/workflows/android.yml`: JDK 17 + `cd AndorsTrail && ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`, APK artifact) and the README itself carries **no build instructions** — skipped here, since the 9.6.1 distribution download + AGP 9.3.1 + SDK 36 make-up comfortably exceed the 90 s triage budget. **License caveat:** code is GPLv2, but gameplay content is copyright its authors and "only licensed for use in Andor's Trail" — not reusable content, and `gfxcontrib.md` carries per-asset terms. Score breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (standard Gradle app with CI, unverified here), runs on phone 2.5/3 (phone-native, shipped on F-Droid/Play, not run in this pass), depth 3/3 (a decade of hand-built maps/quests/dialogue), polish 1/2 (dated low-res art, 277 MB of translation assets for a 2D tile RPG). |

Build skipped: `gradle-8.11.1-bin` is **not** in `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists` (only 8.9/8.13/8.14.3/9.x) and AGP 8.9.1 is absent from the module cache, so the 90 s budget would go on downloads; SDK android-36 is installed and network is up, so no toolchain blocker remains. Single locale (English). Caveat: breadth is real but it is an endless arcade shooter, not an authored campaign; no ads, tracking or IAP by design. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (single documented command, conventional SDK-36 app, unverified here), runs on phone 2.5/3 (phone-native + F-Droid release, one-finger — not launched this pass), depth 2/3, polish 2/2 (code-built vector art, 333 tests, F-Droid reproducibility flags such as `dependenciesInfo=false`). |
| 23 | **Accelerrace** (`Accelerrace`) | Arcade endless racer (retro two-lane dodger, HTML5-in-WebView) | *_5.5 — desk audit_* | Android only (`org.asafonov.accelerace`, WebView wrapper: `MainActivity` + `MyWebChromeClient`/`MyWebViewClient`, minSdk 29 / compileSdk 33 / targetSdk 35, portrait-locked, **zero manifest permissions — no INTERNET, genuinely offline**; AGP 8.13.2 + wrapper Gradle 8.14.3; shipped on F-Droid `org.asafonov.accelerace` and GitHub APK releases); the same `app/src/main/assets` tree is a plain browser game with no build step | **Desk audit — no build run** for the Android target, but the HTML5 tier was exercised for real: headless Chromium at a **412×915 phone viewport** rendered it to a 193 KB PNG with **16 427 unique colours** (σ R 42.6 / G 43.8 / B 37.9 — not a blank screen) and the dumped DOM carried live per-frame transforms on the road/tree line nodes with the player car computed at `left: 368.4px`, i.e. the game loop is actually animating. Android build skipped: README documents **no build command at all**, and `gradle-8.14.3-all` (wrapper wants `-all`, only `-bin` cached), AGP **8.13.2** (8.13.0 cached) and SDK **android-33** (only 35/36/37 installed) would all be downloads inside the 90 s triage budget. Counted content instead: **618-line `main.js`** + 314-line CSS + 87-line HTML, **207 lines of Java** across 3 classes, **6 sprite PNGs** (2 enemy cars, house/small house/tree/palm), Reddit Mono variable font under SIL OFL 1.1, **10 changelogs (v3–12)**, zero third-party Gradle deps (`fileTree` only), MIT license (Alexander Safonov, 2024). Controls are real phone controls: tap left/right halves via `document.body` click, plus **`SensorManager` gyroscope** in `MainActivity` bridged into JS over `asafonov.messageBus` (Android-only — the desktop run cannot exercise it). Caveat: one endless mode, score-only, **no high-score persistence** (settings object is in-memory, no `localStorage`), which is the whole reason depth is low despite clean code. Score breakdown: buildability 1/2 (tidy Gradle project but undocumented build + three missing toolchain pieces, HTML5 tier needs none), runs on phone 2/3 (phone-shaped and touch/gyro-driven, rendered at phone size but not on an emulator or dev2), depth 1/3 (single endless dodge mode, 2 enemy sprites, ~618 LOC, scores evaporate on exit), polish 1.5/2 (smooth DOM-transform rendering, fully offline, no ads/tracking SDKs — docked for no persistence). |

Score breakdown: buildability 0.5/2 (standard multi-module Gradle layout, but the checkout is a submodule skeleton with no engine source, no resources, no documented command, and four missing toolchain pieces), runs on phone 2/3 (Android-exclusive portrait touch app with a live F-Droid/Play release — none of it built or launched here), depth 2/3 (a real 16-level engine rated around master strength gives effectively infinite replay plus FEN, eval and three modes — credited from declarations only, since no engine code is in this tree), polish 1.5/2 (piece-set/colour theming, move-animation settings, 3 locales, Fastlane release metadata; docked for home ads, network permissions and stale version metadata). |
| 45 | **BallBreak** (`BallBreak`) | Arcade reflex survival (finger-as-magnet: balls spawn on the screen edges and home in on your touch point; you must steer the box-free path and let the balls smash the boxes without a ball or box ever touching your finger) | *_5.5 — desk audit_* | Android only (single-module in-tree Gradle app, `de.georgsieber.ballbreak`, **minSdk 15** / compileSdk 34 / targetSdk 36, versionCode 10 / versionName 1.8.1, AGP 8.0.2 + Gradle wrapper 8.0, Java 1.7 source level, GPL-3.0; **live on Google Play, F-Droid, Amazon Appstore and Huawei App Gallery** per the README badges); portrait/landscape-agnostic fullscreen single-activity canvas game with touch as the only required input and an optional accelerometer tilt — no desktop, iOS or web target in this clone | **Desk audit — no build run** (score 1 buildability · 2.5 phone · 1 depth · 1 polish). A build *was* attempted and failed, so nothing is claimed as run: `timeout 90 ./gradlew --offline -q assembleDebug` → **BUILD FAILED in 30 s**, `BUG! exception in phase 'semantic analysis' in source unit '_BuildScript_' Unsupported class file major version 71` — the host's only JDK is **OpenJDK 27** and the wrapper pins **Gradle 8.0**, which predates it. Three independent blockers behind that: **AGP 8.0.2** is absent from `~/.gradle/caches/modules-2` (8.2.2 / 8.6.1 / 8.7.3 / 8.13.0 / 9.x only), **platform android-34 is not installed** (35 / 36 / 37.0 only), and `app/build.gradle` declares **`targetSdkVersion 36` against `compileSdkVersion 34`**, which AGP rejects outright — a fresh build needs code edits, not just downloads. The README documents **no build command at all** (it is a player-facing blurb with store badges and a screenshot), so `assembleDebug` is inferred from the tree. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack, which is meaningless here because **every round is generated at runtime**: the whole game is **7 `.java` files / 734 LOC**, of which `GameView.java` is 397 LOC (`Ball` / `Box` / `Particle` / `Vector2` are 20–60 LOC value types, `GameActivity` 260, `Highscore` a 4-field POJO). Rules, read from `GameView.update()` / `startRound()`: each round spawns **`circleAmount` balls**, each on a random point of one of **4 random edges** (top / left / bottom / right) with a random RGB, plus **one box per ball** placed uniformly but retried until its 3×-boxSize clearance rect misses the finger; each ball accelerates toward the touch point at a fixed `0.065` lerp per tick and is destroyed only by intersecting a box (**8 particle shards** per break), while **any ball or box intersecting the finger circle ends the run**; clearing all balls does `circleAmount++` and restarts the round, so **score = rounds survived**, shown centre-screen with a fading number that grows 5 px per frame. So the "content" is procedural, not authored — there are no levels, maps or data directory to count, and no campaign, modes, difficulty or unlock system of any kind. Persistence is real but minimal: a **SQLite `highscore(id, name, date, points)`** created in `getExternalFilesDir()` via `openOrCreateDatabase`, ranked `ORDER BY points DESC` and shown as a **top-6 AlertDialog**; new scores prompt for a name. Presentation/optimization: everything is drawn with `Canvas` primitives in `onDraw` (no layouts, no sprite assets beyond launcher mipmaps — **8 XML res files total and `strings.xml` carries exactly one string, `app_name`**), sizes are screen-scaled (`min(w,h)/83` balls, `/71` boxes) so it fits any DPI, and an **accelerometer low-pass filter (`ALPHA`) shifts the playfield** via `modX`/`modY` for tilt play. Caveats, honestly: the frame loop is `invalidate()` plus `Thread.sleep(30)` **inside `onDraw`**, i.e. a ~33 fps ceiling blocking the UI thread rather than a `SurfaceView`/`Choreographer` loop — the clearest optimization ding in the tree; **zero audio files and zero audio APIs** (0 mp3/ogg/wav), **zero tests** (no `app/src/test` or `androidTest`), **zero localization** (the UI strings "Game Over", "Tap to start", "Highscores" are hardcoded English literals), and the manifest declares **no `uses-permission` entries at all** (`accelerometer` is a `uses-feature required=false`), so it is genuinely offline but also has no settings screen, no colour-blind option and no back-button handling. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (tidy 7-file single-module Gradle project with complete build files, but no documented command and a pinned Gradle 8.0 / AGP 8.0.2 / compileSdk 34 / JDK ≤20 chain this host cannot satisfy, plus a self-inconsistent targetSdk 36), runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-exclusive, minSdk 15 covering very old hardware, live on four stores, touch-only fullscreen canvas that adapts to any resolution; not launched this pass), depth 1/3 (one mechanic, one endless ladder that adds a ball and a box per round, a local top-6 highscore — no content, modes or progression), polish 1/2 (particle bursts, random palettes, tilt filter, SQLite highscores, zero permissions, no ads or IAP; docked for UI-thread `Thread.sleep` rendering, no audio, no tests, English literals only). |

The rules are real JezzBall: a **28×20 grid (560 cells)** with states NONEXISTANT/CLEAR/SAFELY_CLEAR/SAFELY_CLEAR_FINISHED/FILLED/COMPRESSED, ball speed `0.025` with the bar moving at the same speed, **timer `12 500 ticks + 2 500 per level`**, `lives = level + 1` per attempt, score bonus `percentCleared × timeLeft × level` with the win threshold a hard `PERCENT_COMPLETED = 75`, region detection via `Grid.checkEmptyAreas` + `getCollisionRects`, and a **replay/rewind rig** — `RETAINED_CHECKPOINTS 16` at `CHECKPOINT_FREQ 32` ticks with `revertGameStateTo()` — the most unusual piece of engineering in the clone. Modes: **1–3 local players** (`Utilities.createDropdown(this, 3)`) each with their own bar, colour and `filledGridSquares`, against a random **`StupidAIActor`** (20 placement tries every 128 ticks); no campaign, but a level-select spinner up to your highest reached level. There is no authored content to count, by design: one `View` draws one ball of radius `min(w,h)/20`, driven by **two `FlingAnimation` instances (one per axis)**, with hand-written wall reflection inside `onDraw` — each hit clamps the position, cancels the fling and restarts it at `-storedVelocity`, fires `HapticFeedbackConstants.VIRTUAL_KEY`, and advances `counter`. The music is real synthesis, not a sound file: `onWallHit()` walks a **14-entry diatonic ladder** `{0,2,4,5,7,9,11,12,11,9,7,5,4,2}` from middle A (semitone 69) and synthesises a **4-second Karplus-Strong plucked string at 44.1 kHz on a worker thread** — pick-direction lowpass (p=0.9), pick-position comb filter (beta=0.1), one-pole damping at .996, string-tuning allpass, dynamic-level lowpass, peak-normalised to PCM16 — played through a `MODE_STATIC` `AudioTrack`. The glow is a genuine AGSL `RuntimeShader` (API 33+) composed from **signed-distance fields**: circle `d1` blended by polynomial `smin` with a `mode`-selected `d2` (mirrored circle / box / inverted box), giving **3 distinct glow variants** via `counter % 3`; below API 33 it degrades to a plain `drawCircle`. Theming is adaptive rather than hardcoded — ball colour `system_accent1_500`, window background flipping `system_accent2_50`/`_900` by night mode, and a `RippleDrawable` hotspot recoloured to a random 6 %-alpha tint on every bounce. Caveats, honestly: this is **not a game** — its own description says "no levels, no score, no accounts" — so the depth axis is genuinely near-empty (unbounded fiddling with one ball, nothing to progress through or beat); **zero tests**, **zero audio assets** (all synthesised at runtime), the whole UI vocabulary is **two strings** (`app_name` in en + fa), no settings screen, and the manifest declares **no permissions whatsoever** (fully offline, no ads, no IAP, no trackers). Four modes, not one: `enum class GameMode { CAMPAIGN, ENDLESS, ZEN, DAILY }` — `DailyChallenge.kt` picks the day's level with a coprime stride (`epochDay * 317 + difficulty.ordinal * 193` mod 600, "all 600 levels a turn before repeating; no platform-dependent hash"), `EndlessDeck.kt` drives wave runs with persisted `endlessBestScore`/`endlessWaves`/`endlessRuns`, and `ZEN` relaxes the shot limit and scoring in `GameEngine`. Star ratings are computed, not stored by hand: `GameEngine.stars()` compares `shotsUsed` against `threeStarMaxShots`/`twoStarMaxShots` with `require(threeStarMaxShots in 1..twoStarMaxShots…)`. Determinism is the design thesis and it is visible in code — one engine shared by solver, certificate checker and game; shot resolution is a pure turn-based function, so frame rate cannot change collisions, score or RNG. Content counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (correct here — there is no authored level list at all): **24 `.java` files / 1 797 LOC** (core 21 files / 1 658 LOC, `android` 2 files / 98, `desktop` 1 file / 41), led by `view/GameScene.java` 308, `view/MenuScene.java` 237, `view/BackgroundScene.java` 161, `view/TileSprite.java` 147. The board is *derived*, not authored — `Constants.MODE_WS {2,3,4}` × `MODE_HS {3,4,6}` gives exactly **three difficulties: 6 / 12 / 24 cards (3 / 6 / 12 pairs)**, and `GameHelper.reset` reshuffles `i / 2` ids on every play, so even **hard mode draws only 12 of the atlas's candies** per board. Art counted region-by-region from the loaders in `AssetHelper`: `candy1.png` 512×512 divided **5×5 = 25 candy sprites** (hand-extracted from public-domain illustrations by Luisa Midori, credited in the README), `tiles.png` 256×128 as **2×1 = the card front/back**, `icons.png` 4×2 = **8 icons** (play, easy/normal/hard, sound on/off, fullscreen on/off), `candy2.png` 6×2 = 12 used only as floating background decoration, plus 2 particle effects (`firework.p`, `trail.p`) and one explode pool. Audio is the most content-dense part and is genuinely functional, not filler: **15 OGG files** — spoken **`talk_on` / `talk_off` / `talk_hardness0-2`** voice cues that announce sound state and the chosen difficulty (this is how a pre-reader navigates the menu), **three randomised variants each** of `yes0-2` / `no0-2` / `hit0-1` with a no-immediate-repeat rule in `ServiceProvider.playOne`, plus `start` and `end`; **no music track**. Progression is thin by design: no levels, no campaign, no unlocks and no timer — the whole ladder is the difficulty button, and the only record is *fewest pair-attempts to clear* (`Statistics.best`, lower is better, shown against the running attempt count) plus a games-won tally, persisted per difficulty through `StorageService` keys `best0..2`/`count0..2` and reloaded on boot. Polish is where the 5.5 lives: a **240×380 virtual canvas with integer upscale** for density-independent UI; a hand-built flip tween (pause .3 s → alpha .4 at .06 → pause .2 → 1.0 at .1) so a wrong pair visibly flips back; a 12-particle burst per matched card; **fireworks whose repeat count scales with efficiency** (`min(4, 5×matched / (1+total))`, `FireworkItem` + particle layer); a live match-percentage readout; **12 drifting candy sprites + 6 particle items** behind every scene; tiles sized as `min(0.9·w/w, 0.9·h/h)` with even gaps; a pulsing play button on a 3–8 s idle timer; and a two-press exit guard. Launcher icons are generated from `extra/icon.svg` at **6 densities** by the Makefile via Inkscape. Caveats, honestly: **zero tests**, `values/strings.xml` is literally **empty** — every string is inline in Java, so the game is **English-only with no locale support** — no music, the `submodules/marm` asset-resizer submodule is **unpopulated** (only needed to rebuild art), a `System.out.println(bs[i]) // DEBUG` is left in `GameHelper.reset` so every board dumps its tile ids to logcat, and the tree is stale in visible ways: last commit **2023-04-23** ("remove travis, fix Makefile issues and add run & install targets"), and both CI badges the README still shows (CircleCI, Travis) point at dead services. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (clean three-module libGDX layout with documented `desktop:run` / `debugInstall` and `make run` / `make install` wrappers — docked hard because Gradle 2.14.1 refuses both JDKs present here, and AGP 2.2.0, the three tube42 libs and android-26 are missing or retired); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-first, minSdk 11, portrait-locked, touch-only, `WAKE_LOCK`-plus-nothing permission footprint, live F-Droid package — not built or adb-launched this pass); depth 0.5/3 (three board sizes and a best-score record, nothing else: one mechanic, no levels, no progression, no timer, no variants, no multiplayer); polish 1.5/2 (25 candies, voice-narrated menu, randomised match/miss cues, efficiency-scaled fireworks, tweened flips, drifting background, density-independent layout — docked for zero tests, zero localisation, no music and a debug print in the hot path). |

Systems depth is real: **14** `ChipType`s (`EMPTY, SUB, SHR, MEM, ACC, RES, SHL, ADD, NOP, SPLT, DUP, CLK, ENTRY, CPU`) with **10** `ChipUpgrades` (`POWERUP, REDUCE, SELL, SUB, SHR, MEM, ACC, CLK, RES, MAXOUT`) — six of them attack types with distinct cooldown/range/heat curves, plus splitter and duplicator nodes — and **28** `Hero.Type` modifiers in `Hero.kt` (**829 LOC**) spanning chip speed/range/double-hit for SUB/SHR/MEM, heat conversion and reduction, attacker freq/speed/strength debuffs, extra lives, extra starting cash, cheaper upgrades, better refunds, and `LIMIT_UNWANTED_CHIPS` / `CREATE_ADDITIONAL_CHIPS` / `CHANGE_TRACKS` that rewrite the stage generation itself (the README's "more than 25 heroes" is accurate at 28). Polish counted: **179 images** (PCB-and-castle art, chip glyphs, hero portraits, launcher icons at 6 densities incl. `mipmap-anydpi-v26` adaptive), **334 base strings** translated to **de 324 / fr 326 / it 324 / zh-rCN 323** with a `values-night` theme and `generateLocaleConfig = true`, `lint-baseline.xml` and `proguard-rules.pro` in place, `dependenciesInfo.includeInApk/Bundle = false` explicitly citing F-Droid issue 1056. The whole campaign is generated. `lib/domain/use_cases/level_generator.dart` (426 LOC) is the depth of this game: **12 hand-picked 3-colour palettes** (`#E63946/#F4A261/#2A9D8F`, `#06D6A0/#118AB2/#EF476F`, …) × **6 grid sizes by band** — 5×5 (levels 1-5), 6×6 (≤10), 7×7 (≤15), 8×8 (≤25), 10×10 (≤40), 12×12 beyond — with **2 colours until level 6, 3 after**, a mirrored half-board for the first five levels and asymmetric fill afterwards (`fillProb` 0.44-0.56 plus a 0.42 leak-left pass that creates multi-cell runs). It **rejects any level with an empty row or column clue** or an unused palette colour. The generator is not a blob painter: `_countSolutions` runs AC-style line-possibility propagation (`_generateLinePossibilities` enumerates every legal line per clue with a `minSuffixLengths` bound, honouring same-colour runs that may touch) then a **bounded DFS that stops at 2 solutions or 800 visited states**, and accepts a board only when exactly **one** solution exists *and* it took at least `minRounds` propagation rounds (1, 2 or 3 by size) — i.e. it refuses puzzles solvable without real reasoning — with a fallback after 50 seed offsets. Seeds are deterministic (`level*31337 + offset*7919`), and the next **2-3 levels are pre-generated in a `compute()` isolate** so the level list never stalls. Rules engine `colorgram_rules.dart` (198 LOC) computes run clues, checks rows and columns term-by-term, and `suggestHint` returns the first wrongly-filled cell then the first missing correct one. Modes, read from code: **endless numbered campaign** (level select renders `highestUnlocked + 10` slots, gated by completion) and **Random** with **5 difficulties mapped to real parameters** — Easy 5×5/2 colours, Medium 7×7/2, Hard 8×8/3, Master 10×10/3, Expert 12×12/3 — plus a **"Multiplayer" that is honestly not networked**: it shares a `Difficulty-seed` room code (e.g. `Easy-123456`) so two people race the identical generated puzzle, joined by typing or pasting the code. Systems: **50-deep undo** (`_maxUndo = 50`), **free unlimited hint** (flashes the cell 1.4 s, no currency or cooldown), an X/cross tool alongside the palette tool buttons, **long-press-to-cross** on a 400 ms timer, a per-second run timer, per-level **best moves and best time** maps, a total-moves stat, and a **mid-run save** persisting board, move count and elapsed seconds — all through **Hive** (`UserProgressAdapter` + Riverpod providers). Polish counted: dark-only custom theme (`AppColors` `#121212`/`#1E1E1E`/`#FFCC00` gold), bundled **BebasNeue** display font, clue badges that dim once their row/column is satisfied, gradient tool tray, haptics at a dozen call sites with a settings toggle, an in-app how-to-play screen, a reset-progress confirm dialog, and **25 PNGs** — a 5-density adaptive launcher including a **monochrome layer** for themed icons, plus 4 phone screenshots and an icon in `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US` (8 files, one locale, no changelogs). Caveats, honestly: **zero audio** (`find` returns 0 `.mp3/.ogg/.wav`), **zero tests** (no `test/` dir despite `flutter_test` in dev deps), **English-only** with no `l10n`/ARB tree, the *revealed picture* reward is procedurally random rather than authored art so the "hidden image" hook pays off in a blob, `pubspec.yaml` still says `description: "A new Flutter project."`, there is no mistake or rating system so a level is pass/fail on completion, and the repo carries an AI-DECLARATION (`level: pair`, local Ollama + OpenCode, no online LLM) — disclosed voluntarily, which is the honest end of that spectrum. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (self-contained project with a documented `Makefile`, but that recipe builds Godot 2.1 from source with `scons`, which is neither installed nor 90-second work, the README documents no command, and 2.1 editor/templates are absent); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android is the primary preset with F-Droid + Flathub releases, landscape touch controls and onscreen buttons — docked for the 32-bit-only ABIs and because nothing was adb-installed here); depth 2/3 (9 authored maps with escalating density, 3 enemy types + boss, 2 ranged weapons with ammo economy + melee, cage-collection objective, unlock ladder and a 3-sink cowbell shop — but one linear campaign, no procedural mode, no difficulty or endless replay); polish 1.5/2 (81 hand-drawn sprites, 5 tracks, 22 SFX, particles, CRT shader, savegame, 3 locales — docked for the NC-licensed art split, a committed keystore path, arm32-only export and zero tests). |

No build was attempted inside the 90 s budget because the pins are all absent and the README documents **no build command at all** (31 lines: what Cuberite is, an F-Droid link, three useful links, CI badges) — measured: the wrapper pins **`gradle-8.7-bin`** while `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists` holds 8.0 / 8.5 / 8.9 / 8.10 / 8.13 / 8.14 / 9.x and **not 8.7**; the AGP cache carries **8.2.2 / 8.6.1 / 8.7.1 / 8.7.3 / 8.13.0 / 9.2.x / 9.3.1 / 9.4.1 but not 8.3.1**; the installed SDK is **android-35 / 36 / 37.0** against `compileSdk = 34`; and there is **no `adb` on PATH**, so nothing could be installed and no frame is claimed. **The decisive finding is provenance, not toolchain: this repo contains no gameplay.** The README states it plainly — \"The Android app for Cuberite does not contain source code for the Cuberite server. It is designed to download and run compiled builds\" — and the code matches: `InstallService.DOWNLOAD_HOST = "https://download.cuberite.org/androidbinaries/"`, with the zip name taken verbatim from **`Build.SUPPORTED_ABIS[0]`** (`\"$abi.zip\"`, no fallback mapping), fetched alongside a matching **`.sha1`** that is compared against a locally generated SHA-1 (one retry) before `ZipInputStream` extraction, under a **5-minute partial wakelock**. Runtime is `ProcessBuilder("$filesDir/Cuberite", "--no-output-buffering")` in a foreground service whose notification carries the first non-loopback **IPv4** address and is refreshed on network change; `stop()` just emits the server's own `stop` command, with a separate kill path. Boot autostart is a `BOOT_COMPLETED` receiver gated by a setting, storage can be moved to SD card (that option caused two of the CHANGELOG fixes), and the console screen accepts arbitrary server commands. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack: **24 Kotlin files / 2 757 LOC**, dominated by `services/InstallService.kt` **362** (download, verify, unzip, state machine `NEED_DOWNLOAD_SERVER|BINARY|BOTH|PICK_FILE_*|DONE`), then `SettingsDialogs.kt` 266, `CuberiteService.kt` 261, `SettingsViewModel.kt` 258, `SettingsScreen.kt` 243, `CategoryScope.kt` 217, `MainActivity.kt` 173, `ControlViewModel.kt` 150, `ControlScreen.kt` 137, `theme/Theme.kt` 126, `ConsoleScreen.kt` 107. Presentation is thin by design: **22 resource files, 1 XML layout** (everything else is Compose), **8 vector drawables** (`ic_console`, `ic_control`, `ic_settings`, `ic_webadmin`, `ic_notification`, launcher fg/bg/**monochrome**), **5 PNGs** (mostly Fastlane shots), **58 base strings** localised to **de 34 / nl 34 / pt 34 / zh-rCN 51** with `android:localeConfig` wired, and **zero audio, zero maps, zero images that are game art** — the pack's `maps/levels=0 images=9 audio=0` is correct here for once. **No tests** anywhere in `app/src`. Judged honestly as what it is: an infrastructure utility that earns phone points for being genuinely Android-native and touch-only, loses all three depth points because the game it serves is a runtime download (and the server source lives in the separate `cuberite/cuberite` repo, not this clone), and earns polish credit for Material 3, five locales, SHA-1-verified downloads with retry, foreground-service hygiene, boot autostart and a working SD-card path — docked for a decade-old `targetSdk 28`, a 1.6.3 build last touched 2024-08, no minification and no tests. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (small, tidy single-module Compose project with a wrapper and Fastlane already in place — docked because the README documents no command and gradle 8.7 / AGP 8.3.1 / android-34 are all absent offline); runs on phone 1.5/3 (Android-native, minSdk 21, live F-Droid package, portrait touch UI — but it *hosts*, it does not play: a phone running this shows a notification and a log console, and the actual gameplay needs a second device); depth 0.5/3 (zero game content in the repo by design; the shipped capability is start/stop/kill, console command entry, LAN IP discovery, boot autostart and SD-card relocation); polish 1.0/2 (Material 3, 5 locales, verified downloads, foreground-service notification with live IP, monochrome adaptive icon — docked for expired targetSdk, no audio/art, and no tests). |

Mechanics read from code, not from the blurb: the four pickers are concatenated into a 4-character string and used verbatim as a **filename**; the app tries `<code>.txt` for text and `<code>.jpg` then `<code>.png` for an image, falls back to `default.txt` (auto-created with "Incorrect. Try again!"), scrolls to top, and that is the whole loop. `title.txt` renames the activity, `.nomedia` is written so riddles stay out of the gallery, `readme.txt` is refreshed on every version bump, and a first-run dialog copies the bundled demo. Presentation, counted honestly: **21 strings and exactly one locale** (en, no `values-<locale>`), 4 XML resources, **15 PNGs of which only 5 are launcher densities** — the other 10 are store-listing art, so there is **zero in-game artwork** — and **0 audio files** in the whole tree. No tests anywhere. Licence caveat worth stating: **CC BY 4.0** on the code is permissive-but-attribution-required, and the README spells out that the credit must be *visible to the user*, which makes it weaker than MIT for reuse in a shipped app. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (a trivially small, dependency-free single-module project — docked hard because AGP 4.0.1, Gradle 6.1.1, buildTools 29.0.3 and android-29 all have to be downloaded, jcenter must be replaced before configuration, and the README documents no command); runs on phone 2/3 (Android-native from top to bottom, minSdk 14, four wheel pickers are a genuinely good touch target and no keyboard path is needed, live F-Droid + Play listing — docked because nothing was adb-launched here and because `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` + `requestLegacyExternalStorage` at targetSdk 29 means the whole premise depends on storage access that Android 11+ restricts); depth 1/3 (it is an authoring/display tool: 5 demo codes, one fixed 4-digit namespace of 10 000 filenames, a single linear lookup with no branching, no scoring, no progression, no persistence of solved state — all real depth has to be authored by the player as files); polish 1/2 (minified release with resource shrinking, `supportsRtl`, `.nomedia` hygiene, a first-run demo loader, per-version readme refresh, a byte-cheap ~2 MB APK — docked for one English-only 21-string locale, **no audio**, one untamed activity, zero tests, a jcenter-era toolchain and CC BY attribution obligations). |

Authored content is audio, not levels: **22 WAVs / 1.7 MB** in `res/raw` — `a0`–`a9` (female voice) and `b0`–`b9` (male voice), i.e. **20 individually recorded spoken digits**, plus `game_enter` and `number_view` UI cues; digits are looked up at runtime by `getIdentifier(female + rand_num, "raw", …)` and played through a fresh `MediaPlayer` per digit, released on completion. Mechanics read from code: Spoken Numbers runs on a **fixed 3 600 000 ms (one-hour) CountDownTimer** whose tick interval *is* the inter-digit delay, so imperfect-but-honest cadence control — `+0.50`/`-0.50` buttons change `timeDelay` **live mid-run** (increment/decrement re-arm the timer and persist to SharedPreferences), plus a pause button, **binary mode** (`r.nextInt(2)`, digits 0/1) or **decimal** (`nextInt(10)`), and male/female voice. Recall has three touch paths — **reveal one / reveal all / clear** — and Evaluation mode swaps in an `EditText` where `checkCorrectness` walks the typed string digit by digit, paints each character green (`101,195,104`) or red (`255,127,138`) via `ForegroundColorSpan`, reports `Total correct: N`, and writes `HIGHSCORE` to SharedPreferences only when the new count beats it; a second tap becomes the Restart button. Flash Anzan is parameterised: rows (default 5), digits **clamped 1–5** (default 3), per-number timeout and flash timeout (1000 ms each), **continuous mode**, and **subtractions mode** that negates a random ~half of the terms, with numbers generated by `Math.random()*10^digits`, summed into `final_answer`, displayed one at a time by a `Timer` after a `.`/`..`/`...`/`Go!` pre-roll, then a show-answer button (its speech-synthesis switch is built but `setEnabled(false)`). Presentation counted: **7 layouts** (+1 `layout-v21` variant) over **17 vector drawables** (rounded/`circle_button`/shadow/`border_fancy` button stacks, generated from XML state lists rather than PNGs), **2 slide anims**, one overflow menu (`dekadico_menu.xml` with light/dark + info), manual dark mode (the head commit is literally "fix dark mode"), and **26 PNGs of which 12 are launcher densities and 14 are Fastlane store art** — so almost no raster art ships in the app. Caveats, honestly: **22 strings, one locale** — the Fastlane descriptions are translated but `res/values` has no `values-de/es/ru`, so the UI itself is English-only and still contains the untrimmed template leftovers `hello_blank_fragment` and `textview`; `minifyEnabled false` on release with a stock proguard file; no Room/DataStore — plain SharedPreferences for 12 keys; the Spoken Numbers cadence is only as good as `CountDownTimer` on the UI thread with a new `MediaPlayer` allocation per digit; **no tablet or landscape-specific layout**; and the app is a *training drill*, not a game — no campaign, no unlocks, no levels, no difficulty curve beyond two sliders. No build was attempted: the README's entire "Building from Source" section is four steps — open the root in **Android Studio**, install Python 3.x, install ccache, "Run the project" — with the flat statement *"Building from source is strictly supported only for F-Droid build variants"* and *"**Build Host:** Only **Windows 11 (25H2) x64** is officially supported as the build environment"*, so there is no CLI command to time-box and upstream disclaims Linux. **The decisive finding is that this clone contains none of the engines it is named for:** `git submodule status` lists **57 submodules, every one uninitialised** (leading `-`), and 13 of them are the engines themselves under `app/src/main/jni/` — `DoomRPG-RE`, `DoomIIRPG-RE`, `WolfensteinRPG-RE`, `Doom64EX-Plus`, `DOOM64-EX-Plus-Enhanced`, `PsyDoom`, `UZDoom`, `perfect_dark`, `ArxLibertatis`, `fteqw`, `widelands`, `Vanilla-Conquer`, `Classic-RBDOOM-3-BFG` — each an **empty directory on disk** (`ls -A` = 0 entries for all 13); only `AndroidGraphicsLayer/` (7 files: `SDL3VirtualController.c`, `SwappyController.cpp`, `AngleShaderCache.cpp`, `SDK3GyroInput.c`) and `etcpaksources/` (2) are populated. So the 960 MB measured is **`third_party/` 597 MB of prebuilt-ish dependency sources** (icu4c 247 MB, boost 185 MB, ffmpeg 105 MB, angle 25 MB, openssl 21 MB, vpx 16 MB, 44 modules total) plus **`app/src/main/assets/game_files` 74 MB**, not game code. Host pins measured, not assumed: **NDK 30.0.16248370 is installed** (matches exactly), but the wrapper wants **gradle-9.8.2** while `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists` tops out at **9.8.1**, the module cache carries **AGP 9.4.1 maximum against the pinned 9.5.0**, and the SDK has **android-35 / 36 / 37.0 but not the 37.2 platform** the build pins — three downloads, then a 57-submodule init and a 13-engine NDK cross-compile, all outside a 90 s triage. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack: **198 Kotlin files / 22 529 LOC + 4 Java**, split **81 `ui/`, 65 `engine/`, 25 `utils/`, 21 `translator/`, 4 `main/`, 2 `net/`** — so the app is a real launcher, not a shell: a `EngineTypes` enum with exactly the **13 engines**, 15 `engineinfo/` packages each carrying its own Compose settings screen (`DoomRpgComposeSettings.kt`, `WolfensteinRpgComposeSettings.kt`, `UZDoomEngineVersions.kt`, …) over a shared `IEngineInfo`/`IEngineUIController` contract. Engine roster read from the enum and README, not the blurb: **Doom RPG, Doom II RPG, Wolfenstein RPG** (the three Erick194 reverse-engineerings of the old BREW/iOS mobile RPGs), **Doom 64 EX Plus** and its **Enhanced** fork, **PsyDoom** (PSX Doom, Vulkan 1.0 required), **UZDoom** (GZDoom-class, WAD/PK3 modding), **Perfect Dark** (`.z64` ROM, GLES 3.0, ARMv7 supported), **Arx Libertatis** (Arx Fatalis, GLES 2.0 + 3.0, **web co-op across PC and phone** via a Tailscale-reachable fork), **FTEQW** (Quake 1 and Hexen 2 fully playable single-player, Quake 2 with all official addons via yquake2, Quake 3 nominal only, Half-Life 1 explicitly excluded), **Widelands** (full RTS, the one entry with **no third-party assets required** — genuinely standalone), **Vanilla-Conquer** (C&C Tiberian Dawn + Red Alert), and **Classic-RBDOOM-3-BFG** (Doom 3 BFG, GLES 3.2, ETC2 texture cache). Honest caveat that governs the whole entry: this is **legal tier B** — every engine is free source but every *game* needs retail data the repo does not ship (Doom RPG needs a BREW file through the BarToZip tool, Doom II RPG and Wolfenstein RPG need `.ipa` files, Doom 64 needs the Steam remaster, PsyDoom needs a PSX disc image, Perfect Dark a ROM, Arx a retail install, C&C the CNCNet resources) — only Widelands is playable out of the box, so "13 games" is a capability, not a bundle. Presentation and polish counted: **assets/game_files** carries the support payload rather than art — a **3.1 MB `gm.sf2` General MIDI soundfont**, **LanaPixel.ttf 3.1 MB** and **UnifontExMono.ttf 14 MB** (CJK pixel fonts for the Doom RPG text), **`gamecontrollerdb.txt`**, plus per-engine data dirs (`doom64ex-plus` 1.8 MB, **`doom64ex-plus-enhanced` 18 MB**, **`uzdoom_dev` 28 MB**, `doombfa` 3.8 MB, `ArxLibertatis` 3.2 MB, `widelands`, `com.codelobster`); **145 PNGs** in `res/`, **361 strings** localised to **`values-ru`** and **`values-zh`** only; there is a whole **`machine_translator/` module** (21 Kotlin files) wrapping **CTranslate2** + sentencepiece + a Bing translator client, so in-app text translation is a shipped feature. No tests anywhere in `app/src`; the manifest asks for **`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`** plus `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, `INTERNET`, `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` and `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK` — justified by loading game data from arbitrary paths and by Arx web co-op, but it is a broad ask, and the Google flavor is explicitly proprietary (Crashlytics) while only the F-Droid flavor is source-buildable. Honest limitations from the README itself: Vulkan renderers are disabled or unplayable on UZDoom and FTEQW with ANGLE as the workaround, Mali/PowerVR low FPS on PsyDoom and Doom 3 BFG is declared unfixable, Quake 3 multiplayer is non-functional, and ARMv7 Doom 3 BFG will OOM. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (a documented — if Windows-only and GUI-only — path, correct NDK and a cached-Gradle/AGP toolchain one minor version behind, but **zero engine source in the clone**, 57 uninitialised submodules and a 13-engine NDK build stand between this tree and an APK), runs on phone 2.5/3 (unambiguously Android-native and phone-shaped: minSdk 24, per-ABI splits, on-screen Xbox-layout controller overlay that is mutually exclusive with physical pads, per-engine settings UI, texture shrinking to save 200–300 MB, live F-Droid package at 3.5.6 — docked because nothing was built or adb-launched here and the flow needs an external data file dropped on the device first), depth 2.5/3 (thirteen classic engines including three full mobile RPGs, both Doom 64 forks, a GZDoom-class moddable port, Arx Fatalis with cross-platform co-op, and a standalone Widelands RTS — docked because all of that depth is *engine capability* behind retail data this repo cannot supply, and the launcher itself adds no campaign, progression or scoring), polish 1.5/2 (Compose Material 3 launcher, 22.5 k LOC across 15 engine-info modules, ANGLE/Vulkan plumbing, a bundled soundfont and CJK fonts, an in-app CTranslate2 machine-translation module, controller database, Fastlane listing with 7 screenshots — docked for **zero tests**, only en/ru/zh strings, `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, and an officially Windows-only build). |
| 69 | **Dagger: Dota 2 Stats** (`Dagger-Dota-2-Stats`) | **Not a game — a Dota 2 companion/statistics browser** (search a player, page their match history, open per-match stats, compare two players on a spider chart, bookmark matches/players with notes, follow competitive matches and leaderboards, and watch live Twitch streams in a Picture-in-Picture player; data is fetched from OpenDota + the Dota 2 web API and cached locally for offline reading) | *_5.5 — desk audit_* | Android only (`com.nikola.jakshic.dagger`, **versionCode 38 / versionName 1.3.8**, **minSdk 24** / compile+targetSdk **34**, **AGP 8.10.0** + Kotlin **1.9.20** + KSP **1.9.10-1.0.13**, Gradle wrapper **8.11.1-bin**, JVM target 17, **MIT**, **live on F-Droid and Google Play** under that package; single-activity + fragment architecture — `MainActivity` plus `StreamPlayerActivity` declaring `supportsPictureInPicture` and its own `taskAffinity` — portrait phone layouts with **3 `layout-land` variants**, `supportsRtl=true`, adaptive icon in `mipmap-anydpi-v26`, Fastlane `en-US` with **18 changelogs**, full description, feature graphic and 8 phone screenshots); no iOS, desktop or web target; the only permissions are `INTERNET` and `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` — no storage, no ads, no trackers | **Desk audit — no build run, and the one build attempted failed in 7 s.** A build *was* attempted: `JAVA_HOME=…adoptium-17 timeout 88 sh ./gradlew --offline assembleDebug` → the wrapper distribution **did download and unpack** (`~/.gradle/wrapper/dists/gradle-8.11.1-bin` now exists) and configuration then died with **BUILD FAILED in 7s** — `could not resolve plugin artifact 'com.android.application:com.android.application.gradle.plugin:8.10.0'` against Gradle Central Plugin Repository / Google / MavenRepo, because `--offline` and **AGP 8.10.0 is not in the module cache** (8.2.2 / 8.6.1 / 8.7.1 / 8.7.3 / 8.13.0 / 9.2–9.4.1 only). Nothing was installed or launched — there is **no `adb` on PATH** and no APK exists — so no frame or run is claimed. Other pins measured: installed SDK is **android-35 / 36 / 37.0** against `compileSdk 34`; `./gradlew` in this clone has **no execute bit** (harness `core.fileMode=false`), hence `sh ./gradlew`; and a non-offline build would need AGP + the whole androidx/Retrofit/Hilt/SQLDelight graph plus android-34 from the network, far past a 90 s triage. **The build command does exist, just not in the README:** `.github/workflows/pr.yml` runs **`./gradlew build --stacktrace`** on `ubuntu-latest` with JDK 17 — the 67-line README is a player page (features, library list, F-Droid/Play badges). Third-party resolution is unusually fragile anyway: besides google/mavenCentral it needs **`maven { url "https://jitpack.io" }`** for `com.github.nikolajakshic:spiderchart`, `chucker` and `process-farewell`, and a `mavenLocal { url "libs" }` whose `libs/com/` tree is **empty on disk**. It also is **online-by-construction**: `DaggerService.BASE_URL = "https://dagger.nikolajaksic.com/"` and `TwitchService.BASE_URL = "https://dagger-proxy-twitch.nikolajaksic.com/"` are **author-hosted proxies** that the app cannot reimplement locally, alongside `api.opendota.com` and the Steam `ILeaderboard` web API; hero and item catalogs (`heroes.zip`, `items.zip`, versions 4/4) and the leaderboard URL are pulled from `remote-config.json` at runtime, so even the *metadata* is a download. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack: **105 Kotlin files / 6 958 LOC** in `app/src/main`, split **`profile` 30 files / 2 034 LOC**, **`common` 16 / 541**, **`bookmark` 13 / 751**, **`matchstats` 11 / 1 364**, **`leaderboard` 8 / 452**, **`stream` 6 / 309**, **`di` 5 / 252**, **`competitive` 4 / 294**, **`search` 4 / 336**, **`assets` 1 / 236**, **`util` 1 / 88** — a real multi-screen app, not a webview wrapper. **The substance is a local database, and it is counted: 14 SQLDelight `.sq` files / 534 lines** — `MatchStats.sq` 106, `Match.sq` 64, `MatchBookmark.sq` 57, `Hero.sq` 49, `Player.sq` 47, `PlayerStats.sq` 35, `HeroAsset.sq` 31, `Competitive.sq` 28, `Peer.sq` 27, `SearchHistory.sq` 25, `Leaderboard.sq` 23, `PlayerBookmark.sq` 20, `LocalConfig.sq` 15, `ItemAsset.sq` 14 — plus a hand-written `DaggerSchema.kt` and **migrations disabled on purpose** (`verifyMigrations = false`, with a comment that `generateSqlDelightInterface` fails when it is on), so "offline support" is a genuine schema-backed cache rather than a screenshot cache. `MatchRemoteMediator.kt` couples **Paging 3** to that cache, so match history is an infinite scroll over a persistent store. Presentation counted: **38 layouts + 3 landscape variants**, **50 drawables**, **3 menus**, **2 anims**, **2 xml** (backup rules), **69 strings in exactly one locale** (no `values-<locale>` exists, though the Fastlane listing is en-US), **71 images of which 8 are store screenshots + a feature graphic + launcher/web art in `assets/` (2.5 MB)** — so almost no raster art ships in the app — and **0 audio files**. Tooling is the notable part: **Hilt 2.51.1** + KSP, **SQLDelight 2.0.1** with androidx-paging3 and coroutines extensions, **Retrofit 2.11 + Moshi 1.15.1 codegen**, OkHttp BOM 4.12 with a logging interceptor, **Coil 2.6**, Navigation Safe Args, `viewBinding`, release with **`minifyEnabled true` + `shrinkResources true`**, **Spotless + ktlint 0.50.0**, and debug-only **LeakCanary 2.14 + Chucker 4.0.0** (no-op in release) — an unusually disciplined single-module project. Tests are the weak spot and were counted honestly: **3 files, 2 of them untouched generated stubs** (`ExampleUnitTest`, `ExampleInstrumentedTest`), leaving **one real test**, `NullPrimitiveAdapterTest`. Judged as what it is: an excellent *companion* app with zero gameplay — no score, no progression, no authored content of its own (every hero, item, match and leaderboard row is Valve data fetched at runtime), and its core requires two author-hosted proxies to stay alive. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (clean modern Kotlin/Hilt/SQLDelight project, wrapper committed, CI documents `./gradlew build` on JDK 17, and a coherent toolchain — docked because AGP 8.10.0 / compileSdk 34 are absent offline, jitpack and an empty `libs/` mavenLocal are in the resolution path, the README documents no command, and the single attempt died at plugin resolution in 7 s); runs on phone 2.0/3 (Android-exclusive, minSdk 24, live F-Droid and Play listing, portrait touch UI with landscape variants, RTL, PiP Twitch playback, and a genuinely offline-capable cache — docked because nothing was adb-launched here and because the app is inert without network: two hardcoded author-hosted proxies, OpenDota, Steam's leaderboard API, and runtime-downloaded hero/item zips); depth 0.5/3 (no gameplay at all — it is a read-only stats browser; the 14-table cache, bookmarks-with-notes, player comparison spider chart and competitive/leaderboard feeds give it *utility* depth, not replay depth, and it authors no content); polish 1.5/2 (Hilt + SQLDelight + Paging 3 mediator, minified/shrunk release, Spotless ktlint, 38 layouts with landscape variants, LeakCanary/Chucker in debug, CI, Fastlane with 18 changelogs and a full store kit, and only two permissions — docked for a single English locale of 69 strings, two of three test files being stubs, and a dependency on private proxy infrastructure). |

Audio is a genuine composed soundtrack: **63 audio files** including **4 OGG tracks** in `assets/music` (`cimmerianDawn`, `dreadnaught`, `intoTheDark`, `spaceTheatre`) — these sit under **`LICENSE_SOUNDTRACK`, Creative Commons **BY-NC 4.0**, not the Apache code licence**, the one legal caveat worth stating because it blocks commercial reuse of the music. Art pool: **321 PNGs** across `textures/{mapObjects, smallGameObjects, planetStarCommons, farBg, icons, ui/nui, mainMenu, decorations}`, with **57 MB** of engine resources and **6.2 MB** of core assets. Code measured: **564 Java files / 64 526 LOC** under `engine/src` — **`game` 312 files / 34 254 LOC**, **`ui` 47 / 7 870**, **`world` 18 / 2 037** — over a `modules/core` subproject, with **63 files in `game`**, **35 in `game/planet`**, **27 `game/screens`**, **22 `game/console`**, **21 `game/item`**, **17 `game/input`**, **16 `game/ship`**, **15 `world/generators`**. Onboarding is real and countable: **33 tutorial step classes** (`ButtonPressStep`, `BuyItemStep`, `BuyMercenaryStep`, `CheckGunReloadStep`, `CheckItemEquippedStep`, `CloseScreenStep`, `CreateWaypointStep`, …) — a scripted first-run walkthrough, which almost nothing else in this catalog has. Tests are the strongest signal in the row: **32 real JUnit files** under `engine/src/test` (`IniReaderTest`, `PlayerCreatorTest`, `RubbleTest`, plus a full set of `game/console/*CommandHandlerTest` such as `ChangeShipCommandHandlerTest` and `DieCommandHandlerTest`) — genuine tests, not generated stubs. Mechanics read from README and code rather than the blurb: hull + shield hit points with per-weapon-type vulnerabilities, consumable-fed guns with rearm timers, repair kits that tick while idle, ship and equipment purchases with compatibility traps, mercenaries that loot and drop everything on death, asteroid mining for early money, collision warnings and screen-edge planet indicators, a full-screen map, inventory, station talk screens, and selectable keyboard / keyboard+mouse / controller schemes with in-game remapping. Factions are authored, not generic: `factions/{ehar,laani,player}.json` plus a `factions.json` index and per-faction hull and ship sets. Judged honestly on the rubric: depth comes from a shipped item/faction/planet pipeline, code-free JSON content authoring and a tutorial; polish comes from schema-validated content, real tests and a composed soundtrack — but it loses phone points because **the touch build is a different repository** and the documented controls are keyboard/pad, and loses buildability points because the one attempt died on an offline plugin resolution and the full graph needs Terasology's Artifactory. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (documented command, wrapper committed, JDK 17 present, multi-module Gradle with a `build-logic` included build — docked because the attempt failed in 6 s on `grgit` 5.0.0, and libGDX/box2d/nui plus a private Artifactory and Sonatype snapshots all have to come off the network); runs on phone 2.0/3 (a genuinely phone-shipped game — Play + F-Droid package `com.miloshpetrov.sol2.android` — with a modest 6.2 MB content footprint on a libGDX engine that already runs on weak hardware: docked because there is **no `android/` module, no manifest and no APK in this clone**, so the touch build cannot be produced or verified from here, and because the documented scheme is keyboard/mouse/pad rather than touch); depth 2.5/3 (open-ended solar-system sandbox with trading, mining, factional combat, ship and weapon progression, mercenaries and 27 flyable ships — docked only because there is no authored campaign or ending and `systemsConfig` is a small fixed set, not a procedural galaxy); polish 1.5/2 (Apache-2.0 code with an explicit separate soundtrack licence, 22 JSON schemas validating 110 content records, 63 audio assets and 4 composed tracks, particle emitters, a 33-step tutorial, 32 real unit tests, NUI screens and a Steam-released 1.x build — docked for the CC BY-NC music, the missing in-tree Android module, and a dependency graph that reaches a company Artifactory). |
| 71 | **Devinettes** (`Devinettes`) | Riddle / lateral-thinking puzzle app (a static list of authored enigmas in verse: read the verse, type the answer, get Correct or Wrong) | *_4.5 — desk audit_* | Android only (`com.workingagenda.devinettes`, **versionCode 3 / versionName 1.2**, **minSdk 15** / compileSdk 23 / **targetSdk 24**, **AGP 2.2.3** + Gradle wrapper **2.14.1**, Java 7-era source, single-module `app`, GPL-2.0-or-later code with the riddles themselves under **CC-BY-NC-ND 3.0 US**; three activities — `MainActivity`, `RiddlePanel`, `AboutActivity` — **zero `uses-permission` in the manifest**, so it is a fully offline, no-network app; portrait touch UI, `supportsRtl`, full mipmap density set incl. `ic_launcher_3`; **live on F-Droid as `com.workingagenda.devinettes`** per the README badge, and the README states the **Play signing key was lost**, so F-Droid is the only distribution channel; no iOS/desktop/web target, no APK in the tree) | **Desk audit — no build run, and the build failed in 2 s.** A build *was* attempted, so nothing is compiled or launched: `timeout 50 sh ./gradlew --offline assembleDebug` → **"Could not determine java version from '27'"** — the wrapper unpacks **gradle-2.14.1** (cached as `-all`, so no download was needed) and that Gradle cannot read the host's JDK 27 class-file level; the only other JDK on the box is **Adoptium 17** (`~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17`), still far too new for a Groovy-2 toolchain. Behind that sits a wholly retired toolchain: root `build.gradle` resolves both `buildscript` and `allprojects` through **`jcenter()`** (shut down 2021), the pinned **AGP 2.2.3** is not in the module cache (which starts at 8.2.2), `buildToolsVersion "23.0.2"` is not installed (**34.0.0 / 35.0.0 / 36.0.0** are), and `compileSdkVersion 23` is absent from the SDK (**android-35 / 36 / 37.0**). There is also **no `adb` on PATH**, so even a built APK could not be installed. The README (9 lines) documents **no build command at all** — it is a store-badge page plus the lost-key note and the licence split. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is *right* here but for the wrong reason: the content is a hardcoded Java list, not data files. `RiddleCollection.Collect()` builds exactly **19 `new Riddle(...)` objects** (title, verse, author, SHA-1 answer list) and the file carries **42 distinct 40-hex SHA-1 answer hashes**, i.e. 19 riddles with 2–3 accepted spellings each. `Riddle.checkAnswer` is a plain string compare against those pre-hashed answers (answers are **SHA-1 of the typed string**, computed in `RiddlePanel` via `MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA1")`), so the app holds no plaintext answer for anything, and there is **no hint system** — the About screen instead advertises "hints and answers" as a paid donation. Progress is a boolean per riddle in `SharedPreferences("score")`, with a `calcScore` toast and a reset action; long-clicking a list row toasts its author. Code is small and honest: **8 first-party `.java` files / 807 LOC** (`RiddleCollection` 230, `Riddle` 176, `RiddlePanel` 174, `MainActivity` 130, `RiddleAdapter`, `AboutActivity`, plus the generated test stub) with **zero real tests**. Polish counted: **40 images** — five densities × six 24 dp Material icons (`ic_check_circle`, `ic_code`, `ic_email`, `ic_info`, `ic_input_white`) plus a hand-drawn `ic_sphinx` and launcher icons — **7 layouts**, **3 menus**, 3 styles, and **7 strings in one locale only** (no `values-<locale>`), i.e. an English-only UI despite the French-flavoured name. **No audio of any kind** (`find` returns 0 `.mp3/.ogg/.wav`). Single shallow clone at **`4d9ced4` (2017-05-24, "Version bump 11.2 Critical Bug Fix")**, 800 KB, no changelog. Breakdown: buildability 0.5/2 (a tidy tiny single-module project, but jcenter, AGP 2.2.3, buildTools 23.0.2 and SDK 23 are all gone, and the one attempt died on a JDK incompatibility before configuring); runs on phone 2/3 (genuinely Android-native and phone-shaped — minSdk 15, 800 KB, zero permissions, offline, touch-only, live F-Droid package — docked because nothing was installed or launched here, no APK ships in the tree, and targetSdk 24 is far below modern Android); depth 1/3 (19 fixed riddles with hashed answers and a solved/unsolved flag — no hints, no levels, no progression, no generated content, and once solved there is nothing left to play); polish 1/2 (five consistent Material screens, a hand-drawn sphinx icon, full density set, RTL, backup enabled — docked for English-only strings, zero audio, no tests, a 2017 dead toolchain, and a README that openly says the app is stranded at this version). |
| 72 | **Diadem** (`diadem`) | **Not a game — a live-map frontend for Pokémon GO** (a self-hosted web/native client that renders gyms, raids, quests and spawns on a map from a Golbat scanner backend, with filters, area/coverage views, Wayfarer tooling and deep-link sharing; the gameplay lives in the phone game it accompanies, not here) | *_5.0 — desk audit_* | Web first (SvelteKit 2.70 + Svelte 5.57 + Vite 8.2 + Tailwind 4.3 + TypeScript 6.0, node adapter, AGPL-3.0-only), **Android + iOS via Capacitor 7** — a real Gradle project is committed at `android/` (260 KB: `capacitor.settings.gradle`, `variables.gradle`, `gradlew`) and an Xcode project at `ios/` (4.1 MB), driven by `pnpm run build:native` → `build:native:android` / `build:native:ios` = `BUILD_TARGET=native vite build` + `cap sync`, plus a GMS-free path (`pnpm run native:android:nogms` → `scripts/native/build-nogms.sh`); Dockerfile + `docker-compose.example.yml` (mariadb 11.4 with a healthcheck, config bind-mounted twice for runtime and `db:push`) and a Helm chart at `helm/diadem` for the self-hosted server | **Desk audit — no build run** (score 1.5 buildability · 1.5 phone · 0.5 depth · 1.5 polish). No build was attempted inside the 90 s budget: `package.json` pins **`pnpm@11.25.0`** and **pnpm is not installed on this host** (only node v26.7.0 / npm), the clone carries **no `node_modules`**, and the lockfile holds **804 resolutions**, so fetching the package manager and installing is already past triage. The build command *is* documented, just not in the README: the 135-line `AGENTS.md` lists `pnpm run dev`, `pnpm run build`, `pnpm run check`, `pnpm run lint`, `pnpm run format` (the README's only instruction is "run `pnpm run dev`" and points at diadem.malt.ee for setup), and `Dockerfile`/`Makefile` produce `ghcr.io/ccev/diadem` multi-arch images — so `pnpm install && pnpm run build` is the recipe, it just needs the network, MariaDB and a scanner to mean anything. Judged as infrastructure, not a game, and counted rather than trusted: the pack's `maps/levels=0` is right for once, because there is no authored game content at all — the substance is **233 `.svelte` + 340 `.ts` files = 17 684 Svelte lines / 64 960 total lines**, with **289 `.ts` modules under `src/lib`** (map rendering, filters, API clients, auth). **8 page routes** carry the whole product: `(main)/[[map=map]]` (the map, default landing route), `(main)/coverage`, `(main)/wayfarer`, three share targets — `(share)/[directLink=mapObject]/[id]` (one gym/raid/quest), `(share)/a/[area]` (a named area), `(share)/filter/[majorCategory]/[[subCategory]]/[encodedFilter]` (a saved filter encoded in the URL) — plus `(auth)/login/discord/native` and a `/test` harness. It is a server-side app, not a static bundle: drizzle migrations **`0000_fixed_madrox` / `0001_majestic_bucky`** define `session` (Discord OAuth token + refresh token + expiry) and `user` (`discord_id`, `permissions` JSON, `user_settings` JSON), so accounts, permissions and per-user map settings are database rows; the data plane is **`proto/golbat_api.proto`** — gRPC (with an HTTP fallback) against a Golbat scanner's `ScanForts`/`ScanGyms`, i.e. the app is inert without a Pokémon-GO scanning stack the operator runs separately, which is exactly why the docker-compose and Helm chart exist. Presentation counted honestly: **1 017 i18n keys × 5 locales** (`en`, `de`, `es`, `pl`, `pt`, paraglide) ≈ **5 000 translated strings**, **65 real `*.test.ts` files** under `src` (including a mocked grpc-js server that pads a response past the 4 MiB receive cap), Tailwind 4 theming with a swappable `config/custom.example.css` and `config/Home.example.svelte`, launch/splash art under `assets/apple_icon_composer` and `ios/App/App/Assets.xcassets`, and **38 svg/png static assets**. Provenance caveat: this entered the queue from **F-Droid's Games categories** (GAMES.queue line 172, summary "Connect to any Diadem Map") — it is listed there as a map client for a commercial mobile game, so the category filter mis-filed it; all of its data is harvested from Pokémon GO and no world, rules, scoring or progression originates in this repo. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (clean modern SvelteKit monorepo with committed lockfile, `AGENTS.md` documenting the exact build, Dockerfile, Helm chart and a GMS-free Android path — docked hard because the pinned pnpm is absent, install is 804 resolutions, and MariaDB plus a Golbat backend are required before it serves one page); runs on phone 1.5/3 (Capacitor 7 Android and iOS shells are real committed projects and the UI is phone-first and touch-driven — docked because nothing was built or adb-launched here, and a standalone install with no configured backend is a blank map); depth 0.5/3 (zero gameplay — browse, filter, share, coverage statistics, Wayfarer review tooling: genuine utility depth, nothing to play, beat or progress through); polish 1.5/2 (5 locales at 1 017 keys, 65 unit tests, Svelte 5 + Tailwind 4 idioms, typed protobuf codegen, Docker/Helm deployment and launch art — docked for no run and for inheriting its entire value from someone else's game data).
