| 74 | **Doda The Exploda** (`Doda-The-Exploda`) | **Not a puzzle in the usual sense — a toddler/early-reader find-it game** (a field of emoji and symbols is laid out on screen, the header names one of them, you tap that symbol to "blow it up"; 6 difficulty presets from Baby to Adult, two of them timed) | *_5.5 — desk audit_* | Android only (`com.quaap.dodatheexploda`, **versionCode 3 / versionName 0.3**, **minSdk 18** / compile+targetSdk **28**, **AGP 3.1.3** + Gradle wrapper **4.4-all**, `buildToolsVersion 27.0.3`, Java 7/8-era source, **GPL-3.0-or-later**, **zero third-party dependencies** in `app/build.gradle` and **zero `uses-permission`** in the manifest — a fully offline, ad-free, network-free app); live on **F-Droid as `com.quaap.dodatheexploda`** and on **Google Play as `com.quaap.dodatheexploda_official`** per the two README badges; portrait touch UI, `res/values-nl` Dutch locale; no iOS, desktop or web target, no APK in the tree | **Desk audit — no build run (a build was attempted and failed in ~20 s).** The README is **14 lines and is badges only** — it documents no build or install command whatsoever. One attempt was made anyway and is reported as it happened, not as a claim: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17… timeout 75 sh ./gradlew --offline assembleDebug` → the wrapper **downloaded `gradle-4.4-all` and then died** with **`Could not determine java version from '17.0.21'`** — Gradle 4.4 predates JDK 17 by nine years and cannot read its version string; the host has no JDK 8/9/10, only Adoptium 17 and system JDK 27, and the same failure repeats on 27. Everything behind that is retired too: the root `build.gradle` resolves `buildscript` and `allprojects` through **`jcenter()`** (shut down 2021), **AGP 3.1.3 is not in the module cache** (which holds 8.2.2 / 8.6.1 / 8.7.x / 8.13.x / 9.2–9.4.1 only), **`buildToolsVersion 27.0.3` is not installed** (34.0.0 / 35.0.0 / 36.0.0 are) and **`compileSdkVersion 28` is absent** (android-35 / 36 / 37.0 only); there is also **no `adb` on PATH**, so nothing could be installed regardless. The last commit is a 2018 merge (`4885fbf`, 2018-07-13) behind a 2018 wrapper — an abandoned-but-published tree, not a current one. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's uninformative `maps/levels=0`, which is technically right and completely misleading: the "content" here is a **561-entry symbol table hardcoded in `MainActivity.java`** as hex literals, of which **491 are true emoji-plane codepoints (≥ U+1F000)** — animals, food, vehicles, faces — with the remainder in the BMP symbol blocks; the searcher picks from that table at runtime, so there are **no level files and no data directory anywhere**. The game loop is read from code, not the blurb: `Mode.java` is a 6-value enum carrying the preset — **Baby (3 icons, no timer, 0.5 overlap, unlimited hints), Toddler (7, no timer, 1.0 overlap), Child (25, no timer, 15 hints), Child Timed (25, 120 s, 15 hints), Adult (50, no timer, 5 hints), Adult Timed (50, 120 s, 5 hints)** — where `overlap` controls how much icons are allowed to cover each other (Babies get none) and `getIconSize()` clamps icon width to `max(width/16, min(width/numIcons, 100))` dp so a 50-icon Adult board still fits a phone. Scoring is three-tracked, not a single number: `score_found` prints `found/total` plus a running score, timed modes add a **time bonus** and a **hint bonus**, a wrong tap is a **`-5 seconds`** penalty with a `miss` sound, and the completion screen is driven by `showLevelComplete()`; hints are free highlight pulses on a **2 s / 5 s / 30 s** schedule (`scheduleHint` re-arms only if the target has not changed), and the number of hints is shared across the whole 25- or 50-icon round. Presentation is the strongest part and is countable: **42 drawables = 25 background JPEGs (`back1`…`back30`) + 16 explosion PNGs + `doda.png`** plus an `explosion.xml`, so the player can turn on **"Use background image (extra points)"** — the explicit handicap that makes Adult mode genuinely hard by putting bright emoji over a photo instead of a flat field — and **13 OGGs** in `res/raw` (`dodadrum` loop, `miss1`–`miss4`, `plode1`–`plode6`, `plode_f`, `plode_squeak1`) played through a **`SoundPool`** (5 streams, `STREAM_MUSIC`) rather than a `MediaPlayer` per hit, with per-shot **random pitch jitter** (`speed + getRandHundreth()`) and a randomised pick among the plode variants, which is the right way to avoid machine-gun monotony in a game whose entire feedback is an explosion. Code is small and legible: **7 first-party `.java` files / 2 042 LOC** (`MainActivity` 1 110, `DodaView` 393 — the custom layout that places and hit-tests the symbol field — `SoundEffects` 183, `EntryActivity` 112, `Mode` 101, `SplashActivity` 80, `AboutActivity` 63), **4 layouts**, **3 `res/anim`** (`hint`, `not_it`, `was_it` — wrong and right answers are visually distinguished, not just a buzzer), full mipmap density set, and **26 strings translated into Dutch (`values-nl`) at 26/26**, which is unusual at this size. No tests anywhere. Judged as what it is: a well-made, deliberately tiny educational toy for pre-readers that teaches symbol matching and tolerance of overlapping clutter — but it has no campaign, no unlocks and nothing to return to once the 50-icon Adult board is beaten. Breakdown: buildability 0.5/2 (dependency-free single-module project and a committed wrapper — docked to near-zero because the README documents no command, the one attempt died on a nine-year-old Gradle meeting JDK 17, and jcenter, AGP 3.1.3, buildTools 27.0.3 and SDK 28 are all gone); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-native and phone-shaped from top to bottom: minSdk 18, zero permissions, fully offline, no ads, touch-only, icon sizing derived from screen width, `SoundPool` low-latency audio, live F-Droid and Play listings — docked only because nothing was built or adb-launched here); depth 1/3 (six difficulty presets, a hint economy, timed scoring with time/hint bonuses and a miss penalty, and 561 distinct symbols behind 25 hand-picked backgrounds — but no progression, no levels, no unlocks, and no generated variety beyond re-shuffling the same table); polish 1.5/2 (16-frame explosion art, `hint`/`not_it`/`was_it` animations, 13 pitched-random SFX, a full Dutch locale, a splash + about + entry flow, and an explicit harder "use background image" mode — docked for a 2018 dead toolchain, `minifyEnabled false`, English+Dutch only, zero tests, and a README that is two store badges).
| 75 | **Doors of Doom** (`Doors-of-Doom`) | Turn-based dungeon crawler / roguelite (one door per turn: open it, fight whatever is behind it with a single weapon swing or flee, grab the loot, spend gold on keys and potions, craft gear; 5 equipment slots and a level ladder that spawns a unique boss at a fixed depth) | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **Omicron-engine game (Java 8 bytecode, Maven), engine supplied as a git submodule** — Android via the in-tree `pom.xml` pipeline (live on **F-Droid as `com.github.msx80.doorsofdoom`** and Play per README badges, plus a **Flathub** flatpak manifest at `flatpak/`), desktop via `mvn clean compile exec:exec` (the README's own command) or a `doorsofdoom.omicron` cartridge, and a **playable web build at msx80.github.io/DoorsOfDoomOmicron**; `omicron.properties` fixes a **240×136 virtual screen, `FILL_SIDE` scaling, landscape**, so one 8-bit-style canvas letterboxes to any phone; no iOS target, no APK and no `android/` module in the clone (the engine generates it) | **Desk audit — no build run** (score 1 buildability · 2.5 phone · 2 depth · 1.5 polish). Nothing was compiled or launched, and the blocker is structural, not a timeout: the README's documented desktop command is `mvn clean compile exec:exec`, but **`mvn` is not installed on this host** (`command -v mvn` empty, only `java`/`javac` 27), the **`Omicron/` submodule directory is empty (0 bytes)** — `.gitmodules` points at `https://github.com/msx80/Omicron.git` and the game's `pom.xml` depends on `com.github.msx80.omicron:desktop-lib:0.0.12`, so the engine has to be checked out and `mvn install`ed before the game can even configure — and **`~/.m2` holds no omicron artifacts at all** (`find ~/.m2 -iname '*omicron*'` empty). Installing Maven plus fetching the engine is well outside a 90 s triage pass, so no build was attempted. The CI workflow does document the real recipe, which is worth recording: `.github/workflows/build.yml` checks out with `submodules: 'true'`, sets up **JDK 17 (temurin)**, runs `cd doorsofdoom && mvn --batch-mode clean install package`, and uploads `doorsofdoom.omicron`; `pom.xml` has separate `cartridge` (default, jar-with-dependencies renamed by `copy-rename-maven-plugin`) and `desktop` profiles, version **0.0.10** with the comment that the incremental version becomes the Android `versionCode`. Counted content in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is meaningless because **there are no level files — the dungeon is generated turn by turn**: **44 `MonsterDef` enum entries** (MOUSE, SNAKE, KOBOLD, BAT, SKELETON, CRAB … DRAGON, with **110 `Loot.of` drop rows** carrying probability/quantity ranges), **78 `Item` enum entries** (the README says "64 items" — the tree has more), **32 `Craft.of` recipes** in `Craft.ALL` (keys at `KEY_COST 15` and 5-packs, potions, dart-from-venom, elixir-from-blood, leather caps, the Flaming/Ghost/Venom→GoldenSword chain), **10 `Effect` values** (MADNESS ticks self-damage, MAGNETIC maxes gold), and loot is genuinely ranged — each monster's own `Range` sets hp/attack/level-band/gold. Assets: **18 `.wav` SFX, 2 `.mp3` music tracks, 9 sprite sheets** (`sheet1`–`sheet9`) plus a `file1.bin` data blob and `omicronIcon.png` (33 resource files, 14 MB clone). Code: **35 first-party Java files / 4 377 LOC**, `DoorsOfDoom.java` 1 206, `MonsterDef` 481, `Item` 364, plus an own animation layer (`anim/Animation`, `AnimationManager`, `Easing`). Real tests exist and are small but honest: **2 JUnit 5 files / 143 LOC / 5 assertions** (`DoorsOfDoomTest` — loot-distribution statistics with a seeded `Random`; `DumpableTest` — save round-trip). Mechanics read from code, not the blurb: `openDoor()` advances `run.level` and picks from the monster pool filtered by each def's level band, with **one guaranteed unique boss per level** (`true, // unique` enforced at construction) — the **DRAGON** drops the **Dungeon Map** at 1 %, and using it calls `exitDungeon()` for a **5 000-point `EXIT_BONUS`** and a real **"YOU WON!!"** screen, so the run has an ending rather than being endless; the **Crown** is worth `CROWN_POINT 200` each and drops at 4 %, and `Run.score()` folds crowns, kills and level into the final number. Beyond `LEVEL_MONSTER_GROWTH 160` monsters scale `delta = (level-160)/50.0`, i.e. double strength every 50 levels. Persistence is the strongest engineering here: the whole `Run` is serialised to a **string** (`Dumpable.dumpToString`, versioned — the comment admits the version field was bolted on late) into `Sys.mem("savestate")` on suspend and restored by a **"Continue!"** menu action, so a phone kill mid-run resumes the exact monster, gold, inventory and effect timers. Presentation/options: sound, music, cursor and **fast-animation** toggles, easing-driven floating damage text, `shake` on both player and monster hit, high-score store, F-Droid Fastlane listing with 4 phone screenshots, Apache-2.0 (music credited to RandomMind / Juhani Junkala / Vincent Fasano). Caveats, honestly: **no build, no launch and no frame claim** this pass; the README documents Maven but assumes you have it and never mentions the submodule; the engine's Android module is invisible from this clone, so minSdk/targetSdk cannot be quoted; the UI vocabulary is English-only and the "How to run" section points players at GitHub Actions artifacts rather than releases; and the game is a **port of a TIC-80 cart**, so its 240×136 canvas and 64-colour-ish palette are a deliberate ceiling, not an accident. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (clean single-module Maven project with two working profiles and a CI file that names the exact command on JDK 17 — docked because Maven is absent, the Omicron submodule is empty, no engine artifacts are cached, and nothing was verified); runs on phone 2.5/3 (genuinely phone-shipped — F-Droid package, Flathub, landscape 240×136 scaled to fill, touch drive of a single-step action menu, live web build — docked because nothing was adb-launched or rendered here and the Android target lives in the absent engine); depth 2/3 (44 monsters with authored stat and loot curves, 78 items, 32 recipes, 5 equip slots, key/potion economy, level-banded spawns with guaranteed uniques, a real win item and resumable runs — docked because every turn is still the same open/fight/flee choice and there is no map, no branching dungeon and no meta-progression between runs); polish 1.5/2 (own easing animation layer, 18 SFX + 2 composed tracks, suspend/resume serialisation, fast-anim and cursor toggles, hand-drawn 9-sheet pixel art, 5 real assertions — docked for no audio/volume screen beyond toggles, English only, no localization, and the missing build path). |

| 76 | **Dooz** (`Dooz`) | Tic-tac-toe (pen-and-paper classic — 3×3 up to 7×7, PvP or vs a strategy AI) | *_6.0 — desk audit_* | Android only (multi-module Compose app `:app` + `:common` + `:feature_game` + `:feature_settings` + `:feature_about`, `io.github.yamin8000.dooz`, **minSdk 24** / compile+targetSdk **37**, versionCode 24 / versionName **1.0.24**, **AGP 9.3.1** + Kotlin **2.4.10** + Compose BOM 2026.06.01 + Material3 1.4.0 + Hilt 2.60.1 + KSP 2.3.10, Gradle wrapper **9.6.1**, Java/Kotlin target 17, GPL-3.0; **live on F-Droid** as `io.github.yamin8000.dooz` with a full `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US` listing incl. 3 phone screenshots); no desktop, web or iOS target | **Desk audit — no build run, and the one build attempt died at plugin resolution.** `JAVA_HOME=…adoptium-17 timeout 80 sh gradlew --offline -q assembleDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 9s`**: `Plugin [id: 'com.android.application', version: '9.3.1'] was not found` in Gradle Central Plugin Repository / Google / MavenRepo. The wrapper's **gradle-9.6.1-bin dist is cached** (hence reaching configuration in 9 s) and the **AGP 9.3.1 library directory sits in `~/.gradle/caches`**, but the plugin **marker** artifact `com.android.application.gradle.plugin:9.3.1` is not, and `--offline` forbids fetching it; Compose BOM 2026.06.01, Hilt 2.60.1 and KSP are **absent** from the module cache too, so even a successful configuration would have hit dependency resolution. Behind that: `gradlew` ships **non-executable** (`-rw-r--r--`), which is why CI's first step is `chmod +x gradlew`, and the README documents **no build command at all** ("Usage — Just play it!"); the only recipe is `.github/workflows/android.yml` (`./gradlew build`, JDK 17, gradle cache) with `release.yml` on JDK 21. Counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is literally correct and meaningless because the content is code: **68 Kotlin files / 4 524 LOC**, zero Java, **zero test sources** (no `src/test`, no `src/androidTest`, no `*Test*.kt`). `enum class GameType { Simple }` is the **only variant**, board size is a runtime setting 3×3–7×7, and the depth lives in `SimpleGameAi.kt` (**289 LOC**): Hard plays the Crowley–Siegler child-strategy ladder the README cites — **Win → Block → Fork → Block Fork → Center → Corner → Side** — Easy picks a random empty cell, Medium coin-flips between the two per move; `SimpleGameLogic.kt` does n×n win detection. Modes and options are real enums, not UI strings: `GamePlayersType { PvP, PvC }`, `FirstPlayerPolicy { DiceRolling, HumanFirst }`, `AiDifficulty { Easy, Medium, Hard }`, `ThemeSetting { Dark, Light, System }`, editable player names, all persisted through **DataStore + Hilt** (`SettingsDataStoreRepository`, `SettingsKeys`). Presentation: Material3 with **dynamic color**, one bundled Persian face (`samimbold.ttf`), **6 dice-face vector icons** (`ic_dice_1..6`), **5 SFX** (`dice.mp3`, `pencil.mp3`, `win.wav`, `lose.wav`, `draw.wav` — no music), **3 locales** (en 54 strings, fa 51, ja 54) with a `LocaleUtils` runtime language switch, adaptive + monochrome launcher icon. Privacy/quality: **`VIBRATE` is the only permission**, no INTERNET, `allowBackup=false` plus `data_extraction_rules`, release **does** run R8 (`isMinifyEnabled`/`isShrinkResources` with `proguard-android-optimize.txt`), `dependenciesInfo` stripped from APK and bundle. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was compiled or adb-launched**, the README claims **SDK 21+** while `minSdk` is **24**, and this is one pen-and-paper mechanic with no campaign, unlocks or progression. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (clean five-module Gradle build with a version catalog and CI running `./gradlew build` on JDK 17 — docked because the README documents no command, an offline build fails on the AGP plugin marker with Compose/Hilt/KSP uncached, and the committed wrapper is not executable); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-exclusive Compose app, minSdk 24, touch-first, live F-Droid release at 1.0.24, one benign permission, fully offline — not launched this pass); depth 1/3 (7 board sizes × 3 AI difficulties × PvP/PvC, a cited strategy AI and a dice-roll opening — but a single fixed mechanic, no unlocks, no meta-progression); polish 1.5/2 (Material3 dynamic color, Hilt/DataStore settings, 3 locales including a Persian-first naming scheme, 5 SFX, R8 release, full Fastlane listing — docked for zero tests and no music). |

| 77 | **Dots-n-Boxes** (`Dots-n-Boxes`, a.k.a. Käsekästchen / Kaesekaestchen) | Puzzle (classic pen-and-paper Dots and Boxes: draw a line, complete a box, most boxes wins — 3×4 up to 6×9 grids, vs a heuristic computer or two players on one device) | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | Kotlin Multiplatform + Compose, three targets from one codebase: **Android** (`androidApp/`, `de.stefan_oltmann.kaesekaestchen`, **minSdk 26** / compile+targetSDK **36**, live on **Google Play** per README badge), **desktop JVM** (`:app:run`, `createDistributable`, `createMsix` for Windows) and **web WASM** (`:app:wasmJsBrowserDevelopmentRun`); Kotlin **2.4.10**, Gradle Kotlin DSL with a committed wrapper and a `libs.versions.toml`, GPL-3.0 (LICENSE is the verbatim GPLv3 text); no iOS target | **Desk audit — no build run, and the one attempt died in 4 s on an uncached plugin.** `timeout 90 sh ./gradlew :app:jvmTest --offline -q` → **BUILD FAILED in 4s**: `Plugin [id: 'org.jetbrains.kotlin.multiplatform', version: '2.4.10', apply: false] was not found` — the plugin *marker* artifact (`org.jetbrains.kotlin.multiplatform.gradle.plugin:2.4.10`) is absent from `~/.gradle/caches` and `--offline` forbids fetching it; Compose Multiplatform is uncached too, so even a successful configure would have hit resolution. Worth stating separately: `gradlew` ships **non-executable** (`-rw-r--r--`), so every command needs `sh ./gradlew` — a clone artefact, not an upstream bug. The README *does* document the recipe (**9 commands**: `:app:jvmTest`, `:app:run`, `:androidApp:assembleDebug`, `:app:wasmJsBrowserDevelopmentRun`, `:app:createDistributable`, `:app:createMsix`, `:app:detekt`, `:app:recordScreenshots`, `:app:verifyScreenshots`), which puts it ahead of most rows in this catalog. **No `adb` on PATH**, so no emulator install was possible either; no frame is claimed. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literally true, meaningless — a Dots and Boxes board is generated, not authored): **56 Kotlin files / 6 008 LOC** across `commonMain` + `androidMain` + `jvmMain` + `wasmJsMain` + tests, split into a genuinely layered architecture — `model/` (`Board` 281 LOC, `BoardSize`, `Box`, `Line`, `Player`, `GameMode`), `controller/` (`GameLogic` 255 LOC, `PlayerManager`, `GameLogicCallback`), `data/StartScreenState`, and **17 UI files** (`BoardView`, `BoardGeometry`, `GameScreen`, `ScoreboardScreen`, `StartScreen`, `AppButton`/`AppSlider`/`AppFooter`/`OwnerSymbols`, a `theme/` package). Content read from enums, not the blurb: `BoardSize` has exactly **4 presets — VERY_SMALL(3,4), SMALL(4,5), MEDIUM(4,6), LARGE(6,9)** i.e. **12 to 54 boxes**; `GameMode` is **SINGLE_PLAYER / MULTI_PLAYER**. The opponent is an honest but shallow heuristic, not a search: `Board.findGoodLineForComputerMove(Random)` first closes every box it can (`closeAllPossibleBoxes`), then samples random lines **that do not hand the opponent a box**, with a random fallback — no chain/sacrifice lookahead, no difficulty setting. Testing is the standout: **50 `@Test` cases** over **10 test files** — 43 in `commonTest` (`GameLogicTest` 10, `BoardTest` 9, `BoardGeometryTest` 8, `StartScreenStateTest` 6, `PlayerManagerTest` 5, `BoardAiTest` 5) plus **7 in `jvmTest`** including **Compose golden-screenshot tests** (`StartScreenScreenshotTest`, `GameScreenScreenshotTest`, `ScoreboardScreenScreenshotTest`) and a `BoardInteractionTest`, i.e. the repo enforces pixel parity against recorded `recordScreenshots`/`verifyScreenshots` targets. Presentation counted: **9 handcrafted SVG icons** in `iconResources/` (`cheese-symbol`, `cheese-trophy`, `mouse-symbol`, `mouse-trophy`, `play`, `logo`, `arrow-back`, `person`, `group`) compiled to Compose `ImageVector`s at build time by the **Valkyrie** Gradle plugin, a generated launcher-icon set plus `ic_launcher-playstore.png`, and **12 strings × 2 locales** (German default + `values-en`) — so the UI vocabulary is tiny by design. **Zero audio files** and no third-party runtime dependency beyond Compose. Privacy is a real feature here: **no `uses-permission` entries at all** — no network, no storage, no tracking, fully offline. Caveats, honestly: nothing was compiled or launched; the game is a **single referee-free mechanic** with no campaign, unlocks, progression or scoring meta, and the AI cannot be tuned; the desktop, Windows-MSIX and WASM web targets are documented but unexercised here. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (clean, well-factored KMP project with a committed wrapper, version catalog and nine documented commands — docked because the Kotlin 2.4.10 multiplatform plugin marker and Compose MP are uncached so the offline build fails in 4 s, and the wrapper is not executable); runs on phone 2.5/3 (real Android app at minSdk 26 / target 36, Compose touch UI, live Play Store listing, zero permissions, and the same code also ships as desktop and WASM — docked only because nothing was adb-launched here); depth 1.5/3 (4 board sizes from 12 to 54 boxes, PvP and vs-computer, correct box-chain capture logic and a non-suicidal move picker — but one fixed mechanic, one shallow AI with no difficulty tiers, and no campaign or meta-progression); polish 2/2 (handcrafted cheese-and-mouse SVG art vectorised at build time, DE+EN localisation, 50 tests including Compose golden screenshots, Detekt wired in, no permissions, no tracking, no ads) — docked only in the total, not the category, for shipping **no audio** and a 12-string UI. |
| 78 | **DroidFish** (`DroidFish`) | Chess GUI + analysis workstation (full UCI front-end: play a human or engine on the same device, 6 game modes, live engine analysis with PV/score/depth, editable PGN game tree with variations and comments, ECO opening names, opening books, Syzygy/Gaviota endgame tablebases, and pluggable external engines — bundled Stockfish 16.1 and a deliberately weak CuckooChess) | *_8.0 — desk audit_* | Android only (`org.petero.droidfish`, **versionCode 100 / versionName 1.90**, **minSdk 16** / compile+targetSdk **28**, AGP **7.1.1** + Gradle wrapper **7.2-bin**, Java 8 source level, **NDK `ndkBuild`** for the native engine, GPL-3.0; phone-first UI with **28 portrait + 5 landscape layouts**, left/right drawer menus, tap-then-tap or drag-to-move, and a piece-set picker; live on **F-Droid as `org.petero.droidfish`** per the README badge); no iOS, desktop or web target, and **no APK in the tree** (the build produces the Stockfish binaries into `assets/` itself) | **Desk audit — no build run, and the one attempt died on the uncached Android plugin.** `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-amd64-linux.2 timeout 55 sh ./gradlew --offline -q :DroidFishApp:assembleDebug` → **BUILD FAILED in 45 s**: `Could not resolve com.android.tools.build:gradle:7.1.1 … No cached version available for offline mode` (the host's AGP cache holds 8.2.2 / 8.6.1 / 8.7.x / 8.13.x / 9.2–9.4.1 only), and behind that sits `compileSdkVersion 28` while the installed SDK carries **android-35 / 36 / 37.0 only**, the wrapper's **`gradle-7.2-bin` dist is not in `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists`** (2.14.1 through 9.8.1, no 7.2), and the module needs **`ndkBuild`** to cross-compile 103 C++ files — no `ndk-build` and no `adb` on PATH. That is a three-download, multi-minute build, outside a 90 s triage, so nothing is claimed as compiled or launched. The README is a 700+ line **user manual** generated into `doc/droidfish_manual.pdf` by `pandoc`, and it documents **no build command at all** — the build logic lives entirely in Gradle: `buildEco` compiles the ECO database into `assets/eco.dat` from `buildSrc/.../eco.pgn` on every `preBuild`, and `copyToAssets` copies the NDK-built `stockfish` / `stockfish_nosimd` binaries into `assets/` before Java compilation. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is right and irrelevant — a chess app's content is rules, data and art, not level files: **188 Java files / 49 597 LOC** across five Gradle modules (`DroidFishApp`, `CuckooChessEngine`, `CuckooChessApp`, `CuckooChess`, `EngineServer`) plus **103 C++/header files / 23 311 LOC** under `src/main/cpp` — a vendored **Stockfish 16.1** (`stockfish/` with its own `build_sf.mk` and `incbin`), plus **real tablebase probing in C++** (`rtb/` Syzygy, `gtb/` Gaviota) and a `nativeutil` JNI shim. Engine strength is shipped, not fetched: **two NNUE nets in `assets/`** — `nn-b1a57edbea57.nnue` at **65 429 575 bytes** (full net) and `nn-baff1ede1f90.nnue` at **3 480 122 bytes** (small net) — for a **67 MB asset tree**; the Java side wraps it in `engine/` with **9 classes**: `InternalStockFish`, `ExternalEngine` (UCI process), `NetworkEngine` + `OpenExchangeEngine` (remote engine over TCP, with the `EngineServer` module as the desktop half), `LocalPipe`, `UCIEngine`/`UCIEngineBase` and a dynamic `UCIOptions` model that reads the engine's advertised options and exposes `UCI_LimitStrength` / `UCI_Elo` for a real strength slider. Game modes are an enum with **6 values** (`PLAYER_WHITE 1`, `PLAYER_BLACK 2`, `TWO_PLAYERS 3`, `ANALYSIS 4`, `TWO_COMPUTERS 5`, `EDIT_GAME 6`), i.e. engine-vs-engine and bare analysis boards as well as play. Authored data: **`eco.pgn` — 2 014 `[ECO]` records / 11 716 lines**, compiled to `assets/eco.dat`, which powers "Add opening name" alongside the three pre-installed opening books (internal book in `CuckooChessEngine/chess/Book.java`, 189 LOC, plus a Polyglot/CTGA importer in `book/CtgBook.java`, 31 KB). Position editing, PGN headers/comments/NAGs/variations, Scid and OI-File-Manager interop, Chess960, blind mode, speech output (`Speech.java`), `movesound.ogg`, and a castling-rights-aware setup dialog are visible in the tree and documented in the manual. Presentation counted: **488 strings fully translated into all 13 locales** (`values` + `be, de, es, fr, it, ko, nl, pl, pt, ru, tr, uk, zh-rCN` — **488/488 in every one**, not English-with-a-few-keys), **17 piece sets shipped as zip-packed SVG** (`alfonso, alpha, cburnett, chesscases, chessicons, chessmonk, freestaunton, kilfiger, leipzig, magnetic, maya, merida, merida_new, metaltops, pirat, regular, wikimedia` — **12 SVGs each, 204 pieces**), a bundled `DroidFishChessNotationDark.otf` piece font, **28 portrait + 5 landscape layouts**, 21 SVG UI icons in `res/raw` (`analyze, blind, book, engine, flip, magnify, tb, thinking…`), density-split drawables and an adaptive `mipmap-anydpi-v26` launcher. Tests are real and unusually broad for an app of this kind: **33 `*Test*.java` files — 16 JVM unit tests in `CuckooChessEngine`** (`BitBoard`, `Book`, `ComputerPlayer`, `Evaluate`, `Game`, `History`, `KillerTable`, `MoveGen`, `Move`, …) **and 17 instrumentation tests** in `DroidFishApp/src/androidTest` including a **33 861-byte `GameTreeTest`** and a **31 439-byte `GameTest`**. Caveats, honestly: **`targetSdk 28`** is five Android releases stale (Play would reject it; F-Droid ships it anyway), `minifyEnabled false` on release, the manifest declares **`WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` + `INTERNET` + `VIBRATE`** (storage for the `DroidFish` folder, network only for the remote-engine feature — not a zero-permission app), there is **no store metadata in the tree** (no `fastlane/`), the build needs a 2021-era NDK to match AGP 7.1.1, and the last commit is `06948d5`, 2024-02-25, "Change version to 1.90". Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (a genuinely well-structured five-module Gradle project whose tasks even generate their own data and stage native binaries — docked because the README documents no build command, AGP 7.1.1 / compileSdk 28 / gradle 7.2 are all absent offline, and the NDK cross-compile of Stockfish makes the build heavy); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-native since minSdk 16, phone-shaped touch UI with drawer menus, portrait and landscape layouts, a decade of F-Droid releases, and an engine that runs on the device rather than in a cloud — docked because nothing was built or adb-launched here and targetSdk 28 predates scoped storage); depth 3/3 (the deepest *tool* in this catalog: Stockfish 16.1 with two NNUE nets, 6 play/edit/analysis modes, a full PGN game-tree editor with variations and comments, 2 014 ECO openings, opening books, Syzygy and Gaviota tablebase probing, Chess960, remote engines over a network protocol, and a strength limiter down to beginner level — chess itself supplies unbounded replay); polish 1.5/2 (488 strings translated into 13 locales at 100 % coverage, 17 piece sets, bundled piece font, 21 hand-drawn SVG icons, 28+5 layouts, 33 test files, speech and move sounds — docked for a stale targetSdk, an unminified release, three permissions and no store metadata in-tree). |

That is a multi-download, multi-minute build, outside a 90 s triage, so nothing is claimed as compiled or launched. Counted in the wrapper instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literally correct and meaningless — DCSS generates its dungeon): **29 Java files / 7 435 LOC**, and they are all **input and terminal plumbing**, not game logic — `keyboard/CrawlKeyboardView.java`, `keyboard/DirectionalTouchView.java`, `keymap/KeyMap.java` + `KeyMapper.java` + three `KeyMap*Preference` classes, `CrawlInputConnection.java`, `KeyBuffer.java`, `view/TermView.java` (terminal renderer), `activity/` 6 screens (Splash, Game, Help, Preferences, CustomKeyboard, CharacterFiles) across **8 manifest activities**, **28 XML resources** — 14 portrait layouts (backup/restore morgue and saves dialogs, character binding, config editor, custom keyboard, input, main, help, transparency seekbar) plus one landscape variant — **3 on-screen keyboard layouts** (`keyboard_qwerty.xml`, `keyboard_sym`, `keyboard_symshift`) with a `KeyboardLayoutSpinnerAdapter`, and `ConfigEditor` / `EditConfigFilePreference` for hand-editing `.crawlrc`. **English only** (`values/` with no locale variants), **no audio, no tiles, no art beyond the launcher icon** — deliberately the console port, as the README states. Game content was verified from the pinned **donor clone `games/crawl` `cf8c9bc`** that this port compiles: **144 `.des` files holding 6 127 `MAP`-grid vaults** and a **25 MB / 1 445-file `crawl-ref/source/dat` tree** (monster, item, spell and god definitions plus Lua triggers) over the full DCSS 0.29 engine — that is the real depth, but it is **not present in this clone** until `git submodule update --init` runs. Caveats, honestly: nothing built or launched; the wrapper's last commit is `91430b7`, **2023-10-12**, targeting DCSS 0.29 while upstream is well past 0.33; `minSdk 9` plus a sole `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` permission encode pre-scoped-storage assumptions its save/morgue handling depends on; and the README steers players to the *official tiles* Android port instead. Breakdown: buildability 0.5/2 (a real published app with a small readable Java layer, but the submodule carrying 100 % of the game is empty, `jni/` is absent so Gradle cannot configure, the documented recipe is a 2012 manual perl + NDK-r7 sequence, and nothing was verified); runs on phone 2/3 (phone-native since minSdk 9, a decade of F-Droid/Play releases, purpose-built touch keypad, directional pad, key remapping and terminal scaling — docked because an on-screen keyboard is a clunky way to drive a 60-key roguelike, nothing was adb-launched here, and targetSdk 33 with legacy external storage is stale); depth 3/3 (DCSS itself: 27 species × 25 backgrounds, 6 127 verified vaults, 15 runes, full religion/skills/mutation systems, and a console UI that loses nothing mechanically); polish 1/2 (honest and functional — three bundled bitmap fonts, config editor, morgue and save backup/restore, custom keyboard layouts — but monochrome ASCII, English only, zero audio, zero art, and an unmaintained 2012-era build). |

| 80 | **Eat Poop You Cat** (`Eat-Poop-You-Cat`) | Party / telephone-drawing game (the "eat poop you cat" paper game digitised: one player writes a sentence, the next draws it, the next captions the drawing — nobody sees more than the previous panel; 3–8 players, then a reveal of the whole degraded chain) | *_7.5 — desk audit_* | **Kotlin Multiplatform, three shipped frontends from one `shared` module**: **Android** (`androidApp/`, `dev.develsinthedetails.eatpoopyoucat`, **versionCode 25 / versionName 1.9.9**, **minSdk 26** / compile+targetSDK **37**, AGP **9.4.1**, Kotlin **2.4.20**, Compose Multiplatform **1.12.0** + compose plugin 2.4.20 + Compose BOM 2026.09.00, Koin 4.2.2, **Ktor 3.6.0**, **Room3 3.0.3** + KSP, DataStore 1.2.1, FileKit 0.16.0, kotlinx-serialization 1.11.0, navigation-compose 2.10.0-beta01, Java 21 toolchain, Gradle wrapper **9.7.1**, GPL); **desktop JVM** (`desktopApp/`, Compose Desktop `currentOs`) and **web WASM** (`webApp/`, `wasmJs` target); **live on F-Droid as `dev.develsinthedetails.eatpoopyoucat` and on Google Play** per README badges; portrait phone UI — a freehand canvas with undo/redo/eraser, a sentence text screen, and a card-stack gallery of past games; no iOS target | **Desk audit — no build run, and the documented build command failed inside the budget.** `BUILD.md` *does* document the recipe (`JDK 17+`, `Gradle 8.3.0+`, `./gradlew assembleDebug`), so it was actually tried: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17 timeout 88 sh ./gradlew --offline -q :androidApp:assembleDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 1m 17s`** at `buildSrc/build.gradle.kts` line 1: `Plugin [id: 'org.gradle.kotlin.kotlin-dsl', version: '6.7.3'] was not found` in the Gradle Central Plugin Repository — the Kotlin 2.4.20 embedded-`kotlin-dsl` marker is not in `~/.gradle/caches` and `--offline` forbids fetching it. Worth stating what *was* already in place, so the failure is toolchain-staleness and not the project: the wrapper's **`gradle-9.7.1-bin` dist is cached**, the **AGP `9.4.1` directory is in `~/.gradle/caches/modules-2`**, JDK 17 sits in `~/.gradle/jdks`, and the installed SDK carries **android-37.0**. Behind `buildSrc` sit uncached Room3/KSP, Koin 4.2.2 and Compose-MP 1.12 anyway, so even a successful configure would have hit resolution; **no `adb` on PATH**, so nothing could be installed. Nothing was compiled past `buildSrc` or launched, so no frame is claimed. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is meaningless — the content of this game is code and paper-game state, not levels: **130 `.kt` files / 12 137 LOC**, of which **123 files / 8 717 LOC are `shared/src/commonMain`** (the other roots are 4 + 1 + 1 + 1). It is a genuinely layered build, not one giant composable: `data/models/` (`Game`, `Entry`, `Player`, `Roster`, plus the drawing primitives `Line`, `LineSegment`, `LineProperties`, `Coordinates`), `data/local/` (**Room: 4 `@Entity` + 4 `@Dao`** — Game/Entry/Player/Roster, with `Converters`, `Serializers`, `AppDatabase`), `data/AppRepository`, `di/` (Koin `AppModule`/`DatabaseModule`/`DataStore`), `feature/` (**13 screens**: Home, NewGame, Nickname, **Draw**, **Sentence**, PreviousGames, PreviousGameDetail, InProgressGameDetails, ImportGames, StartNetGame, JoinNetGame, Credits, PrivacyPolicy), `core/ui/` (theme + `Scaffolds`/`Components`/`Profile`), `core/utilities/` (**`ImageExport` 439 LOC**, `Gzip`, `DrawingHelpers`, `FileUtils`, `ReadMetadata`). The drawing engine is real: `DrawViewModel` (348 LOC) holds a `List<Line>` of `LineSegment`s with **undo *and* redo stacks** (`undoCount`/`redoCount`), a `DrawMode { Draw, Erase }`, and `LineProperties(strokeWidth 12f, color, eraseMode)`; the palette is a committed **16-colour 4-bit `PIXEL_PALETTE_4_BIT`** (black/white/yellow/brown/flesh/red/pink/green/orange/purple…) plus a **3-colour 2-bit palette**, matching the README's "only Monochrome drawings" rule with a soft retro cap instead of a full colour picker. Sharing is a real format, not a screenshot: entries serialise through kotlinx-serialization, **gzip into `EPYC-<game>.json.gz`**, which the manifest registers as a **VIEW intent filter on `application/gzip` with pathPrefix `/EPYC-` and suffix `json.gz`** — so a shared game file opens the app. `ImageExport` renders the whole chain (drawings + captions + player names) to a bitmap with Compose `CanvasDrawScope` and `TextMeasurer` for the share preview. Multiplayer is the depth: `GameMode { UNKNOWN, LOCAL, LAN, INET }` — **pass-and-play on one device**, **LAN over Wi-Fi on port 3792 (`SERVER_PORT`)**, and **internet via a Tor onion address**, with an `epyc://<address>:3792/<gameId>` deep-link scheme, **16 `GameEvent`s** (`RequestNetGame`, `JoinGame`, `TakeYourTurn`, `TurnComplete`, `RequestMissingEntries`/`ResponseMissingEntries`, `GameComplete`, `TooSlow`, `NotRightNow`, …) and a **`ManageServerLifecycle`** driving a `ForegroundService` that serves the game from the phone with notification actions (`NotificationActionReceiver`). Persistence/progression: Room keeps every game, entry and roster, so `PreviousGamesScreen` (760 LOC) is a browsable archive with an in-progress section, and an **ImportGames** flow plus a backup-file saver restore old `.json.gz` chains. Localisation is the strongest number here: **10 locales** (`en, de, es, fr, ja, pt, ta, tr, uk, zh-rCN` — **108 string keys**, translated on Weblate) mirrored by **142 Fastlane metadata files** across the same 10 locales (title, short + full description, privacy policy, feature graphic, icon, and **4 phone + 4 7-inch + 4 10-inch screenshots** each) — that is F-Droid-grade store packaging, not a bare repo. Quality signals: **`screenshot` plugin wired with `android.experimental.enableScreenshotTest`** for Compose screenshot tests, **5 test files / 664 LOC / 14 `@Test`** (`DrawViewModelTest`, `PreviousGameDetailsDaoTests`, `DrawingHelpersTest`, `ExtensionsTest`, `ImportPreviousGamesTest`), CI in **4 workflows** (`android.yml`, `android-package.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `codacy.yml`), a `.githooks/pre-push` hook installed by an `installGitHooks` Gradle task, and `tag.py` for version bumps. `NetworkPlay.md` and `BUILD.md` are real docs beside the README. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built or launched**; the README's permissions claim (**"No permissions needed"**) is **stale** — the merged `shared/src/androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml` declares **7 permissions** (`INTERNET`, `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`, `ACCESS_WIFI_STATE`, `CHANGE_WIFI_STATE`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_CONNECTED_DEVICE`, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`), all for the LAN/Tor server and its notification, so this is not an offline-only app any more; `usesCleartextTraffic="true"` is on; **zero audio and zero music** (`find` returns 0 files); the drawing blend mode is literally `if(eraseMode) BlendMode.SrcOver else BlendMode.SrcOver`, i.e. erase paints white over a white canvas rather than compositing, so erasing under a stroke's edge leaves white gaps; the internet mode depends on a Tor onion service that the tree does not bundle; `navigation-compose 2.10.0-beta01` and `screenshot:0.0.1-alpha16` are pre-release pins; and there is **no single-player or AI mode** — every game needs other people. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (clean four-module KMP build with a **documented** `./gradlew assembleDebug`, cached wrapper/AGP/JDK, screenshot-test wiring and four CI workflows — docked because the offline build died in 77 s at `buildSrc` on an uncached `kotlin-dsl 6.7.3`, Room/Koin/Compose-MP pins are absent, and nothing was produced); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android-native since minSdk 26, target 37, touch-first freehand canvas with undo/redo/eraser, live F-Droid + Play listings, adaptive + monochrome launcher icon, `${deeplinkScheme}` app links — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and the LAN mode needs a foreground service plus notification permission); depth 2/3 (three transport modes including phone-served LAN and Tor-soaked internet play, a 16-event turn protocol with timeouts and missed-entry recovery, a browsable Room archive of every chain, `.json.gz` game import/export and a rendered reveal image — docked because the mechanic itself is one loop with no scoring, no campaign and no solo/AI path); polish 1.5/2 (10 locales at 108 keys, 142 store-metadata files, Room persistence, gzip share format, Koin DI, screenshot tests and a codeql/codacy pipeline — docked for zero audio, white-out erasing, 7 permissions contradicting the README's "none", pre-release dependency pins, and only 14 tests). |
| 81 | **Edge Roll** (`Edge-Roll`) | Endless one-thumb arcade — tumble a cube end over end across a procedurally generated bridge of floating tiles that crumble seconds after you leave them, with lateral branches, widening pads, gem spurs (+3) and gaps that end the run; one point per new tile | **7.0** | Android only (`edge.roll`, **versionCode 11 / versionName 1.1.2**, **minSdk 28** / compile+targetSdk **35**, **AGP 8.7.3** + Kotlin **2.0.21**, Gradle wrapper **8.13-bin**, JVM 17, **libGDX 1.13.1** android backend, **GPL-3.0**, single-module `app/`); four native ABIs shipped — `libgdx.so` for **armeabi-v7a (160 KB), arm64-v8a (164 KB), x86 (190 KB), x86_64 (193 KB)**, extracted from `gdx-platform` natives at build time so nothing native is checked in; touch-first portrait UI with **LEANBACK and gamepad launcher categories** declared and `VIBRATE` as the **only permission** (no INTERNET at all); live on **F-Droid as `edge.roll`** and via GitHub releases per README badge; no iOS, desktop or web target and no APK in the tree | **Built from source here — no launch, and the score is a desk tally on top of a real build.** `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17 timeout 90 sh ./gradlew --no-daemon -q assembleDebug` **produced `app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`, 8 332 486 bytes, in one pass under the 90 s budget**; a follow-up `--offline assembleDebug` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 4s**, so the whole graph is now in the module cache (AGP 8.7.3, Kotlin 2.0.21, `gradle-8.13-bin` and android-35 were already present on this host; only libGDX 1.13.1 had to come off the network). This is the rare clone whose **README documents the build and the documented command just works** — *"Requires a JDK (17+) and the Android SDK (platform 35, build-tools 35.0.0)"*, `export ANDROID_HOME=…`, `./gradlew assembleDebug` → `app-debug.apk`, with a signed release produced by CI on every `v*` tag (`.github/workflows/build.yml`). Nothing was installed or launched: `adb` exists at `~/Android/Sdk/platform-tools/adb` but **`adb devices` is empty** — no emulator running and none started inside a 3-minute triage — so **no frame is claimed**. Code measured: **13 Kotlin files / 2 396 LOC**, and the split is a real little engine rather than one blob — `game/EdgeRoll.kt` 594, `core/GameChromeView.kt` 654 (hand-drawn HUD: score, `BEST n`, pause button, banner, GAME OVER card, window-inset handling), `core/BoxBatch.kt`, `core/Gdx3DGame.kt`, `core/GameSession.kt`, `core/GameHostSession.kt`, `core/SoundFx.kt`, `core/Haptics.kt`, `core/Palette.kt`, `core/Scores.kt`, `core/Settings.kt`, plus `App.kt` and `GameActivity.kt`. The pack's `maps/levels=0` is **correct and meaningless** — the content *is* the generator, so it was read rather than counted: `generate()` walks the bridge forward inside a 28-tile `RENDER_AHEAD` window with a **distance-scaling lateral wiggle** (`p = min(0.42, 0.2 + 0.002·dist)`), a widening pad whose absences read as gaps (`p = max(0.16, 0.5 − 0.003·dist)`), a **10 % gem-spur branch** 1–2 tiles long with the gem at the tip, and per-tile gem odds of 3–5 %; the crumble grace period itself is the difficulty curve, `crumbleTime() = max(0.42, 1.2 − 0.01·score)` with a `dwellLimit` 0.8 s beyond it. Input is its own buffered swipe detector (tap = roll forward, centre/enter = roll forward, d-pad mapped) so rolls feel snappy. Audio is the neat trick: **0 audio files in the tree** yet the game is not silent — `SoundFx.kt` synthesizes **10 effects as raw `ShortArray` PCM at load** (`tap` 1150 Hz, `tick` 2300 Hz, a swept `pop`, a 3-harmonic coin with vibrato, noise-based crumble, plus square/saw/tri helpers and a one-pole `lowpassed()` for the slide) and feeds them to `SoundPool` only after every sample decodes, with a `SharedPreferences` master mute and a separate haptics toggle. The standout artifact is a **measured renderer-optimization dossier** under `perf/`: **18 benchmark runs in `perf/data/results.csv`** against a Motorola G7 Power **throttled to GPU 216 MHz / CPU 614 MHz with the big cluster offline**, plus `throttle.sh`/`bench.sh`/`restore.sh` (device returned to stock clocks afterwards), `IMPROVEMENTS.md`, `METHODOLOGY.md`, per-run logcat and an animated graph. The headline numbers are real and checkable: shipped 234-tile view **24.85 → 59.67 fps** (vsync-locked 60), a 1 020-tile stress field **6.91 → 50.26 fps (7.3×)**, and **draw calls 1 023 → 4** by instancing all tiles through `BoxBatch`; the diagnosis is the interesting part — raising the GPU to 725 MHz changed nothing (25.2 vs 24.9 fps) while raising the CPU jumped it to the cap, i.e. it was **draw-call-submission/CPU-bound**, not fill-rate-bound. Presentation is deliberately tiny: **13 resource files total** — 6 drawable assets, an adaptive `mipmap-anydpi-v26` launcher, 1 anim and **3 strings in a single locale** (no `values-<locale>`), because the HUD is drawn in a custom View and the art is shader-flat colour — offset by **Fastlane with 20 files** (`en-US` title/short/full description, **4 phone screenshots**, changelogs) and a CI workflow. Honest gaps: **zero tests** anywhere, **no assets/ directory at all**, one locale, one endless loop with no modes, unlocks or authored content, and no on-device verification here — the perf dossier is someone else's (the author's) device measurement, not mine. Breakdown: buildability **2.0/2** (documented command, wrapper committed, modern cached toolchain, builds clean twice — once online, once offline); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-exclusive since minSdk 28, native for all four ABIs, 8.3 MB APK, touch-and-swipe first, disruptive-free — no INTERNET, no storage, only VIBRATE — plus a live F-Droid package, TV and gamepad entry points; docked because nothing was adb-installed or launched and there is no shipped APK in the tree to inspect); depth **1.0/3** (procedural bridge with branches, gem risk-reward and a shrinking grace window is a genuinely good arcade loop, but it is one loop: no modes, no progression beyond a saved best score, no authored content or variation beyond the ramp — replay is score-chasing and nothing else); polish **1.5/2** (custom-drawn HUD with inset handling, procedural PCM audio instead of a sample pack, haptics, settings persistence, four-ABI libGDX packaging, a CI release pipeline with Fastlane store kit, and a real 18-run perf study with a 7.3× measured win and a correct root-cause diagnosis — docked for zero tests, one locale and three strings, no art at all, and a report that cannot be reproduced without the throttled device it was taken on). |

Nothing was exported and no `adb` launch was attempted, so no screenshot and no APK size are claimed. **The README (18 lines) documents no build command** — it is a logo, a one-paragraph blurb, F-Droid/GitHub badges, a "No AI Used" table and four screenshots — so the build recipe comes from `export_presets.cfg` and from Godot itself, which is trivially well-behaved here (no plugins, no native extensions, no addons). Counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is flatly wrong for this repo: **42 level scenes** (`lvl_1`–`lvl_40` plus `lvl_301`/`lvl_302`) at **838–1 023 lines of scene each**, driven by a sequential `NextLevel` chain (`Beginning.tscn` → `lvl_1` → …) and a `Beginning_old.tscn` legacy intro kept in-tree; **49 `.tscn` total**, one prefab (`Prefabs/Egg.tscn`), **28 GDScript files / 1 527 LOC** — `auto_load.gd` **407** (the whole game state, `ConfigFile` save of per-level best times + settings to `user://settings.cfg`), `main_menu.gd` **274** (level grid, per-band screenshot backdrops, `Level #N` + best-time panel), `egg_controller.gd` **265**, `moving_platform.gd` 74, `teleport.gd` 67, `enemies.gd` 60, `spikes_fall.gd` 48, plus `platform_fall`, `spikes`, `hint_controllers`, `indicator`/`indicator_mobile`, `music_volume`/`sound_volume`, `egg_ending_crack`/`egg_ending_fall`/`egg_part` and `load_next_scene`. Phone input is real and self-contained, not bolted on: `egg_controller._input` reads `InputEventScreenTouch` **or** `InputEventMouseButton` and splits the screen into **four quadrants — the left/right 20 % strips, halved vertically** — where the top corners press `high_jump` (held) *and* fire a directional hop while the bottom corners fire the hop alone, so the egg's jump arc is chosen by *where* you tap; a `resetHigh` flag plus `jump_interval` timer gate the charged jump, and `mobile_hint.gd` on `OS.get_name() == "Android"` hides the desktop key/click indicators and freezes the game (`Engine.set_time_scale(1e-15)`) behind a **two-panel touch tutorial** until the first input arrives. Presentation counted: **176 sprites** (`Sprites/` Backgrounds, Beginning, Blue/DarkBlue/DarkGray/Gray/Green floors and blocks, Clouds, Elements, Hints incl. `Hint_Mobile(_2).png`, Images, Labels, LevelScreenshots, Mountains, UI) plus **9 SVGs** in `Vector/` (teleport, hamburger, hint backgrounds, clang, background), 8 `Themes/*.tres` and `Styles/{ButtonBoxes,Sliders}` — a themed UI rather than default Godot widgets; audio is **11 music tracks of public-domain Mozart** (Oboe Concerto K.314, Piano Concerto No.17, Rondo K.511 *reversed*, String Quartet K.465 "Dissonant", Symphony No.40, Figaro, Trio No.1 — each filename naming its PD source ensemble) and **12 sound effects**; Fastlane adds 6 store images. Caveats, honestly: **no automated tests** anywhere; English-only (`values`/strings live inside scenes, no localisation files); one mechanic — hop to the teleport — with **no upgrades, no collectibles, no difficulty selector and no unlock system beyond the sequential `NextLevel` chain**, so the 40 levels are a fixed authored course rather than a progression system; the death model restarts the level; and the exported APK is not in the tree. Breakdown: buildability 1.5/2 (the cleanest possible Godot import — 2.6 s, zero addons, three export presets committed, CC0 — docked because the README documents no command and the repo ships no import cache, so the tree as-cloned does not run until `--import` is run); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android is a first-class export target with a real F-Droid package, a purpose-built four-quadrant touch scheme rather than an on-screen d-pad, an Android-only tutorial overlay, adaptive icons in-tree and a 1280×720 canvas stretch that suits phone screens — docked because no APK was exported and nothing was adb-launched here); depth 1/3 (42 hand-authored levels with per-level best-time records, a genuine platforming vocabulary of moving, falling and crumbling platforms, spikes, and patrolling enemies — but one verb, no meta-progression, no scoring beyond the clock, and no variation between the two bonus scenes and the main course); polish 1.5/2 (a public-domain Mozart soundtrack of 11 tracks with 12 SFX, 176 hand-made sprites, custom themes/sliders/button styles, splash screen, mobile hints, Fastlane store kit and a CC0 dedication — docked for no tests, no localisation, and store metadata in English only). |
| 83 | **Egyptian Mouse Pounce** (`Egyptian-Mouse-Pounce`) | Card game — Egyptian Rat Screw / Rat-a-Tat-Cat (slapjack): both players flip through half a deck, face cards and aces buy 1–4 challenge chances, and anyone who sees a pair can slap the pile to take it; first to hold all 52 wins, with cats reacting to the outcome) | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **Flutter 3 / Dart ≥3.10 single app, six shipped targets from one `lib/`**: Android (`com.dozingcatsoftware.mouse_pounce`, **version 1.6.0+10**, `std` and `fdroid` product flavors, Java/Kotlin **21** toolchain, portrait-locked activity, **Impeller explicitly disabled** in the manifest with the Lenovo Duet "renders upside down" note), **iOS** (`ios/Runner.xcworkspace`), **web** (`web/index.html` + icons/manifest), **Linux** and **macOS** CMake/Xcode runners; **GPL-3.0**; live on **F-Droid** (Fastlane `metadata/android/en-US` with 5 phone screenshots, icon, full/short description and 10 changelogs) and packaged from `pubspec.yaml` → `version: 1.6.0+10`; deps are all pub packages — `just_audio` (voice clips), `shared_preferences`, `file_picker` + `image` + `archive` (custom card-set import), `flutter_markdown_plus` (in-app about), `url_launcher`, `path_provider`, `intl` | **Desk audit — no build run, and no build was possible on this host.** `flutter` and `dart` are both **absent from PATH and from every conventional SDK location** (`~/flutter`, `/opt/flutter`, `/usr/lib/flutter`, snap, and a `find / -maxdepth 4` all return nothing) and `.pub-cache` does not exist, so nothing Flutter-shaped can be compiled here at any speed — the 90 s window is moot, not merely tight. The README (14 lines) is a two-sentence blurb, a platform list, a credits block and one genuinely useful build note — *"If you're building on Android, you'll need to specify the \"std\" build flavor"* plus *"there's also an \"fdroid\" flavor"* — but **no `flutter build` command**, and the flavors are real and correctly wired (`productFlavors { std { dimension "deploy"; signingConfig = signingConfigs.release if key.properties exists } fdroid { } }`, with a `preStdReleaseBuild` task that fails loudly rather than emitting an unsigned std APK, and a comment explaining the signing config lives on the flavor because buildTypes would otherwise override it). Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is correct and meaningless here — a 52-card deck is the entire board: **9 Dart files / 3 170 LOC**, and the split is a real engine + UI rather than one widget: `game.dart` **316** (pure, testable rules core), `main.dart` **1 380** (board, dialogs, AI scheduling, animation state machine), `card_images.dart` **527**, `custom_card_images.dart` **241**, `stats_dialog.dart` **196**, `stats.dart` **168**, `soundeffects.dart` **56**. Rules read from code, not the blurb: `_challengeChancesForCard` gives **ace 4 / king 3 / queen 2 / jack 1** and only for *face* cards, `_isStopper` accepts any jack-or-better (plus ten when the `ten_is_stopper` variation is on), the challenge count decrements on non-stoppers and awards `challengeChanceWinner` at zero, `_moveToNextPlayer` skips empty hands so a cardless player is passed over, `addPenaltyCard` inserts at the **bottom** of the pile with a `numPenaltyCardsInPile` counter so penalty cards **cannot** trigger a slap, and `gameWinner()` only fires on an empty pile with exactly one non-empty hand. Slap legality is a first-class rule set, not a hardcoded pair check: `canSlapPile()` supports **7 `RuleVariation`s** — `ten_is_stopper`, `slap_on_sandwich` (top == 3rd), `slap_on_run_of_3` (both ascending and descending, with wraparound modulo the 13 ranks), `slap_on_same_suit_of_4`, `slap_on_add_to_10`, `slap_on_marriage` (K+Q adjacent) and `slap_on_divorce` (K,_ ,Q) — each individually persisted under a `rule.<variation>` SharedPreferences key, plus **3 `BadSlapPenaltyType`s** (`none`, `penalty_card`, `slap_timeout` — implemented as a **5-card** lockout, `setSlapTimeoutCardsForPlayer(5, …)` — and `opponent_wins_pile`), each decremented per play in `playCard()`. Modes are **three, not one**: `human_vs_human`, `human_vs_ai` and `ai_vs_ai` (a "watch the AI play itself" attract mode). The AI is an honest reaction-time model rather than a cheat: `AISlapSpeed {slow, medium, fast}` maps to `_aiSlapDelayMillis()`, an `aiSlapCounter` snapshot is taken before each delayed slap and re-checked inside it so a stale slap is **discarded** if the pile changed in the interim, and a lost challenge schedules an AI move 500 ms out; an `AIMood` (`happy`/`very_happy`/`angry`) picks `bubble_happy.png` / `bubble_grin.png` / `bubble_mad.png` for 5 s after a won or lost pile, with 0.5 s fades. Touch model is phone-correct: the pile's top card carries its own `GestureDetector(onTapDown:)` → `_doSlap(tap.globalPosition, displaySize.height)`, and in pass-and-play the **screen half decides who slapped** (`pnum = globalOffset.dy > globalHeight / 2 ? 0 : 1`) — which is exactly how two people share one phone at a table. Animation is a real 7-state machine (`none`, `play_card_back`, `play_card_front`, `ai_slap`, `waiting_to_move_pile`, `pile_to_winner`, `illegal_slap`) with a 600 ms illegal-slap shake, and the whole thing is data-driven from `AnimationMode` rather than ad-hoc setState calls. Authored content counted rather than assumed: **230 asset files / 4.7 MB**, of which **4 bundled card sets × 52 cards = 208 `.webp`s** (`default` 1.1 MB @ 521:726, `original` 1.9 MB @ 500:726, `large text` 576 KB, `large 4 colour` 596 KB — i.e. both a legibility and a colour-blind accommodation), **11 cat images** (4 cats, 4 paws, 3 speech bubbles) and **9 MP3 voice lines / 160 KB** — 5 "mad" (`sauerkraut_mad_1-3`, `ginger_mad_1-2`) and 4 "happy" (`boojie_happy_1-4`) — each a dedicated `just_audio` `AudioPlayer` preloaded at startup and picked at random per mood by `SoundEffectPlayer`. The standout feature for this catalog is **user-supplied card art done properly**: `custom_card_images.dart` (241 LOC) imports a folder or zip of `2C.webp|png|jpg`-style files, **converts every image to webp at quality 90 on an isolate**, writes into `custom_<timestamp>.tmp` and renames on completion so a half-finished import is never listed, caps each zip entry at **25 MB** against zip bombs, stores a `info.json` with display name and aspect ratio (and deliberately **not absolute paths**, because the iOS container changes between launches), tolerates `.webp/.png/.jpg/.jpeg`, and supports deleting sets. Persistence and metrics are real: `Stats` keeps 10 counters split per mode (`vsAiWins/Losses/TotalCardsPlayed/SlapsByHuman/SlapsByAi` and the player-1/player-2 equivalents), serialises to JSON in `shared_preferences`, is written at most every **10 s** while a game runs and immediately at game end, and has a reset. Tests are genuine, not stubs: **`game_test.dart` 265 LOC / 15 `test()` blocks** driving the rules through an ASCII card notation (`cards('4S KH TD 2S AS')`) — face-card chains, sandwiches, runs, marriages, penalty cards, and the "player with no cards keeps the round alive by slapping" edge case — with a `widget_test.dart` present but empty. Installed-store polish is complete for F-Droid: `assets/doc/about.md` is a written rulebook rendered in-app via `flutter_markdown_plus`, `assets/misc/no.png` is the illegal-slap stamp, and Fastlane carries **19 files** including 5 screenshots and a changelog for all 10 versions. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched**, so the score is a desk tally; the tree ships **no APK**; **English only** — every string is inline in `main.dart`, there are no `AppLocalizations`/`l10n` files and the only `intl` use is number formatting in the stats dialog, despite F-Droid's translated-description apparatus; **no haptics** (`HapticFeedback` appears nowhere) which is a miss for a slap game; `minSdk = flutter.minSdkVersion` and `targetSdk = flutter.targetSdkVersion` are **not pinned in the repo at all** — they inherit whatever Flutter the builder has, so build behaviour is not reproducible from the tree; the manifest asks for **no permissions**, which is a genuine plus, but `file_picker` pulls in platform plumbing that has to be verified on device; the AI reacts purely on a delay timer with no skill model, so `slow/medium/fast` is reflex speed only; and the game is strictly **two players** (`numPlayers = playerCards.length`, `startGame()` splits the deck in half), not the 3–8-player party version. Breakdown: buildability 1/2 (a clean, small, idiomatic single-package Flutter project with committed flavors, a correct signing split and no native code to fight — docked hard because **there is no Flutter/Dart SDK on this host and no pub cache**, so not one line could be compiled, and because the README's build note names a flavor but never a command and the SDK levels are unpinned); runs on phone 2.5/3 (Android is first-class with a real F-Droid flavor and store listing, portrait-locked, immersive full-screen, tap-the-pile slapping and split-screen local multiplayer on one phone, plus iOS/web/Linux/macOS targets from the same code — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and the app requires a Flutter toolchain newer than anything cached); depth 2/3 (a faithfully implemented ERS engine with the full face-card challenge ladder, 7 toggleable slap variations, 3 bad-slap penalties, three play modes including AI-vs-AI, three reflex difficulties, per-mode lifetime statistics and user-importable card art — docked because it is still one card game with no campaign, no unlock ladder, no progression and a two-player ceiling); polish 1.5/2 (four bundled card sets including a large-print and a four-colour deck, 9 recorded voice reactions with per-mood random selection, animated 7-state board with card-flip, pile-gather and illegal-slap effects, cat mood bubbles, a real custom-art import pipeline with webp conversion and zip-bomb guards, debounced JSON stats, 15 real rules tests, an in-app rulebook and complete Fastlane metadata — docked for English-only strings, zero haptics, no localization scaffolding, and SDK levels left to the builder's Flutter). |

Code measured: **5 Java files / 771 LOC** — `MainActivity.java` **456** (board build, sequence shuffle, `CountDownTimer(60000)`, result/stat dialogs, menu), `SavedData.java` 131 (`SharedPreferences`: `STREAK`, `N_GAMES`, `N_WON`, `LANGUAGE`, `TIME`, `STARS`), `Speaker.java` 81 (**`TextToSpeech`** plus a **`ToneGenerator` DTMF** layer), `NumberButton.java` 61, `LangUtils.java` 42 — **zero test sources** (`app/src/test` and `app/src/androidTest` hold only the generated `ExampleUnitTest`/`ExampleInstrumentedTest` stubs). The pack's `maps/levels=0` is correct and meaningless — there are no level files, the round *is* generated: `createButtons()` walks the 6×3 grid and skips each cell on `Math.random() > 0.5` until 9 buttons are placed, with a `taken` list of `Point`s so numbers never overlap. The genuinely unusual feature is the **numeral-system switch**, and it is a real table, not a font swap: `LangUtils` carries 6 hardcoded 10-entry arrays — **Devanagari (०–९), CJK (零–九), Khmer (០–៩), formal/financial Chinese (零, 壹, 貳, 參, 肆, 伍, 陸, 柒, 捌, 玖), Sino-Korean readings (공, 일, 이, 삼, 사, 오, 육, 칠, 팔, 구) and Glagolitic (Ⰰ–Ⰸ for Old Church Slavonic)** — selected from a 7-item menu (English + those 6), persisted under `LANGUAGE`, and applied live to every visible button mid-game when the game has not started. Feedback is multi-channel: every tap fires a pitched `ToneGenerator` tone (long tone on the final 9), a miss fires `playErrorTone()`, and the end-of-round dialog has a **speak button** that reads `tts_time_taken` plus record/streak praise (`tts_new_record`, `tts_streak5/10/25`, `tts_so_fast`, `tts_super_fast`) — an accessibility-grade touch for a 771-line app. Presentation: **28 images** (9 custom vector drawables — `game_background`, `win_background`, `lose_background`, `heart`, `number_button_ready`, `dialog_background`, `dialog_button` and two launcher foregrounds — plus a full 5-density `ic_launcher_eidetic` mipmap set and a Play-store icon), **0 audio files** (all sound is synthesised), **2 layouts** (35 + 70 lines), **26 strings in one locale**, `values-night` theme, 3 README/store screenshots, and a 42-line `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0). Caveats, honestly: **no build was documented and no launch happened**; the game is a single mechanic with **no progression, no difficulty scaling and no unlock** — 9 numbers is the whole game in both modes, so "easy" vs "hard" is only the 2-heart buffer (`MAX_STARS = 2`); the reward for winning is a faster time against your own record, which is the entire replay loop; UI chrome strings are **English-only** despite the 7 numeral systems (the language dialog title and mode toasts are hardcoded in Java); there is **no localisation apparatus** for the 26 strings, **no audio**, **no tests**, and the last commit is a grayscale-instead-of-gameover visual tweak (2025-04-27). Content is a real data table, not a stub: **5 themes — Classic, Detective Manor, Fantasy Realm, Cosmic Expedition, Harbor Town — each 5 categories × 5 options** (`Classic` being the canonical Brit/Swede/Dane/Norwegian/German × Red/Green/Blue/Yellow/White × Tea/Coffee/Milk/Juice/Water × Dog/Bird/Cat/Horse/Fish × Painting/Violin/Chess/Gardening/Reading), i.e. **25 category vectors and 125 distinct values**; **9 clue types** (`sameHouse`, `leftOf`, `nextTo`, `fixedHouse`, `notSameHouse`, `somewhereLeftOf`, `between`, `atEnd`, `notFixedHouse`). The generation path is what earns the score: `generatePuzzle` shuffles a random solution per category, builds a candidate pool of **anchors** (`fixedHouse` for every category × house, `atEnd` on the end houses, `notFixedHouse` decoys), **relational** (`sameHouse`, `notSameHouse`, `leftOf`, `nextTo` in both directions) and **complex** (`somewhereLeftOf`, `between`) clues, adds them **one at a time until `EinsteinSolver.hasUniqueSolution` returns true, then *removes* every clue that is not needed** — a uniqueness-guaranteed minimal clue set, not a fixed level file. The solver is brute-force permutation search over **memoised 3!/4!/5! permutation tables** (`_permsCache`), and generation retries up to **50 attempts** before giving up. Progression: an **unbounded level ladder** (`maxLevelsToShow = 20 + (clearedCount ~/ 12) * 12`, so the visible list grows 12 levels at a time) with a real curve in `_getDifficultyForLevel` — **3 houses / 3 categories at levels 1–5 up to 5 houses / 5–6 categories past 21, every 5th level a step harder than its neighbours**. Modes are three: **levels**, a **seeded daily challenge** (`seed = year*10000 + month*100 + day` fed to the same generator, completion marked per date), and a **practice generator** with live sliders for **houses 3–5 / categories 3–6**. Aids are honest and priced: **max 2 hints** (1 on the easiest tiers) that write the correct value into a cell, **undo move**, a **Check Solution** button, an **Auto Check** setting that verifies on every edit, and a **"Move Crossed Clues to Bottom"** setting — clue-crossing is the core notepad verb and is persisted along with per-level completion and the current level through **Hive** (typed `@HiveField` adapters on both the engine and the state model, saved after every change). Polish counted: **7 screens**, a fixed dark `ColorScheme` with no light option, **Fastlane metadata (8 files: title, short + full description, icon, 4 phone screenshots)** but **no changelogs**, and **1 image asset / 64 KB** (`assets/einstein.png`) — the pack's `images=26` is launcher mipmaps across densities, not art. Caveats, honestly, and they are specific: **nothing was built, installed or launched**, so this is a desk tally; **zero tests** (no `test/` directory, no `flutter_test` usage anywhere despite the dev dep); **zero audio**; **English only** with no `l10n`/`AppLocalizations` apparatus at all; `pubspec.yaml` still carries Flutter's default `description: "A new Flutter project."`; **`categoriesCount` is silently clamped** — `selectedCatKeys = availableCatKeys.sublist(0, min(categoriesCount - 1, 5))` against 5-key themes — so the **6-category tier that both the level ladder and the practice slider request never materialises**: every puzzle is 5 categories deep, the slider's top detent is a no-op, and all levels past 21 are the same difficulty; there is **no timer, no score/star system, no streaks and no statistics screen** (only completion flags), so replay rests entirely on the generator; no `android:screenOrientation` is pinned; last commit is `b00bc3e`, **2026-09-09**, "Update V1.0.4 & Fixed #12".
