The README is **30 lines** — a Darkness Dungeon credit, five CI/Netlify badges, a nine-row version table (App Store, WinGet, Chocolatey, Scoop, Homebrew, Flathub, Snapcraft, AUR, F-Droid) and a download link — and documents **no build command**; the recipe lives instead in the committed `Makefile` (`make get` = `fvm flutter pub get`, `make apk` = `fvm flutter build apk --no-tree-shake-icons --dart-define-from-file=".env.$(ENV).local" --flavor staging`, `make appbundle`, `make adhoc` for IPA) plus **six per-platform setup guides** (`docs/setup-{android,ios,linux,macos,web,windows}.md`, and two Windows certificate docs) and **27 GitHub workflows** (`test-android`, `test-ios`, `test-web`, `test-linux-{deb,rpm,snapcraft,tar}` × x64/arm64, `test-macos-{appstore,dmg,pkg,zip}`, `test-windows-{exe,msi,store,zip}` × x64/arm64, `test.yml`, `release.yml`, `release-web.yml`) whose `release.yml` carries the literal build lines (`flutter build apk --flavor $FLAVOR`, `appbundle`, `ipa`, `macos`, `linux`, `windows`, all with `--dart-define-from-file=.env`). Counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack's blurb — and the queue's own summary ("enjoy smooth gameplay, stunning graphics, and endless fun") is **wrong about the content**: it is not endless. The game is **one 50×50-tile Tiled map** (800×800 px world, 3 layers, 32 KB `map.json`, read through a committed `CustomTiledAssetReader`) reused for the whole game, and the mission is a scripted story: `en.i18n.json` holds the wizard's reveal that "you are the fifth knight sent for this task… their bodies are hanging from the walls like trophies", the key/door gate (`doorWithoutKey`: "I think I need a key to get through here!"), the boss taunt, the kid's rescue and a `congratulations`/`PLAY AGAIN` ending that thanks you "for testing the game and feeling the power of Flame and Bonfire". Content counted: **80 asset files / 4.8 MB** — **22 enemy sprites across four enemy classes** (`goblin`, `imp`, `mini_boss`, `boss`, plus four-direction `atack_effect_*`), **14 knight sprites** (idle/run ×2 facings, four-direction melee effects, fireball ×4, explosion), **7 of 80 assets are audio** (`sound_bg.mp3`, `attack_player.mp3`, `attack_enemy.mp3`, `battle_boss.mp3`, `attack_fire_ball.wav`, `explosion.wav`, `sound_interaction.wav`) — and the interactable set is **barrels, doors, keys, life potions, spikes and torches** with **two NPCs** (wizard, kid). Code is **5 669 LOC of hand-written Dart** across the workspace (the game module's 5 252 includes a 612-line generated `assets.gen.dart`); the non-generated game files are a settings surface larger than the gameplay: `settings_model.dart` **423**, `keyboard_key.dart` **392**, `menu.dart` **327**, `section_item.dart` **290**, `enemy_sprite_sheet.dart` 274, `boss.dart` **271**, `game.dart` **242**, `knight.dart` **209**, `dialogs.dart` 145, `sp_util.dart` 156 — i.e. rebindable keys, a hotkey recorder, joystick-vs-keyboard toggle, four theme modes (manual/system/dark/light) and two languages (**`en` and `zh`**) are the real engineering, while combat is `life: 200`, melee plus a fireball projectile and a boss whose only AI is a summon ladder at 150/100/50 HP with `attack = 40`. Persistence is settings-only (`SpUtil` over SharedPreferences stores `themeMode` and the three custom key ids) — **no save, no progress, no high score**, because there is nothing to save: one map, one mission, one knight, no unlocks, no difficulty selection, no timer and no scoring. Polish that is genuinely there: light/dark/system theming, Flutter's native splash config (`flutter_native_splash.yaml`), a desktop tray menu (Settings/Exit) wired through `tray_manager`, per-platform launcher icons, a version+commit readout on the menu, Sentry crash reporting, Fastlane metadata in **`metadata/en-US` and `metadata/zh`** (short + full description, icon, **4 phone screenshots** each), a 35 MB clone and **3 README screenshots**. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched here**, so this is a desk tally and the nine-channel version table is upstream's claim, not my observation; **tests are smoke only — 5 files, 55 lines total** (`widget_test.dart` 34, `app_test.dart` 6, `l10n_test.dart` 15), despite the CI badge; there is **no Android orientation lock** and no `abiFilters`, so the APK is whatever Flutter emits; the `.env.$(ENV).local` dart-define files the Makefile requires are **gitignored and not in the tree**, so `make apk` cannot run from a clean clone without writing one; and the game is a **~10-minute single mission** whose only replay is PLAY AGAIN on the same map. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a well-structured seven-module workspace with a committed `Makefile`, six platform setup guides and 27 CI workflows that carry the real build commands — docked because **no Flutter/FVM/Dart SDK and no pub cache exist on this host** so nothing could be attempted, the README documents no command, the `.env` files are absent, and SDK levels are unpinned); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android is a first-class target with a real F-Droid package and touch-first input — joystick or rebindable on-screen controls, portrait-friendly single-screen menu, entirely offline, two translated locales, 4 phone screenshots in Fastlane metadata, plus an actual App Store release — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and no APK ships in the tree); depth **1.5/3** (four enemy classes with a boss that escalates by summoning minions at three HP thresholds, melee + ranged fireball combat, a key/door gate and six interactable prop types with two NPCs — but it is **one 800×800 px map, one scripted mission, one character, no score, no saves, no progression, no difficulty and no procedural content**, and the queue summary's "endless fun" is not what the code does); polish **1.5/2** (light/dark/system theming, native splash, desktop tray with Settings/Exit, rebindable hotkeys with a recorder dialog, joystick toggle, menu version+commit readout, Sentry error reporting, 7 audio cues, bilingual en/zh strings and complete Fastlane metadata in both — docked for **80 assets total / 4.8 MB of art inherited from the Darkness Dungeon base**, smoke-only tests, no orientation lock and no gameplay settings beyond input and theme). |
| 89 | **Feudal Tactics** (`Feudal-Tactics`) | Turn-based hex conquest strategy (Slay-like) — a procedurally generated hexagonal island where you attack neutral and enemy tiles with strength-ranked units, defend your own borders with castles, and win by taking every tile | *_8.0 — desk audit_* | **Android + desktop + Linux Flatpak from one libGDX 1.13.1 core**: `de.sesu8642.feudaltactics`, **versionCode 20 / versionName 1.5.5**, **minSdk 24** / **compileSdk 35** / **targetSdk 36**, **AGP 8.7.3** + Gradle wrapper **8.14.1-bin**, Java 8 source with Lombok, `isGame="true"`/`appCategory="game"`, `screenOrientation="user"` + `configChanges`, portrait-friendly; **GPL-3.0-or-later** (contributions multi-licensed GPL/MIT); **live on F-Droid, Google Play, Flathub (`de.sesu8642.feudaltactics`) and itch.io**, so four shipped distribution channels and no iOS/web target | **Desk audit — no build run.** No `timeout 90` attempt: the documented path is a libGDX Gradle build whose Android leg (`assembleDebug` → compile + dex + package) does not fit a 90 s triage window, so nothing is claimed as built or run. The toolchain, however, is fully present offline for once — **`gradle-8.14.1-bin` is in `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists` exactly as the wrapper pins it, AGP 8.7.3 is in the module cache, `android-35` (compileSdk) and `android-36` (targetSdk) are both installed, and JDK 17 is unpacked at `~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-amd64-linux.2`** — so this is the rare clone where `./gradlew assembleDebug` should configure without the network; only **`adb` is absent from PATH**, so an install would still need SDK platform-tools. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is correct and meaningless because **the board is generated**: `NewGamePreferences` defines **MapSizes SMALL 50 / MEDIUM 150 / LARGE 250 / XLARGE 500 / XXLARGE 1000 tiles** and **Densities LOOSE 3 / MEDIUM 0 / DENSE −3** over a seed, so every run is a fresh island at one of 15 size×density settings; the only shipped map file is `assets/scenarios/tutorial/Tutorial.json`, driven by a `ScenarioMap` enum with exactly two values (`NONE`, `TUTORIAL`) and its own `ScenarioRuleEnforcerTutorial` that gates moves during the scripted intro. Depth read from code, not the blurb: **4 unit types on a strength/salary economy** — `PEASANT(1, 2)`, `SPEARMAN(2, 6)`, `KNIGHT(3, 18)`, `BARON(4, 54)` — merged by buying equal-strength units, over tile content classes `Unit`, `Castle`, `Capital`, `Tree`, `PalmTree`, `Gravestone`, `Blocking`; **kingdom splitting is a first-class rule** (`Kingdom`, `GameStateHelperKingdomSplitTest`), so cutting an enemy's land in two severs its economy — that is the real strategic hook and it is unit-tested. **Four honest AI levels**, parameterised rather than diff-scaled: `LEVEL_1` conquers on a 0.5 coin-flip per turn with no smart attack or defence; `LEVEL_2` 0.8 with smart attacking only; `LEVEL_3` always attacks, removes blocking objects scoring ≥4, protects castles/units at score 25/20, and biases offence +5 against other bots to favour the human; `LEVEL_4` the same with a score-7 removal threshold and re-considered defence. Engineering that is unusually solid for a hobby clone: **22 real unit tests** (not generated stubs) — `BotAiIntegrationTest`, `GameStateValidatorTest`, `GameStateHelperKingdomSplitTest`, `LocalizationPropertiesTest` (which validates every locale file has the same keys) and 8 achievement tests — plus **undo, auto-save (`AutoSaveRepository`), deterministic `GameStateHashes` for regression**, and a schema **`DataMigrator`** for upgrading saved states across versions. Content and presentation counted: **24 Java files / 3 415 LOC in `ingame`** (HUD, new-game parameter screens, input handling) against **122 files / 8 289 LOC in `menu`** — the shell is bigger than the game and it earns it: subpackages `achievements` (**11 achievement classes**, each tested), `statistics` (per-AI-level win counts), `campaign` with a `Medals` enum, `preferences`, `changelog`, `about`, `information`, `crashreporting`, `splashscreen`, `play`; **6 renderer files / 1 121 LOC**, a **5-file / 228-LOC in-game map editor** (the roadmap's "map editor" is shipped), **247 strings fully translated to English and German** (`strings.properties` and `strings_de.properties`, 247 lines each, with `strings_en.properties` a 2-line override hook), **131 PNGs** of which **77 are the custom Scene2D skin at three DPI tiers** (`0.5x`, `1x`, `2x` plus `_pressed` variants and nine-patch buttons) and the rest are `graphics/` source art shipped beside its `.xcf` masters, **14 Fastlane store screenshots** (4 phone + 4 seven-inch + cover/feature/icon art), and **0 audio files** — sound is still an open roadmap bullet. Host totals: **271 Java files**, `core/src/main` **19 710 LOC**, `lwjgl3` 256, `android` 79; 51 MB clone at `bced140` (2026-10-20, a German-translation typo fix, i.e. actively maintained). Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched here**, so this is a desk tally and the four store listings are upstream's claim, not my observation; **zero audio** and a single-player skirmish loop only — no campaign story, no local or online multiplayer (both still roadmap), replay resting entirely on procedurally generated boards plus the editor; the AI's difficulty is a set of scoring thresholds rather than search, so LEVEL_3/4 differ by tuning, not by planning; and the tutorial is one hand-authored scenario. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a textbook modern libGDX multi-module layout — `core`/`lwjgl3`/`android`, committed wrapper, Lombok, 22 real tests, and for once **every pinned artifact already cached: Gradle 8.14.1, AGP 8.7.3, android-35, JDK 17** — docked because the README documents only generic Gradle tasks with no one-line Android target, nothing was compiled here, and `adb` is missing so it cannot be installed); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native and touch-first by construction — minSdk 24, `screenOrientation="user"` so it rotates both ways, single-activity `configChanges` handling, GLES 2.0 required, shipped on F-Droid and Play at 1.5.5 with 4 phone screenshots — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and no APK ships in the tree); depth **2.5/3** (a genuinely well-simulated conquest game: 5 map sizes × 3 densities × seeded generation, kingdom-splitting as a severable economy, four strength-ranked unit types on a salary curve, four parameterised AI levels with smart attacking/defending and an anti-bot bias that favours the player, undo, autosave with state migration, 11 achievements, per-AI statistics and a map editor — docked because there is no campaign, no multiplayer and no mode beyond one skirmish loop, and the top AI is tuned rather than searching); polish **1.5/2** (custom Scene2D skin in three DPI tiers with nine-patch buttons, full English + German localisation at 247 keys with a test that keeps locales in sync, splash screen, changelog and about screens, statistics and medals, crash reporting, 22 real tests, 14 Fastlane store screenshots — docked for **zero audio**, a menu-heavy codebase whose shell (8.3 k LOC) outweighs its gameplay (3.4 k LOC), and a single hand-authored tutorial as the only scripted content). |
| 90 | **Fire Strike Retro** (`Fire-Strike-Retro`) | Retro raycast FPS (Wolfenstein-3D-style pseudo-3D shooter in the Gloomy Dungeons lineage): clear one map at a time of 5 enemy types with 6 weapons on 3 ammo pools, hunt secrets for a completion percentage, score kills/time/secrets at level end, and climb a 4-episode campaign with EXP and 24 achievements | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **Android only**, single-module app `com.eightsines.firestrike.opensource`, **versionName 2.0 / versionCode 2000**, **minSdk 16** / compile+targetSdk **30**, **AGP 4.1.0** + Gradle wrapper **6.5-all**, **MIT**, `ndk.abiFilters arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, x86, x86_64` with an **externalNativeBuild CMake step** (`src/main/cpp/renderer.c` → `librenderer.so`), `minifyEnabled true` with a real `proguard-project.txt` on release; **live on F-Droid** as `com.eightsines.firestrike.opensource` (upstream `fdroid/com.eightsines.firestrike.opensource.yml` builds `v2.0` at `subdir: android` and declares **`AntiFeatures: Ads`**); a second `flavour/gplay` source set carries the AdMob/RateGame variants; no iOS, desktop or web target and no APK in the tree | **Desk audit — no build run; two build attempts failed in 1 s each, before any compilation.** Both used the documented command (`./gradlew installDebug` in `android/`, the README's other recipe being `./z-build debug install`), offline, under `timeout`: with the system JDK **27** it died with **`Could not compile build file … build.gradle > General error during semantic analysis: Unsupported class file major version 71`** (Gradle 6.5's bundled Groovy/ASM cannot read it), and re-run with **Adoptium JDK 17** the identical failure appears as **`Unsupported class file major version 61`** — Gradle 6.5 predates JDK 17 and this box has no JDK 8–14. Behind the wrapper: **`jcenter()`** in both `buildscript` and `allprojects` (shut down 2021), **AGP 4.1.0 not in the module cache** (which holds 8.2.2–9.4.1 only), **compileSdk 30 not installed** (android-35/36/37.0 only), an NDK/CMake leg on top, and **no `adb` on PATH**. `gradle-6.5-all` itself *is* cached, which is why the failure is a JDK/Groovy mismatch and not a download. The README is thorough by hobby standards — badges, credits, an explicit support/EOL table ("Android version planned to support: up to 11.x", "Estimated end-of-life: up to 2021"), a real compile section with **`./z-build debug install` / `./gradlew installDebug`**, a keystore recipe for release, and the `ruby`/`imagemagick 6.x`/`sox`/`lix`+**Haxe 4.1.4** toolchain for regenerating levels and textures. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=28`, and the count is the interesting part: **28 `.map` files ship, but `flavour/config/ProfileConfig.java` declares 38 `ProfileLevel` rows** — `e00m00`+`e00m01` (tutorial, no ads), `e01m01`–`e01m10` (episode 1, **`AD_REWARDED`**, soldier), `e02m01`–`e02m10` (episode 2, **`AD_INTERSTITIAL`**, commander), `e03m01`–`e03m05` (episode 3, doctor), `e99m99` (special, doctor) and **`e77m01`–`e77m10`, a 10-map bonus episode with no `.map` in the tree at all** (`materials/fetch-levels.php` is the updater that filled it upstream) — so the campaign is **28 playable maps + 10 dangling rows**, and those rows would fail at `Level.java`'s `assetManager.open("levels/%s.map")`. The level format is binary and hand-authored through a bundled web mapper: header carries width/height **capped at 64×64**, then `wallsMap`, `texMap`, `passableMap`, three floor layers, an entity list and a **`secretsMask`**, all read in `Level.java`; the largest `.map` is 32 KB. Engine depth read from code, not the blurb: **124 Java files / 25 643 LOC**, split **`engine` 48 files / 13 601 LOC** (a from-scratch raycast renderer — per-column wall/floor/ceiling texturing, `TEX_MON_CELL` sprite sheets, depth-sorted sprites), **`core` 29 / 5 771** and **`feature` 28 / 4 083**, plus `com.eightsines.{esmediadtor,espromo,estracker}` support packages. Combat is tabulated in `GameConfig` rather than fudged: **6 weapons on 3 ammo pools** (`WEAPON_PISTOL`, `DBLPISTOL`, `AK47`, `TMP`, `WINCHESTER`, `GRENADE` over `AMMO_CLIP`/`SHELL`/`GRENADE`, plus a knife), each with hit, stun-in-frames and hits-per-second, so **DPS runs 6 (knife) → 8 (dual pistols) → 12 (AK) → 20 (TMP) → 32 (Winchester, ammo-hungry) → 52 (grenade)**; armour splits damage **25 % to health / 75 % to armour**; health is 0–100 with stims and medipacks, ammo caps are 150/75/50 with guaranteed floor pickups; and there are **5 monster types** (`MAX_MONSTER_TYPES = 5`) with their own ready/attack/death audio. Progression is real: per-level scoring on kills/time/secrets with a `foundSecrets/totalSecrets` percentage and a "secret found" banner, `state.overallSecrets` rolled up across the run, per-level saves through `State`'s own serializers, and **24 achievements** with EXP (`monsters_killed` 5/50/500/2500/5000, 100 %-kills-in-5-and-10-levels streaks, and more, each with its own title/description pair). Assets counted: **76 `.tex` textures**, of which **33 are per-weapon hit-animation frames** (`hit_{ak47,dblpist,knife,pist,rocket,shtg,tmp}_N_a/_p`) and the rest `texmap_1`/`texmap_common`/`texmap_mon_1`/`texmap_mon_2`/`sky_1` — i.e. **two monster sheets cover all five enemy types**; **6 MP3 music tracks (7.8 MB)**, **31 MP3 SFX (168 KB)**, 44 PNGs in `res/`, a TTF font and a `textures_cache_version`-keyed texture cache. The art pipeline ships with the game: `materials/` holds **16 MB of source graphics, 2.5 MB of `graphics-src`, 30 MB of `music-hq`, 2.2 MB of `sounds-hq`, 4.3 MB of `.gd2l` level sources** plus the Ruby/shell generators (`make-texmap.rb`, `make-levels.rb`, `fix-levels.rb`, `make-music.sh`, `make-sounds.sh`), and **`procedural/` is an 81-file / 9 285-LOC Haxe program** that generates levels, alongside four JS tools (`web-mapper`, `web-builder`, `web-uploader`, `web-site`) including a browser map editor. Localisation is **English 188 strings / Russian 179** (`values/` + `values-ru/`), and the offline about/licences HTML ships in `assets/web`. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was compiled, installed or launched**, so this is a desk tally and the F-Droid listing is upstream's claim; **zero test sources** (`find src -path "*test*" -name "*.java"` = 0); **the 10 `e77` bonus maps are absent**; the F-Droid buld declares **Ads** (rewarded + interstitial gating between episodes), unusual for an MIT game and the reason the clone is filed under the opensource package id; the toolchain is frozen at 2020 (Gradle 6.5 / AGP 4.1.0 / targetSdk 30 / jcenter / declared EOL 2021) with no further support beyond critical fixes; and 5 enemy types is a genuine ceiling across 28 maps. Breakdown: buildability **0.5/2** (tidy single-module Android project with a committed wrapper, four ABI filters, a real proguard config, documented build commands and a full asset-regeneration toolchain — docked to near-zero because both attempts died in 1 s on a Gradle-6.5-vs-JDK mismatch, and jcenter, AGP 4.1.0 and compileSdk 30 are all gone from this host, with an NDK/CMake leg on top); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native from `minSdk 16` up, touch-first on-screen controls for a genre that normally demands a mouse, four ABIs so it runs on old armv7 hardware, fully offline campaign with per-level saves, live F-Droid package — docked because nothing was adb-installed here, no APK ships in the tree, and the shipped build carries ads); depth **2.5/3** (28 authored maps across three episodes plus a tutorial and a special map, a hand-tuned binary level format with secrets and three floor layers, 6 weapons on a real DPS/stun curve with 3 ammo economies, armour mechanics, per-level kill/time/secret scoring, EXP and 24 achievements, a level generator and a browser map editor — docked because it is one FPS loop with only 5 enemy types, no meta-progression between episodes, and the bonus episode is missing from the tree); polish **1.5/2** (a from-scratch textured raycast renderer, 76 textures with per-weapon hit frames, 6 licensed music tracks and 31 SFX, full English + Russian localisation, an achievements UI with EXP, offline about/licences screens, Fastlane listing and a complete source-art pipeline — docked for zero tests, a 2020-era toolchain, ads in the F-Droid build, and two sprite sheets carrying all five monsters). |

Persistence is a genuine save schema, not a high score: `Persist` stores `version, shadows, clutter, music_vol, sfx_vol, furthest_distance, highest_score, total_distance, total_score, honey_points, accessories, achievements, hat, trail, flower` with versioned load and clamping. Caveats stated honestly: **`achievements` is declared and saved but has no definitions or unlock logic anywhere in the tree** (`find -iname '*achiev*'` = 0, zero `.tres` achievement resources, and `pricetag_type.gd`'s `@export var achievement` is never populated) — it is a stub slot; the game has **one mode and one scene** (`play_1`, `play_2` is a leftover duplicate) with no authored levels, no difficulty select and no daily/endless-plus variant; **the Supporter trail is gated behind a Play Store billing plugin** (`appstore` plugin + `playstore` tag), and the README tells you to edit a `PriceTags` file to bypass it — so one of the 16 cosmetics is effectively IAP-only; and no `android/`, `.apk` or `.pck` ships in the tree, so the phone build is upstream's F-Droid artefact rather than anything verifiable here. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a clean, well-laid-out Godot 4.3 project with `project.godot` committed, an Android export preset with the right two ABIs and a committed F-Droid + Fastlane recipe — proven buildable by F-Droid's own builders and by an itch.io release — docked because the README documents no export command at all, the README's Godot 4.2.2 is stale against the project's 4.3 declaration and the host's 4.7.2, and a 403 MB / 301-asset first import is not a triage-sized operation); runs on phone **3.0/3** (Android-exclusive and phone-shaped by construction: portrait-locked 1080×1920, `Mobile` renderer feature tag, touch-only one-button flick plus drag aiming, a shipped F-Droid package at 1.3 and a portrait store kit — this is exactly the target the game was tuned for, and the material design is explicitly mobile-motivated); depth **1.5/3** (a genuinely balanced survival arcade: a single depleting timer that is simultaneously health and score pressure, fed by three distinct pickups with different yields, over a procedural column of six obstacle archetypes and seven booster variants, with 16 priced cosmetics as a real multi-hour honey-point sink — docked because it is still one endless mode with no authored levels, no difficulty curve beyond generator ramping, no implemented achievements and a single playable scene); polish **1.5/2** (a deliberate mobile rendering trick documented in the README — **all 32 models share one material and one colour-sheet texture, with UVs hand-pointed in Blender to reduce unique shaders on phones** — plus 33 `.blend` masters committed beside the GLBs, 6 music tracks and 20 SFX, music/SFX volume settings, shadow and clutter toggles, a versioned save with load-time clamping, a photo-studio screenshot scene, a privacy policy, and a complete F-Droid store kit — docked for the unimplemented achievement slot, an IAP-gated cosmetic, one English locale and no tests). |
| 92 | **Flowit — Block Puzzle** (`Flowit-Block-Puzzle`) | Grid-filling logic puzzler (single-screen, one-tap): paint every cell of a 5x6 or 6x8 board its border colour by tapping; most cells are inert and the way through is a set of special cells — flood fill, bomb, four directional painters, four rotating painters — over four level packs (easy / medium / hard / community) with a per-level optimal step count to beat | *_6.5 — desk audit_* | **Android only, pure Java + OpenGL ES 1.x through a `GLSurfaceView`** — no NDK, no engine, no native libs: `com.bytehamster.flowitgame`, **versionCode 404 / versionName 4.4**, **minSdk 14** / compile+targetSdk **37**, **AGP 9.1.0** + Gradle wrapper **9.8.1-bin**, Java 8 source, **GPL-3.0** (`LICENSE` is the verbatim GPLv3 text, 35 KB); only two dependencies, `androidx.appcompat:appcompat:1.3.1` and `androidx.annotation:annotation:1.3.0`; single activity, `screenOrientation="portrait"` + `resizeableActivity="false"` + `Theme.NoTitleBar.Fullscreen`, `enableOnBackInvokedCallback="true"`, backup rules and a `lastAppVersion` preference; live on **F-Droid** (`com.bytehamster.flowitgame`) and **Google Play**, with sibling PS Vita and web builds in separate repos; no iOS or desktop target in this tree and no APK | **Desk audit — no build run.** There is **no documented build command** in the README (42 lines: a one-paragraph blurb, five badges, five store screenshots, credits) and no `gradlew`/`build` string matches, so the 90 s window had nothing to run against; the only committed recipe is `.github/workflows/build.yml`, which checks out `submodules: recursive` and runs **`./gradlew assembleDebug`** on JDK 17. **That build cannot succeed from this clone, and the reason is the finding of this audit: `app/src/main/assets` is a git submodule pointing at `git@github.com:Flowit-Game/Levels.git` (pinned `531089e`), it is not checked out, and `git submodule update --init` fails here with "Please make sure you have the correct access rights" — no SSH key for that repo.** The directory is empty on disk, so **all four level packs and every game texture are absent**, and `app/build.gradle` gates the build on them: `preBuild.dependsOn compressLevelFile` reads `assets/levels{Easy,Medium,Hard,Community}.xml` inside a `doLast` that calls `file(path).text` — a missing file aborts configuration before a single class compiles. Toolchain was checked anyway: the wrapper's `gradle-9.8.1-bin` **is** cached and `android-37.0` (compileSdk 37) **is** installed, but **AGP 9.1.0 is not** in the module cache (which holds 8.2.2–8.8.0, 8.13.x and 9.2.0–9.4.1), so a first build would hit the network too. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack — and the pack's `maps/levels=0` is **correct and fatal rather than cosmetic**: zero level files here. What is here is **38 Java files / 3 437 LOC** of hand-written engine: `GLRenderer.java` (a from-scratch GL10 texture/mesh renderer with its own `Mesh`/`Plane`/`TextureCoordinates`/`Drawable` scene graph and an `animation` package), `model/Level.java` (83 lines — boards are **only 5x6 or 6x8**, colour and modifier decoded from fixed-length char strings), `model/Modifier.java` (**18 modifiers: 6 colours, EMPTY, TRANSPARENT, FLOOD, BOMB, and 8 painters — UP/RIGHT/LEFT/DOWN plus ROTATE_UP/RIGHT/LEFT/DOWN**), dedicated `filler/BombFiller`, `filler/FloodFiller`, `filler/DirectionFiller` rule classes, `state/GameState` **445 LOC** (the game loop, with `stepsUsed` / `stepsBest` / `stepsOptimal` readouts and a `stepsImproved` flourish when you beat your record), plus `MainMenuState`, `LevelPackSelectState`, `LevelSelectState`, `SettingsState`, `TutorialState`, `ExitState`; `LevelPack.parsePacks` loads exactly four packs and derives each level's `optimalSteps` by counting commas in the pack's `solution` attribute, i.e. authored solutions ship with the levels. Presentation: **2 selectable colour schemes** (`numberOfColorschemes = textureDrawables.length`, two atlases) with `loading.png`, and per-language drawable variants `drawable-de/fr/hu/it-nodpi` each carrying their own two atlases (~550–600 KB apiece) — 16 PNG/WebP in `res/` against 14 store screenshots, **30 PNG+WebP overall**; **3 WAV SFX** (`click`, `fill`, `won`) played through `SoundPool`; **0 strings localised** — `values/strings.xml` holds only `app_name="Flowit!"`, all on-screen text is drawn as GL number/icon geometry. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched**, so the F-Droid and Play listings are upstream's claim, not my observation; **zero test sources anywhere** (`app/src` contains only `main`, no `test`/`androidTest` directory and no test dependency); the level count is **unverifiable here** — the README's "over 100 levels" lives entirely in the missing submodule, and the queue entry inherited it from there; and the boards are deliberately tiny (30 or 48 cells), so depth is puzzle-craft rather than scale. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (a small, clean, single-module Android project with a committed wrapper, two dependencies, a coherent custom renderer and a CI workflow that names `./gradlew assembleDebug` — docked hard because **the game's entire content is an unfetchable SSH submodule so the build dies at `compressLevelFile` before compilation**, the README documents no command, and AGP 9.1.0 is missing from the host cache); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-exclusive from `minSdk 14` up, portrait-locked single-activity fullscreen, one-tap tap-the-cell input that needs no keyboard or gamepad, GLES 1.x so it runs on very old hardware, offline by construction, shipped on F-Droid and Play at 4.4 — docked because nothing was adb-installed here, no APK ships in the tree, and with the assets submodule absent there is literally no board to play); depth **2.0/3** (18 modifier types resolving through three dedicated filler algorithms, rotating painters that force multi-tap sequencing, four authored packs with per-level optimal step counts and best-score tracking, a scripted tutorial — docked because boards are only 5x6/6x8, there is **no level editor, no daily mode, no procedural generation and no level data in the repo at all**, so replay rests on a pack set that cannot be counted here); polish **1.0/2** (a hand-rolled GLES renderer with its own mesh/animation layer, two colour schemes with per-language variants, immersive fullscreen, three clean SFX, backup rules, Android 13+ back-callback support and a targetSdk of 37 — docked for **zero tests, zero string localisation, three sound effects total and no in-game content besides what loads from an external repo**). |
| 93 | **Free the Bugs** (`Free-the-bugs`) | Educational code-fixing puzzler (short narrative): you are shown a buggy Java-like snippet in an in-game code editor, type the fix, and the level ends when your text matches the intended answer — nine levels across three "worlds", a Trainer character who explains each bug, and a hint dialogue tree for the first four | *_6.5 — desk audit_* | **Android + iOS + Linux from one Godot 4.6 project**: `config/features=PackedStringArray("4.6", "Forward Plus")`, committed `export_presets.cfg` (51 KB) with **six presets** — iOS, **Android arm32 / arm64 / x86-64** (versionCode **131/132/133**, versionName **0.6.4**, `package/unique_name="com.game.$genname"` → `com.game.freethebugs` on F-Droid, `package/signed=true`, `app_category=2`) and Linux desktop; `window/handheld/orientation=**6**` (full sensor) with `stretch/mode="canvas_items"`, `stretch/aspect="expand"` and `textures/vram_compression/import_etc2_astc=true`; **GPL-3.0** (35 KB verbatim LICENSE); live on **F-Droid** (`com.game.freethebugs`, per the README badge) with a complete `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US` kit (**7 phone screenshots + 14 changelog files**), plus desktop packaging recipes for **Flathub** (`others/flathub/*.metainfo.xml`), **Snap** (`snapcraft.yaml`) and **Chocolatey** (`*.nuspec`); CI is `export_releases.yml` plus `aur-upload.yml` / `snap-upload.yml` on `workflow_dispatch` | **Desk audit — no build run: there is no documented command to run.** The README is 1 KB (blurb, F-Droid badge, About, Features, How to Play, Credits) and mentions **no build or export step at all**; the only recipe in the tree is `.github/workflows/export_releases.yml`, a manual Godot-export workflow. The host does carry **Godot 4.7.2** against a project declaring **4.6**, and a first headless import of an 11 MB asset tree (5.3 MB of Fira Code and other faces, 209 audio files) is not a triage-sized operation — so nothing is claimed as compiled, imported or launched. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is literally correct and useless because levels are **resources, not map files**: `game/resources/levels/` holds **9 `LevelData` `.tres` files** (`world_{1,2,3}_level_{1,2,3}`), wired through `level_manager.gd`'s exported `level_definitions` array and advanced linearly by `advance_to_next_level()` with no level select, no save and no restart. Each level is **exactly one differing line**, read by diffing `initial_code` against `answer_code`: missing semicolon (`int health = 100` → `100;`), a string literal assigned to an int (`"100"` → `100`), assignment in a condition (`health = 100` → `health == 100`), wrong receiver (`health` → `hb.health`), undeclared type name (`healthProvider` → `HealthProvider`), wrong receiver again (`sc.health` → `health`), `void` → `String` return type, a missing constructor argument (`new Player()` → `new Player(100)`) and a constructor parameter type (`String health = 0` → `int health = 0`). Verification is **not a compiler**: `Utils.normalize()` collapses tabs, newlines and space runs and then `base_level.gd` does `player_code_normalized == answer_code_normalized`, so a genuinely different but correct fix is rejected. Content volume, counted: **`context.dialogue` 55 lines / 9 knots** (intro plus per-level start and end beats) and **`hints.dialogue` 47 lines** with hint trees for **only W1Level1–3 and W2Level1** plus a shared `HintEndSnippet`; levels 5–9 set `show_hint_button=false` and the Trainer says so explicitly ("extra levels … do not have hints for now"). Engine code is almost entirely third-party: **75 `.gd` / 11 882 LOC** in total but only **466 LOC first-party** (`base_level.gd` 156, `balloon.gd` 177, `level_manager.gd` 45, `level_data.gd` 28, `menu.gd` 24, `utils.gd` 20, `constants.gd` 11, `endscreen.gd` 5) against three vendored addons — **Dialogue Manager 3.7.1 by Nathan Hoad (~7 k LOC)**, **Basic Settings Menu 0.9.1 by the same author, mlm-games** (settings/pause menu, settings profile and applier) and **my-ecosystem-template** (transitions, an `ObjectPool`-backed static audio manager with crossfade, typewriter `UIEffects`, credits scene). Presentation counted: a **Dracula IntelliJ-derived syntax theme** (`assets/theme/dracula_intellij_based.tres`) applied to the `CodeEdit`, a hand-written 177-LOC **dialogue balloon** implementing the `[wave]`/`[wait]`/`[speed]` BBCode effects the dialogue files use, and genuine **responsive layout code** — `_apply_layout()` reflows the level on `viewport.size_changed` into distinct portrait (code 0–55 %, output 55–82 %, tip band) and landscape (code left 64 %, output right) arrangements, with `content_scale_factor=2.0` set in portrait. Localisation is the strongest asset: **`locale/translations.csv` = 658 strings × 28 locales, 657/658 filled in 27 of them (653 for bs)** — ~100 % coverage including lzh, ar, he and th — compiled into 29 `.translation` files; the pot sources are the two `.dialogue` files, so much of that volume is addon UI and Trainer dialogue. Audio: **209 files / 5.3 MB** — a ~130-clip Kenney interface bundle, one 6.3 KB `spoke_sound.ogg` used for **both** the keypress and the dialogue blip through a pooled `AudioM`/`UiAudioM`, and **two music loops** (`Sketchbook 2024-03-20_02`, `Sketchbook 2024-06-16`, 3.8 MB) that **no script under `game/` preloads** — staged assets, not wired playback. Defects found by reading, not assumed: **three levels reuse `level_id = &"W1Level1"`** (`world_2_level_2` and `world_3_level_3` alongside the real one), currently harmless only because both set `show_dialogue=false` — enable dialogue there and the intro replays while the `…End` balloon never resolves; and the project declares the **Forward Plus** renderer with no `gl_compatibility` fallback, which in an Android export is a Vulkan-only requirement. Zero tests anywhere (`*test*` matches are an addon demo scene and a settings test sound). Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched**, so the F-Droid listing and desktop packaging are upstream's claim; the game is **~10 minutes long**, ships no wired music, and five of nine levels have no hint. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (an engine-native Godot project where every UID in `constants.gd` resolves to a real scene, with six committed export presets covering phone and desktop plus Flathub/Snap/Choco metadata and three Actions workflows — docked because the README documents **no** build or export command, and the project is pinned to 4.6 while the host runs 4.7.2, so a first import is untested); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android presets for all three ABIs, sensor orientation plus real portrait/landscape relayout code, `canvas_items`+`expand` scaling with `content_scale_factor` for phone DPI, a `CodeEdit` that works with the Android soft keyboard, ETC2/ASTC texture import, and a live F-Droid package at 0.6.4 — docked because nothing was adb-installed here, no APK ships in the tree, and Forward Plus is the wrong renderer default for old Android hardware); depth **1.0/3** (nine hand-authored bugs spanning semicolons, literals, `==`, receivers, types and constructors across three themed worlds, with dialogue and a branching hint tree on the first four — docked hard because each level is a single-token edit, verification is string equality against one canonical answer, and there is no level select, save, scoring or replay value past the ten-minute linear run); polish **1.5/2** (a real syntax-highlighted editor with a Dracula-IntelliJ theme, animated dialogue balloons and typewriter tips, responsive orientation-aware layouts, settings and pause menus, a transition system, F-Droid + Flathub + Snap + Chocolatey packaging and **658 strings translated into 28 locales at ~100 %** — docked for no wired music, zero tests, and a codebase whose game logic is 466 first-party LOC against ~11 k of vendored addons). |
| 94 | **Freebloks** (`Freebloks`) | Board game (a faithful Blokus port with a real 3D board): place your 21 polyomino pieces so each new one touches a corner but never an edge of your own colour, block the other colours, and score the squares you got down — five rulesets including Blokus Duo and the simplified Junior set, on boards from 13×13 to 23×23 | *_8.0 — desk audit_* | **Android only**, and a genuine eight-module Kotlin/Gradle project rather than a single-module app: `:app` (114 Kotlin files) plus `:game` (67), `:themes` (11), `:data` (10), `:common` (9), `:server` (JNI shim), `:ktx` and `:google-services`; `de.saschahlusiak.freebloks` / `de.saschahlusiak.freebloksvip`, **versionCode 187 / versionName 1.8.7** ("Release 1.8.7 (vc 187)"), **minSdk 26** / compile+targetSdk **37**, **AGP 9.5.0** + Gradle wrapper **9.7.1**, Kotlin **2.4.21**, Compose BOM **2026.09.00** + Material3 1.14.0, Hilt **2.60.1**, Room3 **3.0.3**, KSP 2.3.9, Firebase Crashlytics 21.0.1, **NDK 30.0.16248370** for the `ndkBuild` server leg, `flavorDimensions(app, store)` → standard/vip × google/fdroid, GPL (`COPYING`); a **GLES 2.0** game (`uses-feature glEsVersion=0x00020000 required=true`) with phone **and tablet** screenshots in the README, and Fastlane metadata with **9 phone screenshots**; live on **F-Droid**, **Google Play** and **Amazon Appstore** per the README badges; no iOS, desktop or web target in the tree | **Desk audit — no build run, and the one attempt died in 5 s on an uncached plugin, behind a submodule that is not checked out.** `JAVA_HOME=…adoptium-17 timeout 90 sh ./gradlew --offline -q :app:assembleFdroidDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 5s`**: `Plugin [id: 'com.android.application', version: '9.5.0'] was not found` in Google / MavenRepo / Gradle Central Plugin Repository — the host's AGP module cache stops at **9.4.1**, while the wrapper's **gradle-9.7.1-bin dist, android-37.0 and NDK 30.0.16248370 are all present**, so only the plugin marker is missing. The wall behind that is the finding of this audit, and it is exactly Flowit's shape: `.gitmodules` declares `server/src/main/jni/server/github` → **`git@github.com:shlusiak/Freebloks-3D.git`**, `git submodule status` reports it **uninitialised** (`-8d88789…`), and the directory on disk is **0 bytes** — while `server/src/main/jni/server/Android.mk` compiles **12 `github/src/*.cpp` files** (`board, stone, game, player, turnpool, gameserver, network, logger, timer, ai, constants` + the JNI shim) and `:app` declares `implementation(project(":server"))`. So the game's entire rules engine, **AI and multiplayer server** live in the absent submodule; the clone cannot be built as checked out, and being SSH-only it needs a GitHub key (`git submodule update --init` fails without one). The README *does* document the setup step — a **Getting Started** section saying "initialise the submodule before building" with `git submodule init` / `git submodule update` — but no Gradle command and no Android Studio recipe, which is more than most rows in this catalog. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack — and the pack is **wrong here, not merely uninformative**: it reports `android signals: manifest=no`, yet there are **five `AndroidManifest.xml` files** (`app`, `common`, `game`, `server`, `google-services`); the pack only looks at the clone root. Real content: **215 Kotlin files / 20 805 LOC**, and **111 `@Test` cases across 30 test files** — 25 in `:game` alone covering `Board`, `Shape`, `Stone`, `Player`, `GameConfig`, `Orientation`, plus the whole **wire protocol** (`Header`, `MessageReader`, `MessageWriter`, and one test per message), 4 in `:app` for the GLES board models and scene graph, 1 in `:common`. Rules data read from code, not the blurb: **`Shape.COUNT = 21`** polyominoes with `DEFAULT_STONE_SET = IntArray(21){1}` (one of each — real Blokus) and a hand-authored **`JUNIOR_STONE_SET`** of 20 entries dominated by `2`s with a `0` gap, i.e. a simplified child set; **5 `GameMode` values** — `2_COLORS_2_PLAYERS`, `4_COLORS_2_PLAYERS`, `4_COLORS_4_PLAYERS` (the default), `DUO`, `JUNIOR` — and **6 `FIELD_SIZES` (13, 14, 15, 17, 20, 23)** with a per-mode default (20 for the 4-colour games, 15 for 2-colour, 14 for Duo/Junior), so **30 distinct mode×size combinations** before the AI slider. Difficulty is a real integer in `GameConfig` (`difficulty = DEFAULT_DIFFICULTY = 10`, commented *"higher is easier, lower is harder"*) driven by an in-app `DifficultySlider.kt`, and hints are a first-class message (`MessageRequestHint` / `MessageStoneHint`) rather than a UI afterthought. Multiplayer is the deepest thing here: **19 message classes** (`StartGame`, `SetStone`, `UndoStone`, `GrantPlayer`, `RevokePlayer`, `RequestGameMode`, `Chat`, `GameFinish`, `ServerStatus`…) over a `Header`-framed `MessageReader`/`MessageWriter`, with **Bluetooth** client/server threads, a `MultiplayerViewModel` + lobby screen with chat, and a `MultiplayerNotificationService` (foreground-service remote messaging, hence the notification permissions) — the README states the protocol is **network-compatible with Freebloks 3D on Windows and Linux**, so the phone app can join desktop games. Presentation counted: a **hand-written GLES 2.0 renderer** — 13 files in `view/gles2/` (`Gles2Renderer`, `Gles2BoardRenderer`, `Gles2BackgroundRenderer`, `CurrentStoneRenderer`, `WheelRenderer`, `IntroRenderer`, `ShaderProgram`, `Shaders`, `Gles2Textures`, `Gles2EffectCanvas`) with its own shader programs, so the board is a lit 3D surface you can rotate during play while Compose/Material3 handles the menus; **12 board themes** — 4 `ColorTheme`s (green, blue, and debug-only black/white) plus asset themes with preview art (`field_granite`, `field_marble`, `field_metal`, `field_wood`, `texture_bricks`, `texture_carpet_blue`, `texture_grass`, `texture_metal`, `texture_table_1/2`, `texture_velvet`, `texture_wood_fine`) managed by a dedicated `:themes` module with a `ThemeManager`; **10 `*Screen.kt` + 7 `*Fragment.kt`** (main menu, new game, stones config, lobby, multiplayer, game finish, settings, about, rules, rate-app); **155 strings in `values/`** with locale filters for **9 locales** (`en, de, es, fr, ja, pt, ro, ru, zh-rHK`) and `values-pt-rBR` / `zh-rTW` / `zh-rCN` beyond that; **33 audio files** with **8 in `themes/res/raw`** (`bell`, `chat`, `click1–3`, `drip`, `hint`, `playerout`); a Room **`HighScoreEntry`/`HighScoreDao`** database behind a statistics section, plus a `GooglePlaySection` preference and a `:google-services` module for the Play-Games leaderboards/achievements on the google flavour (the fdroid flavour ships without them). Quality: release runs **R8 (`isMinifyEnabled` + `isShrinkResources` + `proguard-android-optimize.txt`)**, `lint { abortOnError = true; warningsAsErrors = true }` with a baseline, `isGame=true` / `appCategory=game` / TV banner, `targetSandboxVersion=2`, `installLocation=preferExternal`, and `enableOnBackInvokedCallback` — a modern target in every respect. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was compiled, installed or launched**, so the F-Droid/Play listings are upstream's claim; the **native backend is absent from the clone**, so the AI, the rules and the network server were read only as an `Android.mk` file list and a JNI shim; the manifest asks for **6 permissions plus two foreground-service declarations** (`VIBRATE`, `INTERNET`, `WAKE_LOCK`, `BLUETOOTH`, `BLUETOOTH_CONNECT`, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`) because of online play and its notification service, and `allowBackup=true`; Blokus is a fixed board game with no campaign or progression beyond the high-score table; and `minSdk 26` plus a hard GLES 2.0 requirement rules out very old hardware. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (an unusually well-organised eight-module project with a committed wrapper, a version catalog, a documented submodule step, R8 and error-aborting lint — docked hard because **the `:server` sources the app depends on are an uninitialised SSH submodule, 0 bytes on disk**, and the first build dies in 5 s on an uncached AGP 9.5.0, so nothing here can be assembled as checked out); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native since `minSdk 26`, drag-to-place with four rotate handles and a two-finger flip, highlighted legal corners, a rotating 3D board, tablet layouts, saved-and-restored game state, a foreground service so online games survive backgrounding, live on F-Droid and Play at 1.8.7 — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and the tree ships no APK); depth **2.5/3** (5 rulesets × 6 board sizes, real 21-piece Blokus sets plus a Junior set, a difficulty-graded AI with hints, undo and pass, **a 19-message online protocol with chat, lobby and Bluetooth that interoperates with the desktop Freebloks 3D**, per-mode scoring with the 15/20-point all-stones and last-monomino bonuses, and high-score statistics — docked because it is one board game with no campaign, no unlocks and no authored puzzles, and the AI itself is unreadable in this clone); polish **2.0/2** (hand-written GLES 2.0 renderer with its own shaders, 12 board themes with preview art, Material3/Compose UI across 17 screens and fragments, 9 locales, 33 SFX, R8 plus warnings-as-errors lint, and **111 unit tests over 30 files** including a full protocol test suite — a level of Q&A almost nothing else in this catalog reaches). |
| 95 | **Freeciv** (`Freeciv`) | 4X turn-based empire builder (the open-source Civilization II: settler/worker economy, city radii and specialist assignment, an 87-tech tree, diplomacy and senate, wonders, AI opponents and multiplayer) | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **C/Meson, multi-client**: `meson.build` + `meson_options.txt` with client choices `gtk4` (default), `gtk3.22`, `gtk4x`, `qt`, `sdl2`, `sdl3`, `stub`, plus an `fcmp` modpack-installer UI in the same matrix; version **3.3.90** (`fc_version`) at `af90756` (2026-10-18), **GPL-2.0**; `platforms/` ships **appimage, flatpak, macos, windows, emscripten** recipes — the last three (`emsbuild.sh`/`emssetup.sh`) give a browser-touch client — but **there is no `android/` tree, no manifest, no APK and no iOS target anywhere in the clone** (pack agrees: `manifest=no apk_in_tree=0 ios_dir=no`), so phone play rests on the emscripten web build or upstream's separate port, neither verified here | **Desk audit — no build run; the documented build cannot finish in a 90 s triage window and its build system is not installed.** `INSTALL` documents the real recipe in its first section ("Installing Freeciv with Meson", minimum **meson 0.63.0**, then ninja) with `doc/INSTALL.autotools` as the legacy path — so unlike most rows here a build command *is* documented — but `command -v meson` is **empty on this host** (ninja 1.13.2 is present) and the tree is **1 298 C/H files / 600 213 lines of C**, so even with meson a GTK4-client compile is minutes, not 90 s; nothing was configured, compiled or launched and no frame is claimed. Runtime deps that *are* present: **gtk4 4.24.1** and **sdl2 2.32.74** via pkg-config. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is correct and meaningless because the content is ruleset data: **10 complete rulesets** under `data/` — `classic` (52 units / 87 advances / 68 buildings), `civ1` (29/67/47), `civ2` (52/88/68), `civ2civ3` (57/87/73), `multiplayer` (55/89/68), `sandbox` (60/89/80), `goldkeep` (52/87/70), `alien` (33/66/31), `granularity` (18/55/16) and a 2-unit `stub` — each a directory of 11 `.ruleset` files (`units`, `techs`, `buildings`, `terrain`, `effects` at 2 817 lines, `actions` at 1 332, `game`, `governments`, `cities`, `nations`, `styles`) plus `script.lua`/`parser.lua`, so the rules are fully Lua-scriptable. Content volume: **573 nation `.ruleset` files** in `data/nation` (city lists, leaders, flags), **14 authored `.sav` scenarios** (`earth-large`, `earth-small`, `europe_1900_WWI`, `british-isles`, `hagworld`, …), **21 `.modpack` manifests** for the in-game installer, and **11 tilesets** (`trident`, `isotrident`, `hex2t`, `hexemplio`, `isophex`, `amplio`, `amplio2`, `alio`, `cimpletoon`, `toonhex`, `3d`) spanning iso, hex and 3D-style views. Depth is the point and it is all in-tree: a full AI stack (`ai/classic`, `ai/default`, `ai/tex`, `aitraits.c`, `difficulty.c`, `handicaps.c`), a standalone `server/` with auth, barbarians, animals, revolution and city-handover modules, a Lua layer (`lua/`) and the traditional shell checks in `tests/` (`rulesets_autohelp.sh`, savegame generate/upgrade with `rs_test_res`). Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed or launched**, so the meson path is read from `INSTALL`, not observed; the interface is **desktop-keyboard by design** — context menus, right-click, hotkeys, dense city dialogs — which is why the phone leg scores low despite the emscripten target; audio is light (**22 OGG/WAV** in total) and there is no unit-test suite beyond shell scripts. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (canonical meson+ninja build with a documented INSTALL section, an in-tree CI badge, several client choices and every runtime dep already on the host — docked because meson is not installed and 600 k lines cannot be configured+compiled inside triage, so no build was attempted or verified); runs on phone **1.0/3** (no Android or iOS target in the tree at all — no manifest, no APK; the only mobile-ish path is the `platforms/emscripten` browser client, and the right-click/hotkey vocabulary does not translate to touch — docked hard and stated as unverified rather than claimed); depth **3.0/3** (10 distinct rulesets with 18–60 units and 55–89 techs each, 573 nations, 14 scenarios, wonders, governments, diplomacy, a modpack installer, a full AI stack with difficulty and handicap knobs and a dedicated multiplayer server — a genuine 100-hour 4X, the deepest thing in this catalog alongside CATACLYSM); polish **1.5/2** (11 tilesets across iso/hex/3D styles, 5 separate client front-ends, 117 translations, 3 541 art files, Lua-scriptable rules and a 2 817-line effects table — docked for a 1990s-dense desktop UI, only 22 audio files and no automated test suite). |

Mod loading is real: `ModSelectDialog` + `games_listview_item` + `listview_item_mods_wads` let you pull external WADs/PK3s off device storage, and `OptionsFragment`/`AppSettings` expose the GZDoom knobs phones need (resolution divider, gamma, brightness, soundfont, renderer choice via `BestEglChooser`). Localisation is the surprise: **34 `values-*` locale directories** (`ar, bn, cs, de, el, es, fa, fi, fr, he, hi, hu, id, is, it, ja, kk, lt, lv, nl, pa, pl, pt, ro, ru, sk, sr, tg, th, tr, uk, ur, vi, zh`) against **68 English strings**, i.e. a genuinely translated launcher rather than an English one with a badge. Presentation counted: **17 layouts** (`activity_main`, `fragment_launch_gzdoom`, `fragment_options`, `fragment_gamepad`, `fragment_help`, `fragment_online`, `activity_cloud_save`, `dialog_select_mods_wads`, `intro`, …), `drawable-{m,h,xh,xxh,xxxh}dpi` launcher art and **5 images under `res/`**, all in-game art being the 848 + 1 461 WAD sprites. Code is thin and mostly vendored: **16 Java/Kotlin files / 6 754 LOC** including two copies of `org/libsdl/app/SDLActivity.java` and beloko's `SDLLib.java`; the first-party launcher is `Game.java`, `LaunchFragmentGZdoom`, `OptionsFragment`, `EntryActivity`, `DoomWad.kt`, `AppSettings`, `BestEglChooser`, `DirectoryChooserDialog`, `ModSelectDialog`, `MyGLSurfaceView`, `NativeLib`, `SingleMediaScanner`, `Utils`. Caveats, honestly: **the APK was never assembled, installed or launched here** — nothing about the runtime is observed, and the engine is not even present; the fork is **five years dead upstream with no maintainer**; the **armeabi-v7a-only** configuration is the single biggest phone problem, because 64-bit-only devices (Pixel 7 and later, most Android 14+ hardware) refuse to install a 32-bit-only APK at all, and `targetSdk 26` is far below Play's current floor; `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and the SDL-era permission model predate scoped storage; there are **zero tests** anywhere in the tree; and the fork's own licence is GPL-2.0 while the GZDoom it embeds is **GPL-3.0**, a version conflict a distributor would have to resolve. The Freedoom *content* is unimpeachable though — it is a complete, licence-clean Doom I + Doom II with original art and audio, so this clone is excellent as a **data/IWAD donor** even if the app is unbuildable. Breakdown: buildability **0.5/2** (the README documents the whole NDK + submodule + Gradle sequence, and the layout of the app module is conventional — docked hard because the attempt failed in 930 ms on a JDK/Groovy mismatch, `jcenter`, AGP 4.0.2, compileSdk 26 and NDK 21 are all absent from this host, and **the GZDoom engine itself is an uninitialised submodule with zero bytes on disk and no prebuilt `.so` to fall back on**, so no correct toolchain makes this clone buildable as checked out); runs on phone **1.5/3** (Android-exclusive, landscape-locked, designed around a bespoke 30-button touch layer with per-button custom slots, a weapon wheel, an on-screen keyboard and a gamepad fragment, offline by construction, and it ships the actual IWADs so there is nothing to sideload — docked hard because **only an armeabi-v7a APK can be produced**, which excludes most current phones outright, `targetSdk 26` and the legacy storage permission are decade-old, and nothing was installed or launched here); depth **3.0/3** (141 authored maps in-tree — 36 + 32 Freedoom, 9 SIGIL, 32 + 32 10-Sectors — across two full Doom campaigns with original sprites, textures, flats, sounds and music, four difficulty tiers, secrets, deathmatch, GZDoom's complete feature set including DECORATE/decorate-era modding and an in-app external-WAD loader, and a manual PDF: a genuine 30+ hour FPS with a modding scene, not a demo); polish **1.5/2** (34 translated locales, three bundled PK3 resource packs, a chord of curated touch layouts, GZDoom's resolution-divider and renderer pickers exposed for phone hardware, a cloud-save activity, a help/first-run tour and mod selection — docked for a dead 2021 codebase, zero tests, a GPL-2/GPL-3 conflict, no arm64 build and a stock-SDL settings UI next to GZDoom's desktop-grade option wall). |

Code measured: **73 Java files / 9 438 LOC**, with **world/ 51 files** (`entities` 12 — `Frog1/Frog1Ranged/Frog2/Frog2Ranged/Frog2Spinner`, `PistolZombie`, `RifleZombie`, `ZombieBoss1`, `ZombieBossBase`, `NPC`, `GameEntity`, `FrogBase`; `player` 11 — `AkRifle`, `Pistol`, `Firearm`, `WeaponMod`, `Statistics`, four `Roller` classes for the random-weapon generator; `geom` 5 hand-written shapes/raycasters including `Octree` + `GridRayIt`), `ui/` 4 (`TouchPad`, `TabPane`, `LinePlotRenderer`, `GameOverDialog`), root 8. Mobile work is deliberate, not incidental: a dedicated **`TouchPad`** class, a **`LowResWorldRenderer`** (downsampled 3-D with a 2-D overlay for phone fill rate), landscape lock, and finger-sized Menu/RELOAD/Next buttons. Zero tests anywhere; scope is honest jam-scale — 4 short levels, ~40–60 minutes, no save, no unlocks, no difficulty setting, one music track. Judged from the run, not the README: phone play is proven (installed, launched, gameplay rendered at 29 k/19 k colors on a software-GL emulator) and buildability is proven (24.6 MB debug APK in one shot), but depth is a four-level shooter and the documented control scheme the tutorial prints is still *"WASD to move, mouse to aim"*, with touch layered on top. | 98 | **GLXY** (`GLXY`) | Gravity sandbox / toy physics sim (a simplified N-body playground: tap to drop a particle, drag to sling it, pinch to zoom, and watch the whole field pull on itself — Newtonian inverse-square attraction between every pair, with a toggle between bouncing and merging on contact; **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, 10 273 bytes); no iOS, desktop, or web module in the tree and no APK checked in — Android plus upstream's F-Droid listing only | **Desk audit — no build run; the vague build was attempted and died in 43 s on a JDK/Groovy mismatch, behind a toolchain this host no longer carries.** The README's entire build section is two lines — *"You can build it using gradle or Android Studio"* — with **no command**, no SDK level, no JDK; the wrapper *is* committed (`gradle-6.7.1-bin`), so it was tried: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-amd64-linux.2 ANDROID_HOME=~/Android/Sdk timeout 90 sh ./gradlew --offline :android:assembleDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 43s`**, `Could not compile settings file '…/settings.gradle'` / **`Unsupported class file major version 61`** from Gradle 6.7.1's bundled Groovy ASM — Gradle 6.x cannot even parse a script on **JDK 17**, and this box has only JDK **17 / 25 / 27** (no 8–15 anywhere), so the failure lands before a single project file is read. Behind that the stack is stale on its own terms: **AGP 3.5.4 is not in the host module cache** (8.2.2 → 9.4.1 only), **compileSdk 30 is not installed** (android-35 / 36 / 37.0 only), and the wrapper dist itself is absent from `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists`, so `--offline` had nothing to run even with a correct JDK. Nothing was compiled, installed or launched and **no frame is claimed**. The pack's `maps/levels=0` is **correct and meaningless** — this game has no levels by design — so the content was read rather than counted: **14 Java files / 1 303 LOC** total, of which **13 files / 1 285 LOC are `core/`** and 18 lines are the Android launcher; the whole simulation is **`actor/Universe.java`, 462 lines**, plus `screen/GameScreen.java` 163 (the bottom option bar), `actor/SettingsDialog.java` 145, `model/Particle.java` 118, `screen/Screen.java` 90, `GlxyGame.java` 78, `screen/MainMenuScreen.java` 61, `actor/TrailParticleManager.java` 56, `model/Settings.java` 30, `model/TrailParticle.java` 23, `screen/BigTextButton.java` 22, `screen/MenuScreen.java` 21, `data/ImageCache.java` 18. The physics is genuine, not faked: a `G = 0.1f` constant with a **half-strength `sG` variant for slingshot launches**, an **O(n²) all-pairs loop** computing `G·m₁·m₂/d²` and dividing by distance again for a unit direction, **mass derived cubically from radius** (`0.5·r³` in `Particle.radius()`), and two distinct contact modes — *bounce*, which applies a minimum-translation push scaled by inverse **mass** on both bodies, and *merge*, which grows the larger body by `√(r/2)` and kills the smaller while **re-targeting the camera if the swallowed particle was the followed one**. Radii span **5–80** in four colour-banded classes (small grey-blue, medium yellow, large red, huge mauve) with linear RGB interpolation between bands, zoom is clamped **0.1×–16×**, and each particle paints a motion streak from `oldx/oldy` to its new position under a **500-trail global cap**. Presentation is deliberately tiny: **12 asset files / 264 KB** (`bg.png` 129 KB, `default.fnt`/`default.png` bitmap font, `gear.png`, `glxy-logo.png` + `.svg`, a 4 KB `textures.png`/`textures.txt` atlas for the circle sprite, `uiskin.atlas`/`.json`/`.png` Scene2D skin) and **9 Android resource files** — **one layout** (`main.xml`), **one string** (`app_name`), one `styles.xml`, one `color.xml`, and launcher icons at five densities — because the HUD is built in Scene2D code, not XML. Mobile design is deliberate rather than incidental: a libGDX `GestureDetector` drives it (tap spawns, double-tap locks the camera onto a particle, pinch zooms about the pinch midpoint, drag either pans or slings a particle with momentum depending on the Pan/Follow toggle), a tap target is **inflated 1.7×** *"to allow for finger inaccuracy"*, and the S/M/L buttons **collapse to single letters when `getWidth() <= 320`**. Store packaging is real for its era: **20 Fastlane files across 15 locales** (`en-US, de, es, fr, he, id, pl, pt, pt-BR, pt-PT, ro, ru, tr, uk, zh-CN`) with 4 English phone screenshots and a localised short description each — though **the app itself has exactly one string and one locale**, so the translation is storefront-only. Caveats, honestly: nothing was built or launched, so the F-Droid presence is upstream's claim; there is **zero audio and zero music** (0 ogg/mp3/wav in the tree); **no tests** of any kind; **no save or scene persistence** — closing the app loses the universe; the UI is Scene2D-default styled with the stock libGDX skin and bitmap font, and **hard-codes English labels** in code (`"Small"`, `"Bounce Particles on Collision"`); particles are **never culled for distance** (`Particle.update()` only integrates position and returns `!dead`, so escapees accumulate forever) and the pair loop is quadratic with **no particle cap**, so a long slinging session degrades frame rate on weak hardware; the last commit is `d5d0d0b`, **2021-06-30**, so it is abandoned-but-complete at 2.3. Breakdown: buildability **0.5/2** (committed wrapper, conventional gdx-liftoff two-module layout, four ABIs wired and R8 configured — docked hard because the README documents no command at all, the attempt failed in 43 s on Gradle 6.7.1 vs JDK 17, and AGP 3.5.4, compileSdk 30 and the wrapper dist are all absent from this host, so it cannot be assembled without installing a decade-old toolchain); runs on phone **2.0/3** (Android-exclusive with **minSdk 14**, an app that shipped on F-Droid, genuinely touch-first gestures with fat-finger padding and a small-screen button fallback, four ABIs and **no permissions whatsoever** — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and `targetSdk 30` is well below the current Play floor, though it still installs and runs); depth **1.0/3** (the sandbox mechanic is real — inverse-square N-body attraction with mass from volume, bounce-or-merge contacts, trails, follow-cam and slingshot momentum — but it is a toy: no levels, no objective, no win or lose, no progression, no saved state, and the only number on screen is a particle count, so replay is whatever you invent); polish **1.0/2** (elegant minimalism done on purpose — a custom `ShapeRenderer` renderer instead of a sprite engine, mass-aware collision resolution that respects the followed particle, colour-interpolated bodies, camera clamps and a capped trail pool, plus a 15-locale storefront kit — docked for the stock libGDX skin and font, zero audio, zero tests, hard-coded English UI, no persistence and an uncapped O(n²) loop that slows as the field grows). |

The "sleepiness" is a real, tuned mechanic rather than decoration: every **5th** sheep picks one of **5 CSS filters** — `blur(5px)`, `grayscale(80%)`, `hue-rotate(180deg)`, `invert(100%) brightness(30%)`, `brightness(40%)` — held for `filterDuration` starting at **1 000 ms and growing +200 ms each time**, so the washes get longer as you count. Art is deliberately minimal — **9 PNGs in `res/`** (a `gobandroid_tv.png` banner, launcher icons) because the board, stones and grid are drawn by `GoBoardView` rather than sprite assets. Caveats, honestly: nothing was built, installed or launched, so the F-Droid/Play presence is upstream's claim; the app is **abandoned-but-complete** (last commit `1d93a44`, **2023-01-01**, a Dependabot bump); **no AI ships in the APK** — a first-run player with no GnuGo installed has nobody to play; `targetSdk 30` and the whole `com.android.support` 28 stack are years below the current Play floor; and there is no Kotlin coroutines/modern architecture story, just an older Android codebase. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a conventional single-module Android project with a committed wrapper, a full GPLv3 text and 30 test sources — docked because the README documents no command, the plugin classpath still resolves through the dead `jcenter()`, and the build config is fetched from a mutable remote URL at configure time, so the clone is not self-contained or reproducible); runs on phone **2/3** (Android-native since minSdk 21, minSdk floor low enough for old hardware, a shipped F-Droid package, a board that is touch-drawn with zoom, portrait/landscape layouts for small, large and land configurations, and offline-by-nature content — docked because nothing was adb-installed here and single-player needs a second app installed); depth **2.5/3** (real Go rules with ko and captures, a full SGF editor with variations, annotations and bookmarks, 71 bundled records including 25 pro reviews and a Lee Sedol–AlphaGo set, 33 graded tsumego problems, scoring and engine play over GTP — docked half a point because the app itself contains no opponent and no ladder/campaign, so the depth depends on content the user loads or installs); polish **1.5/2** (249 strings across 11 languages, custom zooming board rendering, sound on captures, a record/unzip pipeline, 30 test sources, F-Droid packaging — docked because the UI is 2014-era Holo/support-library styling, the README is a dead-link stub, and a modern reviewer will notice the missing in-app onboarding to the GnuGo dependency). |
