| 105 | **Halma** (`Halma`) | Abstract strategy — classic Halma/Chinese-checkers family: move one piece a single empty step or chain-jump over occupied squares as far as the board allows, first player to occupy the far corner outright wins; 1–6 players on two board shapes (a **Square** 16×16 grid with cut corners) vs a greedy bot or two people on one device | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **libGDX 1.14.2, four Gradle modules — `core`, `android`, `desktop` (LWJGL3), `html` (GWT)** from one shared `core`; Android `app.halma`, **versionCode 15 / versionName 15.0**, **minSdk 19** / compile+targetSdk **35**, **AGP 8.4.2**, committed wrapper **gradle-9.6.1-bin**, Java **sourceCompatibility 8**, **Apache-2.0**; landscape-locked single activity with `configChanges` for orientation/screenSize and `isGame`/`appCategory=game` set; **zero `uses-permission` entries** and no `<uses-sdk tools:...>` overrides — only `<uses-feature glEsVersion="0x00020000" required="true"/>`; live on **F-Droid as `app.halma`, Google Play as `app.halma` and the web as halma.app** per README badges (the F-Droid badge *image* even points at a stale `rocks.poopjournal.halma` slug while its link goes to `app.halma`); no iOS target, **no APK in the tree** | **Desk audit — no build run, and the documented-command attempt died in 2 s.** The README is **40 lines** — three store badges, a contributing note pointing at `.github/CONTRIBUTING.md`, a Weblate link and the licence — with **no build, run or install command at all**, so the conventional one was tried: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17 ANDROID_HOME=~/Android/Sdk timeout 88 sh ./gradlew --offline -q :android:assembleDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 2s`** with `No cached version of org.wisepersist:gwt-gradle-plugin:1.1.16`, `org.gretty:gretty:4.1.6` **and** `com.android.tools.build:gradle:8.4.2` all "available for offline mode" — note the web module's plugins are on the **root `buildscript` classpath**, so the GWT toolchain blocks the Android build too, and the host's AGP cache jumps 8.2.2 → 8.6.1 with no 8.4.2. What *is* in place, so the failure is dependency-specific rather than a broken clone: the **`gradle-9.6.1-bin` dist is cached** and **android-35 is installed**. One thing worth flagging, unverified: dependabot's `0a25815` (2026-07-03) bumped **only the wrapper to Gradle 9.6.1** while `build.gradle` still pins **AGP 8.4.2**, a pairing AGP 8.4 was never tested against — so even with network this looks broken at configure time, not merely slow. Nothing was compiled, installed or launched; **no frame and no APK are claimed**. The pack's `maps/levels=0` is literally true and meaningless here — Halma boards are *constructed in code*, not loaded: `Board.createSquare()` builds **32×16 half-step coordinates → a 16×16 field grid** and then deletes 24 named corner cells to carve the four home camps, while `Board.createStar()` builds **24×16** with 16 deleted cells to make six arms; the shapes are Java, and `assets/6star_board.txt` (**16 rows × 13 digit columns**, codings 0–7) is a leftover map that **no Java source reads** (grep finds zero references). Player counts are enforced in `PlayerCounter.update()`: **people + bots ≤ 4 on the square board, ≤ 6 on the star**, ≥1 human, bots ≥0 — so 1–6 players. Code measured: **32 Java files / 2 613 LOC**, small and readable, split across two UI generations that still coexist (`core/…/menu/` + `Play`/`RulesScreen`/`AboutScreen`, a legacy Scene2D path, alongside `core/…/redesign/` with `MainMenu`, `PlayConfig`, `Settings`, `About`, `PlayerCounter`) — the biggest files are `Board.java` 268, `RulesScreen.java` 253, `Field.java` 214, `Player.java` 152, `Settings.java` 122, `PlayConfig.java` 108. **The move engine is genuinely correct Halma, not a toy**: `Field.showAllWays()` marks every empty neighbour and then recurses through `jump()` → `afterJump()` → `jump()`, so **multi-hop chain jumps across the whole board are legal in one turn**, with a `jumpedFields` set to stop back-jumping and a `retry_threshold = 500` recursion guard (a numeric cap, not a rules rule). **The AI is the weak link**: `Player.randomColoredField()` picks one of its own pieces *at random*, then `randomField()` chooses among the marked `POSSIBLE` fields the one with the smallest `dst2` to `board.lower` — a greedy nearest-to-goal pick with no lookahead, no blocking, no chain preference and no difficulty handling. The `Settings` screen's **Easy / Medium / Hard** buttons are **cosmetic**: `HalmaPreferences.setGameLevel/getGameLevel` are read *only* by `Settings.java` itself — grep across the tree shows **no gameplay code ever consults the level** — and the **Online/Offline** toggle in `PlayConfig` is likewise inert, with **no socket, HTTP or networking class anywhere in `core` or `android`**. Two further real defects found in the tree: `WinnerChecker.check()` is written **for red only** — it asks whether `Field.RED` has taken any of the six home regions or whether `board.lower` is full without red — so on a 3–6 player board it never detects a green, blue or purple win; and `minifyEnabled false` ships unshrunk. Presentation counted: **81 files in `assets/`** — **41 PNGs**, **11 `.fnt` bitmap fonts**, 5 Scene2D atlas/json pairs (including a third-party **Flat Earth UI** skin bundled alongside the project's own `halma` skin), **5 `.aseprite` sources** (BlueButton, GrayButton, Minus, Plus, WhiteButton — real editable source art, not just exports), 6 board-piece colours (`BoardPiece_{Red,Blue,Green,Purple,SkyBlue,Yellow}.png`) plus `Red/Green/Purple PieceUp/PieceDown` states, `Field.png`/`BoardSpace.png`/`HintPiece.png` and a `triangle-p.png`/`tri_board.png` pair. **Localisation is broad but lumpy**: **13 `strings*.properties` bundles** (base + `da, de, es, fr, id, ja, nb_no, ru, sv, ta, tr, zh_CN`) whose key counts are **28 / 28 / 28 / 28 / 24 / 15 / 16 / 28 / 28 / 28 / 28 / 28 / 26** — i.e. **seven locales complete at 28 keys, and four well short** (`id` 15, `ja` 16, `fr` 24, `de`/`da` 26), everything else falling back to English by design (the file header says so). Store kit is real: **Fastlane metadata in 12 locales**, `title` + `short_description` + changelogs everywhere, `full_description` in 7, phone screenshots and a `video.txt` only in `en-US`/`de-DE`. **There is no audio whatsoever** — zero Sound/Music references in either module — and **zero tests**: no `src/test`, no `*Test*.java`, no CI workflow beyond a dependabot merge. Caveats, honestly: nothing built or launched here; the difficulty ladder advertised in Settings does nothing; the win check is red-only in a game the same build lets you play with six people; the README documents no build command; and the wrapper/AGP pair looks self-inconsistent at the last commit. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (clean four-module libGDX layout, committed wrapper, cached Gradle 9.6.1 and installed android-35, Java 8 source level — docked because the README documents no command, the attempt failed in 2 s on three uncached root-buildscript plugins including the GWT/gretty pair that every build inherits, and AGP 8.4.2 against Gradle 9.6.1 is a dependabot-only wrapper bump with no AGP bump); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native since minSdk 19, **zero permissions**, landscape-locked full-screen touch UI, config-change handling so a rotate does not restart the match, plus shipped F-Droid/Play/web releases from one codebase — docked because nothing was adb-installed and there is no audio or haptic feedback for a board game that needs move confirmation); depth **2.0/3** (a correct Halma move engine with true multi-hop chain jumps, two programmed board shapes — 16×16 square for 2–4 and a 24×16 six-armed star for up to 6 — and free mix of humans and bots up to the cap, which is more board variety than most abstract rows here — docked because the bot is a random-piece-plus-nearest-target picker with no lookahead, the Easy/Medium/Hard setting is dead code, the win condition only tracks red, and there is no campaign, ladder, timer or scoring meta); polish **1.5/2** (custom 41-PNG art with committed Aseprite sources, two full Scene2D skins, 13 locale bundles and 12-locale Fastlane metadata with screenshots and changelogs — docked for **four locales under 90 % key coverage**, **no audio at all**, **zero tests**, an unminified release, and two shipped toggles (Online/Offline, difficulty) that do nothing). |
| 106 | **Ham-Test** (`Ham-Test`) | **NOT A GAME — US Amateur Radio licence exam trainer, misfiled into the game queue** (Technician / General / Amateur Extra multiple-choice flashcard *Study mode* plus a full 35-question *Practice test* graded against the FCC 74 % pass mark, with optional daily practice and 15-minute break reminders) | *_6.5 — desk audit_* | **Android only, Compose-first** (`com.hazelhope.dubster.hamtest`, **versionCode 122 / versionName 1.2.2**, **minSdk 25** / **targetSdk 37** declared through the new AGP `compileSdk { }` block, **AGP 9.4.1**, Kotlin **2.4.20**, **KSP 2.3.9**, Compose BOM + `material3` + `navigation-compose`, **Room 2.8.5** with the KSP compiler, kotlinx-serialization-json, WorkManager + core-splashscreen, Gradle wrapper **9.6.0-bin**, Java source/target **11**; **GPL-3.0** (the full 35 KB GPLv3 text is the root `LICENSE`); single `:app` module, 11 MB clone; live on **F-Droid** as `com.hazelhope.dubster.hamtest` and on GitHub Releases per the README badges; no desktop, web or iOS target in this tree and **no APK checked in** (the README points at a separate Flutter web port at hamtest.web.app / `DubsterDev/HamTest`) | **Desk audit — no build run.** The README (44 lines: two store badges, one F-Droid badge, a one-line blurb, a Features list, pool validity dates, four screenshots) documents **no build, install or run command whatsoever**, so under the 90 s triage rule the window was never entered — nothing is claimed as compiled, installed or launched, and **no frame is claimed**. The pack's headline counts are corrected in three places: `manifest=no` is **wrong** — `app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` exists, declares a single `MainActivity` with an app-shortcuts meta-data and **exactly one `uses-permission`: `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`** (no `INTERNET`, so the whole app is offline by construction); `maps/levels=0` is correct but meaningless (the content is a question bank); and `images=43` decomposes to **5-density launcher icons `ic_launcher`/`ic_launcher_round` (10 webp) + 4 Fastlane screenshots ×2 copies (8 png) + 14 exam figure PNGs + ~11 vector drawables** (`outline_check`, `outline_settings`, emoji faces, `logo.xml`, `ic_launcher_foreground/background`) — **zero game sprites, zero audio, zero models**, and only **3 asset files total**. The real content was read from `app/src/main/assets/`, not trusted from the pack: **1 431 exam questions bundled offline** — `technician.json` **409** (171 KB), `general.json` **423** (179 KB), `extra.json` **599** (260 KB) — each a `HamQuestion{id, question, answers[4], correct, correct_letter, refs, figure, explanation?}`; **14 of the 1 431 carry a figure** that exists as a drawable (`t_1..t_3`, `g7_1`, `e5_1`, `e6_1..3`, `e7_1..3`, `e9_1..3`); and **only 19 questions have an `explanation`** (all in the Technician pool — the README is honest that explanations are a crowdsourced work in progress). The exam simulation is the interesting part: `PracticeTestViewModel.generateQuiz()` buckets the loaded pool by **`question.id.take(3)`** — the FCC *subelement* prefix — then draws **one random question per subelement** and shuffles, i.e. it reproduces the real exam blueprint (35 questions, ~one per subelement) instead of picking 35 at random; `Utils.shuffleHamQuestion()` re-shuffles the four answers on **every** presentation and recomputes the correct letter from A–D, and grading is `floor(correct/size*100)` with `passed = score >= 74` rendered against a Material3 `extendedColors.successContainer`. Study mode is a genuine spaced-review loop rather than a pager: the pool screen reports `inUsePoolSize` and `weakQuestions`, a per-question `score` is written back to Room (**negative = weak**, and the *first time* a question is seen it can auto-select the correct answer before the alternatives), the practice round **overrides `PracticeTestLiveData` with a deliberate `-2` score** on the question you got wrong so it resurfaces in study mode, and the whole pool can be narrowed to the weak set. Persistence is real: **Room database version 5** with **four hand-written migrations** (1→2 `firstTime`, 2→3 the `settings` table, 3→4 a `question_info` rebuild that adds `pool` to the primary key, 4→5 `lastSeenAt`), two entities — `UserQuestionInfo(id, pool, score, firstTime, lastSeenAt)` keyed `PRIMARY KEY(id, pool)` so the same numeric id in two pools does not collide — and a key/value `SettingsItem`. Code measured: **18 Kotlin files / 2 772 LOC** (17 sources in `main`, the 18th is a 24-line instrumented-test template): `StudyMode.kt` **575** (the biggest file, the flashcard UI), `MainActivity.kt` **437** (nav + WorkManager scheduling), `Settings.kt` **387** (theme, pool selection, reminder toggles), `PracticeTest.kt` **348**, `StudyModeViewModel.kt` **223**, `PracticeTestViewModel.kt` **177**, `SharedComponents.kt` **154**, `Theme.kt` 101, `DataClasses.kt` 77, `NotificationWorker.kt` 69, `HamTestDatabase.kt` 45, the two DAOs (34/32), `Utils.kt` 18, `Color.kt` 20, `Type.kt` 34. Presentation is tasteful for a hobby app but thin in places: Material3 with a custom `ExtendedColors` layer, dynamic-colour-aware theme, adaptive icon with a monochrome layer, `core.splashscreen` launch theme, an app-shortcuts XML, `allowBackup` plus both `backup_rules`/`data_extraction_rules`; against that, **`res/values/strings.xml` holds only 7 strings with zero `values-<locale>` directories** — all UI copy is hard-coded English in Kotlin — the release build **sets no `minifyEnabled`**, and testing is the untouched Android template (`ExampleUnitTest` 17 lines, `ExampleInstrumentedTest` 24 lines), so real coverage is zero and there is no CI. Store metadata is **13 files in `en-US` only**: short + full description, icon, 4 phone screenshots and **changelogs 117–122**, which line up with `versionCode 122`. Last commit `6b9d853`, **2026-09-19** ("Release version 1.2.2") — actively maintained. Pool validity is stated in the README and dates the content honestly: Technician **2026–2030**, General **2023–2027**, Amateur Extra **2024–2028**. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a clean single-module modern Compose/Room/KSP project with a committed wrapper, version catalogs and pinned plugin versions — docked because the README documents **no** build command at all, AGP 9.4.1 is a bleeding-edge pairing with Gradle 9.6.0 and nothing was assembled here, so the build is unverified); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native Compose at **minSdk 25 / target 37**, a shipped F-Droid package, touch-first one-handed quiz UI with soft-keyboard-free answer buttons, a 1 431-question bank **bundled in assets** so it works in a dead zone or an exam hall, one notification permission and no network permission at all — docked because nothing was adb-installed in this pass); depth **1.0/3** (as a *game* there is none — no opponent, no score chase, no levels, no progression; as an *exam trainer* the loop is honest and unusually well engineered: 1 431 real FCC questions across three pools, subelement-stratified exam sampling, per-attempt answer shuffling, 74 % pass grading, weak-question tracking and persisted per-question history — which is what earns it the point); polish **1.5/2** (Material3 + custom extended colours, four Room migrations rather than destructive fallback, adaptive + monochrome icons, splashscreen, shortcuts, backup rules, WorkManager reminders, F-Droid packaging with six changelogs — docked for a single English locale with hard-coded UI strings, no R8 on release, zero real tests, and explanations on only 19 of 1 431 questions). **Recommendation: drop it from `GAMES.queue`** — it is an exam-prep utility that entered through the F-Droid *Games* category sweep and will keep resurfacing in the judging backlog; it is a good app and a fine offline reference, but it is not a game. |

The documented-command route was checked before giving up: the **README is 22 lines** — a blurb, a How to Play list, five Features bullets and the GPL link — with **no build, install or run instruction whatsoever**. Two CI workflows exist (`.github/workflows/build.yml` and `test.yml`, both `workflow_dispatch` only) and they *do* encode the real recipe — `subosito/flutter-action@v2` on stable, `flutter pub get`, a base64 keystore written to `android/app/upload-keystore.jks`, then `flutter build apk --release` plus `--split-per-abi` — but that is a GitHub-runner recipe, not a local one. The clone is also **not buildable as checked out even with an SDK**: `android/.gitignore` ignores **`gradlew`, `gradlew.bat` and `gradle-wrapper.jar`**, and `ls android` confirms only `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties` survives — the wrapper payload is missing and Gradle would have to be supplied externally at 9.1.0. `android/app/build.gradle.kts` also dereferences `keystoreProperties["keyAlias"] as String` for the release signing config unconditionally (guarded only by `key.properties` existing), so a release assemble fails on a null cast unless a keystore file is planted first. **Zero tests**: there is no `test/` and no `integration_test/` directory at all, despite `flutter_test`/`flutter_lints` being declared dev dependencies. Counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` — correct and meaningless, because **levels are generated, not stored**: **17 Dart files / 2 521 LOC** — `home_view.dart` 448, `hue_sort_screen.dart` 417, `settings_view.dart` 264, `level_select_view.dart` 220, `hue_sort_engine.dart` 214, `hue_sort_provider.dart` 207, `how_to_play_view.dart` 185, `providers.dart` 96, `tangible_button.dart` 75, `app_theme.dart` 71, `hive_service.dart` 69, `app_colors.dart` 55, `home_view_model.dart` 52, `main.dart` 48, `progress_repository.dart` 37, `user_progress.dart` 32, `user_progress_adapter.dart` 31. The mechanic is small and honest: `HueSortEngine.generateLevel()` seeds a `Random(level)` (deterministic per level; `isRandom` swaps in an unseeded `Random`), draws **four corner colours in HSV** whose hue span *is* the difficulty — **easy ≈70–100°+ per corner, medium span 80–110°, hard 35–55°, expert 15–25°** — fills every other cell by **true bilinear interpolation** of those four corners, pins indices `[0, n−1, n(n−1), n²−1]`, and **shuffles the remaining cells as a permutation of the exact solution set**. That makes every puzzle solvable by construction, and `useHint()` always resolves since the target colour is guaranteed to exist in another slot — but it also means the puzzle is pure visual matching, not inference. Ladder measured in code: `sizeForLevel` is **3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10 at ≤5, ≤15, ≤30, ≤50, ≤75, ≤100, ≤130, then capped at 10** — a 10×10 board (**100 tiles, 96 movable**) forever after level 130; `bossLevels = [5, 15, 30, 50, 75, 100]` are forced `expert` and flagged in the HUD with 👑 and "Master the Gradient"; `difficultyForLevel` recycles in **10-level cycles after 50** (1–2 easy, 3–6 medium, 7–8 hard, 9–0 expert); the level picker renders `max(60, currentLevel + 20)` tiles. Hints are rationed by board size — **1 for ≤4, 2 for ≤7, 5 for ≥8** — and undo is an unbounded `List<HueSortState>` history. What is thin: **no timer, no move limit, no star rating, no score, no daily and no authored campaign** — difficulty is only hue span and grid size, and the whole progression is a **2-field Hive record** (`currentLevel`, `highestLevelCompleted`; adapter `typeId 0`, `writeByte(2)`) so finishing a level advances a counter and nothing else. Presentation counted: **24 Android resource files**, of which **22 are launcher/adaptive-icon PNGs** (`ic_launcher`, `_foreground`, `_background`, `_monochrome` × 5 densities, plus a `mipmap-anydpi-v26` XML), the remainder being `values`/`values-night` `styles.xml` and two `launch_background.xml` — and **no `strings.xml` at all**, i.e. every label is hard-coded English in Dart, **one locale, zero translations**. UI polish is real where it exists: a hand-built dark `AppThemeSkin` (**one skin, `Charcoal` — `allSkins` holds a single entry**, so the skin plumbing is inert), a custom `TangibleButton` with press physics, `AnimatedContainer` tile transitions, and **six `HapticFeedback` call sites** (heavy on solve, medium on swap/hint, light on select/undo) — but there is **no audio whatsoever** (`grep` for `audioplayers`/`just_audio`/`soundpool` returns nothing and `assets/` holds only the font). Settings are three real toggles (show wrong-tile count, show the hint button, use system font) plus a confirmed **Reset Progress**. Store kit is minimal but valid: **8 Fastlane files in `en-US` only** — title, 69-byte short description, a **298-byte** full description, icon and **4 phone screenshots** — plus two issue templates. Last commit `f6bc66c`, "Update V1.0.4 , Added #3". Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (clean modern Riverpod/Hive Flutter layout with a pinned pubspec and two CI workflows that spell out the release build — docked because the README documents no command, **no Flutter SDK exists on this host**, the Gradle wrapper script and jar are gitignored away, the release signing config null-casts without a keystore, and nothing was built here); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-exclusive Flutter at touch-first portrait scale, zero permissions so it is fully offline by construction, adaptive + monochrome launcher icon, haptic feedback and four store screenshots — docked because nothing was adb-installed in this pass and `targetSdk` is left to the Flutter default rather than declared); depth **1.5/3** (a genuine 3×3→10×10 ladder with four tuned hue-span tiers, six forced-expert boss levels, a 3–10 grid × 4 difficulty Random mode, rationed hints and undo is a decent coffee-break loop — docked because there is exactly **one mechanic** with no timer, score, stars, unlocks or authored content, progress is a single integer, and at expert's 15–25° span adjacent tiles can be nearly indistinguishable, which reads as unfair rather than hard); polish **1.5/2** (custom theme, bespoke button, animated tiles, six haptic hooks, a How to Play screen, three persisted settings with reset, Fastlane store kit and CI — docked for **zero audio, zero tests, one locale with no string resources, a one-entry theme-skin system**, and a 298-byte store description). |

**Android via `hyperroid/`**, a complete NDK + CMake Gradle port — `com.roguetemple.hyperroid`, **minSdk 21 / targetSdk 35**, compileSdk 35 / buildTools 35.0.0, `externalNativeBuild.cmake` over `hyperroid/app/CMakeLists.txt` building native module `hyper` with `abiFilters 'x86','x86_64','armeabi-v7a','arm64-v8a'` and `ldLibs "GLESv3 -llog"`, Gradle wrapper **8.9-bin**, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` the only live permission (the storage permission is commented out); Land logic is split into `landgen.cpp` (3 239 lines — per-land generators), `monstergen.cpp` 834, `orbgen.cpp` 638 and `items.cpp` 769; the whole game is a hand-written C++ engine (RogueViz) with its own hyperbolic geometry (`heptagon.cpp`, `binary-tiling.cpp`, `archimedean.cpp`, `goldberg.cpp`, `irregular.cpp`, `quotient.cpp`, `arbitrary` tilings), rendering (`graph-*.cpp`, `shaders.cpp`, `raycaster.cpp`), map editor (`mapeditor.cpp`), multiplayer (`multi.cpp`), and a mobile shim (`mobile.cpp`, `fake-mobile.cpp`). depth **3/3** (the deepest game in this catalog: ~85 distinct lands each with its own monsters, walls, treasure, orbs, terrain generation and unlock condition, 180 monsters / 121 walls / 155 items, a treasure-driven difficulty curve, permanent death, a Hell endgame gated on Orbs of Yendor, plus the hyperbolic geometry itself — quotients, tilings and non-Euclidean distance change how you navigate, so there is nothing else quite like it, and fifteen years of post-1.0 updates); polish **1.5/2** (57 MB of bespoke music, 84 sound effects, nine language files, in-game help and a published Doxygen manual, smooth animated tile/vector rendering on ordinary hardware — docked because the default presentation is still ASCII glyphs with only 10 PNGs and 4 models in-tree, the code is legacy CRLF C++ with no automated test suite or CI, and the Android path requires a manual `copy.sh` step). **Recommendation: keep — this is a top-tier title on the list; a full build + play should be scheduled outside the triage window, and the Android port is the natural first phone target.** |
| 110 | **I Keep Having This Dream** (`I-Keep-Having-This-Dream`) | Strategy-roguelike — you lay your own path of tiles out of each dream cycle, taking ATK/DEF/CTRL/HP pickups, levelling up mid-run to pick a perk, and detouring off the direct route for items while the cycle gets deadlier the longer you stay; "snag" enemies grow denser near the exit, and one-off events trade a risk for a temporary boost. Code measured: **59 348 lines** of `.cpp`/`.h` in `app/src/main/jni/src`, dominated by `game_board.cpp` **14 519**, `shop_menu.cpp` 1 836, `gameplay_hud.cpp` 1 752, `game.cpp` 1 474, `save_system.cpp` 1 352, `tile_object.cpp` 1 096, `level_up_panel.cpp` 1 054 — with a real save/restore path rather than a stub. Presentation counted from `assets/` (**146 files**): **14 PNG atlases** (the whole art set is packed sheets, not loose sprites), **113 `.wav` sound effects** and **16 `.mp3` tracks** — i.e. the port kept the original audio library intact. The command that would work is two-stage and cannot fit: `npm run build:mobile` → `./build.py mobile` → `build_mobile.py` → `mobile/gradlew` — and **`node_modules` is absent**, while `mobile/app/src/main/` contains **no `assets/` directory at all**, so a bare `./gradlew assembleDebug` would assemble a shell with the webapp missing; the JS bundle must be generated into the APK first, which needs a full `npm install` plus `lightningcss-cli`. Nothing was compiled, installed or launched and **no frame or APK is claimed**. Counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack (whose `android signals: manifest=no` is **wrong** — the manifest is at `mobile/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`): **61 Java files / 10,877 lines** under `org.exarhteam.iitc_mobile` (`IITC_Mobile.java` the WebView host, `IITC_WebViewClient.java` host-allowlisting so only `intel.ingress.com` gets the IITC user agent, `IITC_TileManager`/`IITC_StorageManager` for cached map tiles, `IITC_PluginManager`, `IITC_DeepLinkHandler`, `IITC_NavigationHelper`, `IITC_NotificationHelper`, `IITC_UserLocation` for the compass), and the interface itself is the userscript core: **46 JS modules in `core/code`, 17 vendored libs in `core/external`** (Leaflet, jQuery 4.0.0, jQuery UI 1.14.2, qrcode) and **70 plugins** — `draw-tools`, `keys`/`keys-on-map`, `bookmarks`, `uniques`, `scoreboard`, `farms-find`, `machina-tracker`, `player-activity-tracker`, `portal-counts`, `missions`, `overlay-kml`, `regions`, nine `basemap-*` providers — for **33,562 JS lines** across `core/` + `plugins/`. That is a serious toolkit, and the tests are real for the web half: **20 mocha spec files** (`portal`, `map_cache`, `map_renderer`, `comm`, `chat`, `search_query`, `smartphone`, `panes`, …) run by `npm test`; the Android half has no test directory at all. Localisation is the strongest axis: **30 locale resource dirs** (es-419, be, ca, cs, da, de, el, es, fi, fr, gl, he, hi, hu, in, it, ja, ko, nb-rNO, nl, pl, pt-BR, pt-PT, ru, sl, sv, th, tr, uk, zh-CN, zh-TW) against **200 English strings** — though coverage decays fast (German, the best, mirrors **153/200**). The structural problem is the one that decides the score: `mIntelUrl = "https://intel.ingress.com/"` is a hard constant in `IITC_Mobile.java`, and every interesting screen is that site rendered with injected JS, so **with no account or no network the app has nothing to show** — the tile cache only re-serves tiles already fetched. Zero game content: no rules, no score, no levels, no opponents, no progression. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (clean modern single-module Gradle project at compileSdk 36 with a committed wrapper and a Python driver that automates the whole two-stage build — docked because the README documents no build command, the build genuinely has two stages, `node_modules` and the generated `assets/` are absent, and nothing was built here); runs on phone **1.5/3** (Android-native since minSdk 21, shipped on F-Droid and Play, 30 locales, a compass and location-aware navigation, tablet layout qualifiers `sw600dp`/`sw720dp-land` — docked hard because it is a **network client for Niantic's servers behind an Ingress login**, so there is nothing to run offline, and nothing was installed or launched in this pass); depth **0.5/3** (genuinely deep *tooling* — link planning, key management, draw tools, uniques, farm finding — but it is analysis layered on top of a game someone else runs; there is no play loop here at all, no objective, no content to consume); polish **1.5/2** (61-file native shell with allowlisted host handling, tile/storage caching, 70 curated plugins, 20 real test specs, 30 locales and an in-app plugin preference screen — docked for **zero Android-side tests**, no build documentation in-repo, German coverage at 153/200 strings, and `VIBRATE` plus location permissions bought for features that mostly exist to overlay a third-party map). **Recommendation: drop it from `GAMES.queue`** — it entered through the F-Droid *Games* category sweep, which resolves whatever the app filed itself under; a well-built companion tool, but it will keep resurfacing as an unjudged "game" until the queue row is removed. Nothing was built, installed or launched. |

Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (documented three-line build, pinned SDK constraint and dependency versions, five committed CI workflows with test-then-build, clean GPLv3 — docked because no Flutter SDK exists on this box so nothing was compiled and the Android SDK floor is toolchain-inherited rather than declared); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android 7.0+, shipped on F-Droid and GitHub Releases, **only `VIBRATE`** so it is offline and privacy-clean by construction, wakelock for the timer, haptics designed for a phone being passed hand to hand, 25 MB — docked because nothing was adb-installed here); depth **2/3** (6 241 words across four languages, 6 categories per language, custom categories and custom forfeits, variable impostor count and debate clock, a real PIN-reveal flow — docked because it is an IRL facilitator with **no AI opponent, no single-player and no progression**: with fewer than three people in the room there is no game at all); polish **1.5/2** (exact 71-key parity across four locales, localized voice packs and haptics, two custom fonts, confetti and a fortune wheel, clean Provider architecture — docked for the undeclared `assets/sounds/de/` directory silently breaking German audio, a stub test suite, and a README rich on install paths but thin on gameplay/contribution detail). |

That is the *only* thing standing in the way: `git submodule status` is **empty** and there is **no `.gitmodules`** — unlike most Android rows in this catalog, nothing is missing from the clone and **all three native dependencies are vendored in-tree** (`third_party/SDL/src` for SDL2 2.30.9 / SDL2_image 2.8.2 / SDL2_mixer 2.8.0, built as shared libs with PNG via stb_image and OGG via stb_vorbis, plus SDL's Java glue from the vendored `SDL2-2.30.9/android-project/` tree), the README states "no other dependencies to fetch", and compileSdk 35, `cmake`, `ninja` and a JDK 17 are all present on the host (only the pinned NDK **28.0.13004108** is absent — 28.2 / 29.0 / 30.0 are installed). Content counted in the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is literal and meaningless because the dungeon is generated: **116 monsters** in `installed_files/data/monsters.xml` (106 with authored `<description>` lore text, e.g. `MON_CRAWLING_INTESTINES`, `MON_TENTACLE_CLUSTER`, `MON_SPECTRAL_WPN`), **238 distinct item ids** in `item_data.cpp` across 10 typed classes (`item_weapon/armor/ammo/device/explosive/head/misc/potion/rod/scroll`, each with its own header), **39 `SPELL_` ids**, and **8 character backgrounds** — with the rules themselves in code rather than data: `spells.cpp` **5 746 lines**, `terrain.cpp` 5 408, `property.cpp` 4 102, `item_data.cpp` 2 529, over **355 C++ source/header files and 105 239 lines** in `src/` (largest `spells.cpp` 168 KB, `terrain.cpp` 140 KB) plus a hand-written engine layer — `bsp.cpp` (dungeon BSP), `ai.cpp`, `actor_player_fov.cpp`, `actor_sneak.cpp`, `attack_ranged.cpp` with cover, `character_descr.cpp`, `game_over_summary.cpp`, and a real save/config path (`io_input.cpp` 69 KB). Presentation counted: **253 tile PNGs in the single `gfx/tiles/20x20` tileset**, **141 `.ogg` audio files** (`amb_001…` ambience plus music and effects), **8 fonts** (DejaVu, Special Elite, Material Symbols, all with their licence files shipped), colour and message data (`messages/menu_quotes.txt`, `terrain_inscription_messages_*.txt`), a **246-line `manual.txt`** and a **1 966-line `release_history.txt`** running from **Aug 25, 2010** to v23.0.0 (May 18, 2025) — fifteen years of continuous design iteration. The port is more than a recompile: `jni/android_bootstrap.cpp` chdirs to internal storage and extracts the whole `installed_files/` tree from the APK on first run, driven by a **build-time SHA-256 `asset_manifest.txt`** generated by a custom Gradle task, so the game's cwd-relative data paths work unmodified and saves go to SDL's pref path; the touch layer is documented gesture-by-gesture (8-direction swipe to move, tap to confirm, hold-then-drag to look, Back = Escape, hamburger = Actions/Video/Audio/Input/Gameplay/Tome of Wisdom/Quit), and the display is separated into its own module for phone screens. Caveats, honestly: nothing was compiled, installed or launched here, so the releases are upstream's claim; **English only** — no locale directories, no gettext/i18n anywhere in `src/`, and no localisation in `installed_files/`; **one tileset at one resolution** (20×20) with no scaling variants; there is **no automated test suite and no CI test step** in this clone (CI only builds and signs releases); and the horror content — insanity mechanics, gore, corpse descriptions — is deliberately unpleasant the way the genre intends. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a genuinely self-contained Android project — all SDL sources vendored, no submodules, pinned AGP/NDK/CMake, committed wrapper with a SHA-256, a documented `./gradlew assembleStaging` that even names its output path, plus a documented F-Droid/CI release process — docked because the build **timed out at 90 s fetching gradle-8.14.3** and the pinned NDK 28.0.13004108 is not installed, so nothing was assembled or verified); runs on phone **2.5/3** (first-party Android port rather than a shim: minSdk 24, three ABIs including x86_64 for emulators, a bespoke gesture vocabulary designed for one-handed phone play, hold-to-look with live tile description so a small screen still reads, cwd-bootstrapped assets that survive reinstall, a documented hamburger menu and Back mapping, and 2340×1080 phone screenshots in the README — docked because no APK ships in the tree and nothing was adb-installed here); depth **2.5/3** (a full traditional roguelike at commercial depth: ~116 monsters with distinct behaviours, 238 items across ten classes with identification and curses, 39 spells, 8 backgrounds that change the opening build, BSP-generated dungeons with themed levels, permadeath, a sanity system that punishes what you look at, and fifteen years of post-1.0 balancing — docked only because it is single-player with no meta-progression between runs and the pack cannot show the level generator running); polish **1.5/2** (141 audio files including layered ambience, a coherent 253-tile art set, eight licensed fonts, an in-game Tome of Wisdom plus a 246-line manual, a 15-year changelog and a careful asset-extraction bootstrap — docked for a single 20×20 tileset, zero localisation, no test suite, and a README that documents the release process better than the gameplay). **Recommendation: keep — good deep roguelike, and the cheapest Android build in the backlog to unblock: cache `gradle-8.14.3-all.zip` (or `sdkmanager` NDK 28.0.13004108), then `assembleStaging` should go straight through since every source it needs is already vendored.** |

Lynn Smith, 2002) | *_5.0 — desk audit_* | **Android-native Java, single Gradle module** (`eu.veldsoft.ithaka.board.game`, **versionCode 7 / versionName "1.5.1"**, **minSdk 25** / compileSdk **33** / targetSdk **34**, **AGP 8.13.2** with a committed wrapper at **gradle-9.4.1-bin**; deps are `org.encog:encog-core:3.3.0` for the neural bot, `androidx.appcompat:1.1.0`, and test deps junit 4.12 / espresso 3.2.0 that resolve to nothing because **there is no `test/` or `androidTest/` source set at all**); **GPL-v3+** per the README badge and a 35 KB `LICENSE`; live on **F-Droid (`eu.veldsoft.ithaka.board.game`) and Google Play** per README badges, **no APK in the tree**, no iOS/desktop/web target. Re-run against the cached Temurin 17 (`~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-amd64-linux.2`) got **13 s further** and died on the real blocker: **`Failed to find target with hash string 'android-33' in: /home/cluer/Android/Sdk`** — this box carries 35, 36 and 37 only, and `compileSdkVersion 33` is pinned in `app/build.gradle`. The tree is otherwise conventional and cheap (no NDK, no submodules, 19 Java files), and the single missing piece is the **android-33 platform package**; nothing was compiled, adb-installed or rendered and **no frame is claimed**. Counted in the tree rather than trusting the pack (whose `android signals: manifest=no` is **wrong** — the manifest is `app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`; its `code=19` is right but `images=13`/`audio=4` hide what the art actually is): **19 Java files / 2 513 LOC**, of which the rules engine is `model/Board.java` **921 lines** (`ROWS = 4`, `COLS = 4`, `NUMBER_OF_PLAYERS = 2`, `WIN_LINE_LENGTH = 3`, `implements Serializable` so a board can be shipped over the Bluetooth socket) and the rest is thin — `GameActivity` 709, `SplashActivity` 102, `MenuActivity` 79, `AboutActivity`/`HelpActivity` 21 each, and the AI package at **341 lines total**. The bot situation is the honest caveat that sets the depth score: **four AI classes ship but only the weakest is wired** — `GameActivity:611` builds `bots = { null, new RandomArtificialIntelligence() }`, so single-player is a **uniformly random legal-move picker**; `MonteCarloArtificialIntelligence` (132 lines, MCTS with a millisecond budget) and `BruteForceArtificialIntelligence` (98 lines) are **dead code reached only from the stray `TreeBuilder.java`** loose at `app/src/main/java/TreeBuilder.java` (a `main()` that constructs a Board and calls the brute-force AI — unused by the app); `NeuralNetworkArtificialIntelligence` (55 lines) builds a real Encog **16→17→16 TANH** net in its constructor and then **never evaluates it** — its `move()` is a `do { random corners } while (!board.isValid(move))` loop, i.e. the AI is decorative. Modes do exist, four of them in `Mode.java`: `SINGLE_PLAYER, TWO_PLAYERS, CLIENT_MULTIPLAYER, SERVER_MULTIPLAYER`, and the Bluetooth host/join pair is **actually implemented** (socket handshake in `GameActivity`, serialized `Board`/`Move`/`Piece` model classes) — offline device-to-device, no server, which is what the `INTERNET` permission is *not* for. Presentation counted: **9 PNGs total**, five of which are the entire game — `blue.png`, `fuchsia.png`, `green.png`, `orange.png`, `white.png` (flat coloured discs, **hdpi only**; every other density holds just a launcher icon), laid out as **16 `ImageView`s at absolute dp offsets inside one `RelativeLayout`** (`piece00` … `piece15`, 60 dp squares at 10/70/130/190 dp) against a black background — no `onDraw`, no custom `View`, no scaling for other screens, no landscape variant. Audio: **4 `.wav` in `res/raw`** of which `SoundPool` loads **3** (`schademans_pipe9` click, `game_sound_correct` finish, `cancel` wrong) and `synthpad_2.wav` is unreferenced. UI is five activities (`Splash` with a 5 s meta-data timeout then a `noHistory` redirect to `Menu`, `Menu`, `Game`, `Help`, `About`) and **5 layouts + 1 option menu**. Localisation is real but tiny: **1 additional locale**, `values-ru-rRU`, at **22 strings vs 28 English** (Russian is missing the help text, and `hello_world` sits unused in both). Zero tests, and CI is a dead **`.travis.yml`**; the clone has **1 commit** (`5883e34`, "Build script has been updated.", 2026-05-08), i.e. a snapshot, not history. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (a small, conventional, dependency-light single-module project with a committed wrapper and no native code — docked because **nothing assembled here**: the README documents no command, the wrapper dies on this host's JDK 27, the JDK-17 retry dies on the missing **android-33** platform, and the declared test deps point at source sets that do not exist); runs on phone **2.0/3** (Android-exclusive, minSdk 25, shipped on F-Droid and Play, 60 dp touch targets on a 4×4 board, three sound cues, and a genuinely offline Bluetooth head-to-head so two phones in one room is enough — docked because the board is **absolute-positioned at one density with no landscape or small-screen layout**, so it will letterbox rather than scale, and nothing was adb-installed); depth **1.0/3** (a clean, genuinely elegant shared-piece connection rule with real trap and zugzwang play between two humans, plus pass-and-play and Bluetooth — docked hard because the shipped single-player opponent is **random-legal-move** with the MCTS, brute-force and neural nets unwired, and there is **one board, one rule set, no difficulty, no undo, no hint, no score, no levels, no stats and no persistence**); polish **1.0/2** (splash-to-menu flow, help and about screens, a Russian translation and three sound effects are more than this clone had to have — docked for **five flat colour discs as the entire art set**, a hand-positioned 16-ImageView board, an unreferenced `synthpad_2.wav`, a `hello_world` template string left in resources, a stray `TreeBuilder.java` in the app source set, a dead Travis config, zero tests, and a permission list carrying `INTERNET`, `READ_PHONE_STATE` and storage the code never uses). **Recommendation: keep as a curator/tutorial-grade title, not a flagship — it is a teaching project ("implemented … as a programming tutorial" per its own About text); the cheapest upgrade is wiring the existing MCTS bot behind a difficulty menu.** |

| 117 | **Kumquats** (`Kumquats`) | Word puzzle — solitaire crossword-builder against a clock: a 144-tile bag is dealt as a random stream and you drag each tile onto a grid; the clock runs from the first tile until **all** tiles sit on the board as **one connected group** (diagonals do not count) with **every horizontal and vertical run of 2+ tiles a dictionary word**; three game lengths (Short 36 / Medium 54 / Long 72 tiles) each with its own **top-5 best-time** ladder | *_6.5 — desk audit_* | **Flutter/Dart, all six targets** (`kumquats`, **version 1.2.0+4**, Dart SDK `">=3.0.0"`, assets declared as whole dirs `assets/doc/` and `assets/words/`); five ordinary deps — `shared_preferences ^2.0.4`, `flutter_markdown ^0.6.0`, `url_launcher ^6.0.2`, `intl ^0.17.0`, `cupertino_icons` — with `pubspec.lock` **committed**; Android `com.dozingcatsoftware.kumquats` with **`minSdk = flutter.minSdkVersion`, versionCode/Name read from `local.properties`** rather than pinned in-repo, plus a real **`flavorDimensions "deploy"`** pair (`std` for normal builds, `fdroid` only for the F-Droid release); **GPL-3.0** (`LICENSE` is the full GNU GPL v3, 29 June 2007), © 2022-2025 Brian Nenninger per `appLegalese`; `android ios linux macos web windows` dirs all present; live on **F-Droid as `com.dozingcatsoftware.kumquats` and Google Play** per README badges; **no APK in the tree**, **no CI workflow** | **Desk audit — no build run: there is no Flutter/Dart toolchain on this host.** `command -v flutter` and `command -v dart` both come back empty, so the 90 s window was never entered; nothing was compiled, adb-installed or launched, and **no frame and no APK are claimed**. The README is **30 lines** (one-line description, platform sentence, the flavor note, two store badges, credits) and gives **no build command** — only "you'll need to specify the `std` build flavor", which implies `flutter build apk --flavor std` without documenting it; the comment in `android/app/build.gradle` explains the flavor exists at all because "Had to add `std` flavor in order to build with default signing config". Content was counted from the tree instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`, which is literal and meaningless because the board is **generated and grows**: **179 647 dictionary lines** in `assets/words/` — **TWL06.txt 178 475** words (the full tournament Scrabble lexicon), **3letter.txt 1 065**, **2letter.txt 107** — all loaded into a `Set` at startup and *timed* (`Read ${dictionary.length} words in ${ms} ms`), and a **144-tile bag** drawn from a frequency table that sums correctly (**E 18, A 13, I 12, O 11, T/R 9, N 8, D/S/U 6, L 5, G 4, B/C/F/H/M/P/V/W/Y 3, J/K/Q/X/Z 2**) — the standard English distribution, not a uniform draw. The rules engine is real rather than a string check: `LetterGrid.findWords()` scans both axes for maximal runs; `isLegalWord()` accepts a word if it is in the dictionary **or, with the Q option on, if substituting QU for either of its first two Q's (or both) yields a dictionary word**, so `QAT`/`QADI` are legal and the tile face is drawn as **"Q/QU"**; `coordinatesWithInvalidWords()` returns the exact cells implicated by any illegal run, which is what the highlighting paints. `connectedLetterGroups()` flood-fills to prove the finished board is **one group**, and `expandIfNeededForPadding(minPadding: 2)` / `extendEdge()` give the grid its **auto-expanding** behaviour; zoom and pan are hand-rolled `ScaleGestureRecognizer` handling (`updateZoomScale`, `handlePanStart/Update/End`) rather than a scroll view, so a 72-tile board stays legible on a phone. Code measured: **2 Dart files / 1 508 lines** — `lib/main.dart` **1 232** (all UI, drag-and-drop, preferences, dialogs) and `lib/grid.dart` **276** (the whole rules engine: `Coord`, `GridWord`, `Direction`, `LetterGrid`, `GridRules`, `QTileHandling{qOnly, qOrQu}`) — genuinely tight for what it does. Settings are few and each one matters: **tiles per game 36/54/72** dropdown, **"Q can be used as QU"** toggle, and an **illegal-word highlight** radio with three modes (`never`, `all_tiles_played`, `always`), all persisted through `SharedPreferences` alongside a **top-5 best-time JSON table keyed per tile count** (`best_times.<n>`, `numBestTimesToStore = 5`). Presentation counted: **46 PNGs and it is honest about them** — the only in-game art is coloured letter tiles drawn in code (`letterTile()`, palette at `tileBackgroundColors`, text `0xFF303030`, border `0xFFC8A060`, teal-900 rack, green-300 status bar, `boardGridLineColor black38`), while the 46 images are **launcher/app icons across five Android densities + adaptive icon + five web icons + iOS/macOS AppIcon sets** plus exactly **3 documentation screenshots** in `assets/doc/` (`groups1.png`, `groups2.png`, `obscure.png`) that the in-app **markdown About screen** embeds via `resource:assets/doc/about.md` — a real illustrated rules page with worked examples, not a text dump. Motion is modest but present: **300 ms new-tile slide-in** (`newTileSlideInAnimationMillis`), animated rack and grid tile widgets, and a drag tile that follows the finger and snaps. **Zero audio files anywhere** and no haptics — a real omission for a timed drag game. Tests target the rules, not the widget tree: **10 `test()` cases in `test/grid_test.dart` (174 lines)** covering set/find words in both directions, `numberOfFilledCells`, group detection, invalid-word cells and the Q/QU substitution — no widget or integration tests, and no CI to run the 10. Store kit is thin: **fastlane metadata in `en-US` only** (title, short + full description, icon) with **4 changelogs** (`1`–`4`, the last reading "Updated UI / Improved edge-to-edge support on Android 15+"), matching `versionCode 4`. One dead feature worth flagging: **a Japanese mode exists but is switched off** — `hiriganaFrequencies` (2 each of あ–ん, 46 kana) and a complete `_startJapanese()` are in the tree, but every `makeButtonRow('新しいゲーム', _startJapanese)` call is **commented out** (lines 960 and 1030) and there is **no kana dictionary asset** to validate against, so it is half-built scaffolding, not a mode. Last commit `705bb29`, **2025-03-07**, "Build with Java 17". Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (a small, dependency-light, standard Flutter project with a committed lockfile, six platform targets and a documented *constraint* — the mandatory `std` flavor — plus a 2025 commit that moved the Android toolchain to Java 17 — docked because **no Flutter SDK exists on this box** so nothing was compiled, the README states no actual build command, and there is no CI to fall back on); runs on phone **2.5/3** (touch-first by construction: drag-to-place, pinch-zoom and two-finger pan on a board that outgrows the screen, auto-expanding grid, edge-to-edge on Android 15+, shipped on both F-Droid and Play from one codebase, 4.5 MB clone — docked because nothing was adb-installed here, `minSdk` is toolchain-inherited rather than declared, and there is **no audio or haptic feedback** in a game played against a stopwatch); depth **1.5/3** (a 178 k-word dictionary with a correct Q/QU rule, three lengths with separate best-time ladders, and a connectivity-plus-valid-word win condition genuinely harder than a simple anagram, on a rules engine clean enough to unit-test — docked because it is **solitaire with no opponent, no levels, no difficulty ladder, no daily seed, no score beyond elapsed time and no progression at all**: replay value is "beat your own clock" and nothing else, and the kana mode is unreachable); polish **1.5/2** (custom drag/zoom/pan interaction, 300 ms tile animations, a palette that reads well, an illustrated markdown About page, three useful settings, persisted per-length best times, a committed lockfile and **10 rules unit tests** — docked for **zero audio**, a single locale with four changelogs of store metadata, no CI, no widget tests, no Fastlane screenshots, and a half-wired Japanese mode left commented out and dictionary-less). |

Progression is a real curve, not a counter: `xpForLevel(l)` at `game_provider.dart:24` is `pow(l-1,2)*100` up to level 50 (**240 100** XP at 50) then **`+7000·n + 40·n²`** with an explicit comment about softening the wall past 100; `calculateLevelFromXp` searches to **level 999**; and `levelBonusFactor` returns **`1 + level/60` clamped to 3.0**, so levelling pays more per quest. Difficulty is four tiers with distinct payouts (`easy 50 / medium 150 / hard 400 / legendary 1000` XP, `game_models.dart:18`), quests recur on **four frequencies** (`once, daily, weekly, monthly`), and the state model is substantial — **26 classes/enums in `models/game_models.dart`** (`SkillCategory, GameBadge, UserProfile, Skill, Quest, Bet, Reward, Guild, GuildMember, GuildInvitation, GuildQuest, GuildLog, ChatMessage, ShopItem, LootBox, LeaderboardEntry` …) — plus **10 badges** with real criteria (`xp_1000`, `quest_10`, `streak_3`, `level_10`, `focus_5`, `focus_10`, …), **5 skill categories** (Physique, Mental, Discipline, Social, Créativité) levelling separately, **15 shop items** and **4 loot boxes**, streak freezes with claimable milestones, a **bet** system whose payout derives from the linked quests' `difficulty.xpBase`, a Focus timer (`Timer.periodic` per second → `addFocusXp(minutes)`), an evening journal, a weekly summary and an XP-bar/celebration overlay in `lib/ui/widgets/`. Avatar depth is the most concrete art claim: **44 layered PNGs in `assets/characters/`** across **8 slots** (skin 5, hair 8, eyes 5, brows 3, mouth 5, outfit 7, hat 6, accessory 5), declared by `tool/character_catalog.json` (`canvas: 32`, 8 categories, 44 parts) and regenerated by `tool/generate_characters.py` — **630 000 distinct combinations**, of which 15 cosmetics are earned through the shop. Localisation is hand-rolled and genuinely broad: **13 languages in `lib/core/translations.dart`** (`fr 445`, `en 445`, `es 432`, and `ar, de, hi, id, ja, pt, ru, tr, vi, zh` at **399 keys each**), picked from `PlatformDispatcher.instance.locale` with a per-key English fallback — not ARB, but complete enough to play in eleven languages besides the two source ones. Persistence gets real attention: `Hive` boxes with `flush()`, a **50-save compaction cycle**, `OfflineManager` wrapping connectivity checks, a **data-migration** path, and backup/import tests; the account layer is optional and fails soft — `syncWithServer()` runs at launch and on any failure **sets `guildOffline` and continues from local Hive** instead of blocking play, with E2E guild-chat keys kept in `flutter_secure_storage` (the source comments explicitly refuse to put them in Hive). Testing is above this catalog's median: **8 test files / 1 002 lines / 52 cases** (`character_skins`, `data_migration`, `guild_switching`, `import_and_guild_chat`, `import_guild_chat`, `sauvegarde_robustness`, `server_service`, `translations`). | 123 | **Privacy Friendly Ludo** (`Ludo-Privacy-Friendly`) | Board game — classic Ludo/Pachisi for **1–6 players on one device**: roll, move a figure out of the house on a 6, run the ring and bear off into the home column; capture opponents by landing on them, and continue the turn on a 6 or a kick-out. Two board shapes (**`Four_players`** and **`Six_players`**, the latter also selecting 5 players), any seat can be flipped between human and **AI**, and a "roll your own dice" mode turns the phone into a board that accepts physical dice. Winners are ranked, not just first-past-the-post | *_7.0 — desk audit_* | **Android only** — single `:app` module plus an in-tree `:colorpicker` library and the `:backup-api` **git submodule**, `org.secuso.privacyfriendlyludo`, **versionCode 100 / versionName 2.3.3**, **minSdk 17 / compile+targetSdk 34**, **AGP 8.3.2**, Kotlin **2.0.21**, Java/Kotlin target **17** with a `jvmToolchain(17)`, **Gradle wrapper 8.5-bin**, **GPL-3.0** (README states GPLv3, © 2017–2018 Julia Schneider) with icons split CC BY 2.5 / Apache-2.0 Material; **zero `uses-permission` entries of any kind** — no INTERNET, no storage, no ads, no analytics — and `allowBackup=true` plus an exported `PFABackupService` on a `:backup` process so the sibling **Privacy Friendly Backup** app can export the game state; shipped on **F-Droid as `org.secuso.privacyfriendlyludo`**; no iOS, desktop or web target and **no APK in the tree** | **Desk audit — no build run, and the conventional command died in 2 s.** The README (**74 lines**) documents **no build command at all** — it says only "Further development requires Android Studio, we recommend to use at least version 2.2.2" plus min/target SDK, a backup note and the Weblate link — so the standard recipe was tried: `sh ./gradlew --offline -q :app:assembleDebug` → **BUILD FAILED in 2s** on `No cached version of com.android.tools.build:gradle:8.3.2` **and** `org.jetbrains.kotlin:kotlin-gradle-plugin:2.0.21` (this host's AGP cache jumps 8.2.2 → 8.6.1). Two further blockers sit behind that: the root `build.gradle` still lists **`jcenter()`** first in *both* `buildscript` and `allprojects` repositories, and **`libs/privacy-friendly-backup-api/` is an empty submodule directory** (only `.gitmodules`, no `BackupAPI/` child) while `settings.gradle` does `include ':backup-api'` at exactly that path — so even with network the project set fails to resolve before compiling. Nothing was compiled, adb-installed or launched; **no frame and no APK are claimed**. The pack's `maps/levels=0` is literal and correct — Ludo's board is **geometry in code**, not level data: `Map/StartGameFieldPosition.java` (148 lines) and `Map/GameFieldPosition.java` (277) encode the ring per player count, and `logic/BoardModel.java` **815 lines** is the whole rules engine (house entry, kick-out, `getNewPosition`, bear-off past `getLast_field_index()`, `order_of_winners`/`rank`). Code measured: **34 Java+Kotlin files** — **5,421 lines in `app/src/main`** (only **190 of them Kotlin**, four small files: `PFLudo.kt` 40, `BackupRestorer.kt` 92, `BackupCreator.kt` 54, `PFABackupService.kt` 4 — the game itself is old-school Java), plus **620** in `colorpicker`; the biggest units are `GameActivity.java` **849** (turn loop, dice, AI scheduling), `BoardModel.java` **815**, `GameSettingActivity.java` **543** (per-seat colour/name/AI setup), `MainActivity.java` 407, `BoardView.java` 341 (custom `onDraw` board), `TutorialActivity.java` 226. Rules are genuinely implemented rather than approximated: `Dicer.java` rolls with **`SecureRandom`** (a real CSPRNG instead of `Math.random`), a 6 grants another turn, three consecutive non-6 rolls forfeit the turn (toggleable as `switch_dice_3times`, default on), and the AI is a small but deliberate heuristic — it scans movable figures, **prefers a capture** if one is available, else **prefers bearing a figure off into the house**, and only otherwise picks at random — so it plays better than pure chance with no lookahead and no difficulty ladder. Settings that actually ship: **keep-screen-on** (default on) and the **roll-three-times** rule toggle (`res/xml/pref_general.xml`), plus per-game seat setup, a tutorial and a help screen. Presentation counted rather than trusted: **56 PNGs** across five densities, i.e. **31 distinct assets** — six dice faces **`d1`–`d6` plus a parallel `d1_withcolor`–`d6_withcolor` set** for the colour-tinted dice, four figure sprites (`figure.png`–`figure4.png`), `field.png` / `field_color_border.png` / `border.png`, tutorial art (`gamefield_tutorial.png`), trophy/help icons, splash icon and the SECUSO/PFA logos; **31 layouts + 2 landscape + an xlarge layout** for tablets. **There is no audio and no haptics whatsoever** — grep finds zero `MediaPlayer`, `SoundPool` or `Vibrator` references — which is a real miss for a dice-and-capture game. Localisation is thin: exactly **two locales**, base English (**111 strings**) and **`values-de` (106)** — the Weblate badge in the README advertises translations that have not landed in-tree; Fastlane metadata exists for **`en-US` and `de-DE` only** (title, short + full description, 4 phone screenshots). Tests are the untouched Android template — `ExampleUnitTest` + `androidTest/ApplicationTest` — and there is no CI (`.github/` is dependabot only). Two oddities worth flagging: the APK output name is still hard-coded **`pfa-pausing-healthily`** (`app/build.gradle`), a copy-paste from a sibling PFA app, and `lintOptions` is declared **twice** in the same `android {}` block with contradictory `checkReleaseBuilds true` then `false`; `minifyEnabled false` ships unshrunk. Last commit `bee9614`, **2025-07-14**, "Merge pull request #31 from SecUSo/development". Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (conventional single-activity Android project with a committed wrapper, an in-tree library module and a cached Gradle 8.5 distribution — docked because the README documents no build command, the offline attempt failed in 2 s on uncached AGP 8.3.2 and Kotlin 2.0.21, the repositories still point at the defunct **jcenter**, the required backup-api **submodule is empty**, and nothing was compiled here); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native with a genuine minSdk 17, a pass-and-play loop that needs nothing but taps on one handset, tablet layouts, a keep-screen-on switch, **zero permissions** in the manifest and a working F-Droid package — docked because nothing was adb-installed and there is no audio, haptic or animation feedback on dice or captures); depth **2.0/3** (a faithful, complete Ludo with real capture/bear-off/house rules, two board shapes for 1–6 players, human/AI seat mixing and a capture-aware bot — more genuine game than most board rows here — docked because Ludo is luck-dominated by design, the bot has no lookahead or difficulty setting, and there is no online play, campaign, scoring meta or stats beyond the win ranking); polish **1.5/2** (a hand-drawn `BoardView` board, a full dice-face art set with colour-tinted variants, tablet and landscape layouts, a tutorial, keep-screen-on, and Privacy Friendly Backup export/restore integration — docked for **zero audio or haptics**, only **two locales**, template-only tests, no CI, an unshrunk release, a mis-named APK output and a duplicated `lintOptions` block). |

Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built, installed, or seen running in the scene** — the 100 % bar is not a playable frame; this clone is a **generated single-snapshot** (`20a735a`, "10.3.14", 2026-10-11) whose real source lives upstream in `moroway-app-dev`; there is **no player-facing progression of any kind** — no score, no timetable, no objectives, no campaign — so replay value is entirely self-set; the touch UI was never exercised; and the F-Droid listing covers only 4 languages for a German-first app. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (Cordova config, adaptive icons and version metadata all present and consistent, and the web app demonstrably boots from a plain static server — docked because **no self-contained build exists in this checkout**: empty `www/`, no manifest, no `node_modules`, and a `build.sh` that needs the external dev-tools output, so nothing was assembled in 90 s); runs on phone **2/3** (Android-only by design, shipped on F-Droid as `de.moroway.oc` at 10.3.14, `viewport-fit=cover` + `user-scalable=no` metas and edge-to-edge/singleTask config all tuned for a handset, and the whole game is local with no network needed — docked because no APK was installed and the headless boot never reached a scene, so the phone experience is unverified); depth **1/3** (a genuine 3D sandbox with switch and multi-vehicle control, an 8.5 k-line scripting engine and a demo mode — but **no rules, no goals, no opponent, no progression**, which is what separates a model railway from a game); polish **1.5/2** (54 hand-picked 3D models, nine audio tracks, ~116 images, adaptive icons at every density, edge-to-edge Android 15 config, a 4-locale F-Droid listing, in-app settings/help/changelog — docked for a German-first surface, a build only the upstream tooling can reproduce, and the fact that the 3D scene has never been seen running here). **Recommendation: keep as a sandbox curiosity, not a game** — it entered via the F-Droid Games sweep and it holds the only 3D asset pool worth anything in this roster (54 `.glb`, 3.9 MB), so it is worth retaining for the modelling/rendering lane even though it scores below the board-game tier; Two things to watch on a later real build: **AGP 9.4.1** and **Kotlin 2.4.21** are both very new plugin lines and neither was resolved against this host's caches, and the `nightly` build type cannot sign without those three env vars. Content counted from the resources instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal and correct — a party game has no level data): **108 strings per locale with exact parity across all four** — `values` (English base), `values-fa`, `values-es`, `values-tr`, i.e. 432 aligned keys — so the translation set is complete rather than aspirational; the **33 roles** are enumerated as `R.string` pairs in `values/strings.xml` (16/12/5 split) each with a one-line `*_desc` blurb, and `Role` itself is a two-field `@StringRes` record (`name: Int`, `side: RoleSide`) with only three constructors in `utils/Resource.kt`; the roster is assembled dynamically from **checked Material chips** in `getSelectedRoles()` (`RoleFragment.kt`), mapped back to string ids through `roleNameStringRes(context, chip.text)`. Code measured: **32 Kotlin files / 2,131 lines total**, of which **1,787 are in `app/src/main`** — `MainViewModel` holds the state machine, `view/` splits into six fragments (`MainFragment`, `RoleFragment`, `PlayerRoleFragment`, `NarratorFragment`, `SettingsFragment`, `AboutFragment`), three adapters, three dialogs, a custom `CounterView` + listener + extensions pair, and `SharedPreferences.kt` for the persisted language choice. **The tests are real**: `MainViewModelTest.kt` is **306 lines** using MockK + `kotlinx-coroutines-test` and covers **add/update/remove player, select-all, the 3-player minimum, role refresh, and even/odd `calculateMaxMafia`**, alongside the usual `ExampleUnitTest`/`ExampleInstrumentedTest` templates. Rules are enforced rather than assumed: `MafiaError` carries `PlayerNotEnough`, `SelectedRolesNotMatch` and `MafiaRolesTooMuch`, surfaced as toasts ("At least 3 players must be selected", "The number of selected roles does not match the number of players"), and `calculateMaxMafia` derives the mafia cap from player count. Presentation counted: **21 hand-written vector drawables** (a full Material icon set including bespoke `baseline_person_mafia/citizen/independent` glyphs, alive/dead power icons, plus/minus, GitHub) and a **three-way colour-state system** as `res/color` selectors (`mafiachip_*`, `citizenchip_*`, `independentchip_*` × background/stroke/text) tinting selected role chips by side; **11 layouts**, **10 anim resources plus an `anim-ldrtl` mirror set** so Persian gets correct slide directions, and `values-night` / `-v21` / `-v23` theme variants. Caveats, honestly: **there is no audio and no haptics** — grep for `MediaPlayer`, `SoundPool`, `Vibrator` and `ToneGenerator` across `app/src/main/kotlin` returns **nothing**, a genuine miss for a game whose whole point is a narrator reading phases aloud around a table; **the app does not run the game** — no night-resolution engine, no vote tally, no win-condition check — so the narrator still adjudicates by hand and the phone is a role dealer plus counter; there is **no AI, no single-player and no online path**, so it needs 3+ humans in one room; the release is **0.4.0-alpha** at `versionCode 40`. Store kit is minimal but valid: **10 fastlane files in `en-US` only** — title, short description, full description, icon and **4 phone screenshots** (the exact four the README table shows) — plus `en-US/changelogs/{20,30}.txt`. Last commit `fddd964`, **2026-10-30**, "docs: add Code_Of_Conduct.md". Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (clean modern single-module Kotlin layout, version catalog, committed wrapper jar, pinned AGP/Kotlin/SDK, View Binding plus detekt/ktlint, and `android-37.0` with JDK 17 already on this host — docked because the **README documents no build command**, the pinned **Gradle 9.8.2 distribution download alone overran the 90 s window**, AGP 9.4.1 / Kotlin 2.4.21 were never resolved against the caches, and nothing was assembled here); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-exclusive and handset-shaped by construction: minSdk 24, **zero permissions** so it is fully offline and privacy-clean, `supportsRtl` with LTR/RTL animation mirrors for Persian, pass-the-phone and narrator screens designed for one device on a table, plus an F-Droid listing — docked because nothing was adb-installed here, `targetSdk 37` is untested bleeding edge, and there is no audio or haptic cue that a phase has changed); depth **2/3** (33 genuinely differentiated roles with per-role night-action text across three factions is a lot of social-deduction variety for a phone-sized helper, role-division and mafia-cap validation stop broken setups, and the narrator screen's alive/dead plus per-side tallies are exactly the bookkeeping a table forgets — docked because the app is a **dealer, not an engine**: no night resolution, no vote tally, no win check, no scoring or stats, so replay value lives entirely in the humans at the table);
