Installed with `adb install -r` ("Success") and launched `com.willie.mancala/.MainActivity` on `emulator-5554` (android-36 x86_64, headless, swiftshader): **PID 2664 alive**, zero `AndroidRuntime:E` lines. Played it through the soft keyboard — typed `6` pits, `4` seeds, then pit numbers — and the transcript is a live game: a `4 4 4 4 4 4` opening board, Player 1's pit 1 emptied into `0 5 5 5 5 4`, both stores printed as `0`, clean hand-off to "Player 2, choose a pit (1-6)", then Player 2's row becoming `5 5 5 5 0 4`. Evidence: `evidence/mancala-setup.png` (229,228 B, setup + first board), `evidence/mancala-play.png` (155,181 B, two moves played, turn switching, both "Invalid input" paths visible). **The README documents no build command at all** (44 lines: description, F-Droid/GitHub badges, 3 screenshots, "Minimum Android version: API 21", a 10-point rule list, a 2-line changelog — v1.1 initial, v1.2 "update to API 36" — credits to AndroidIDE and **ChatGPT**, and PR boilerplate); the only recipe in the repo is F-Droid's `com.willie.mancala.yml` (`RepoType: git`, two pinned builds — **1.1/vc11 @ `4c2b69ae`** and **1.2/vc12 @ `c37e905a`** — each `subdir: app` with `gradle: yes`, an `AllowedAPKSigningKeys` hash and a Bitcoin donation address). Content counted from the tree instead of the pack's meaningless `maps/levels=0`: the entire game is **one file — `MainActivity.java`, 314 lines**, no Kotlin, **zero test sources, no CI workflow**; presentation is **21 PNGs that are all launcher icons** (five densities × adaptive back/fore + round + legacy, mdpi 1,805 B → xxxhdpi 17,119 B), **3 fastlane JPG screenshots**, **16 XML resources** and **zero audio files**; `values/strings.xml` holds **exactly one string — `app_name`** — so the **65 `appendToOutput(...)` calls carry hardcoded English literals** and the app is single-locale by construction (the only `values-*` directory is `values-night`, which defines an **unused** `Theme.Material3.DayNight` style while `AndroidManifest.xml` still points at the **AppCompat template theme** `Theme.AppCompat.DayNight.DarkActionBar`; `values/colors.xml` and `values-night/colors.xml` are both empty `<resources/>`). The rules engine is genuinely implemented rather than stubbed: `makeMove()` sows counter-clockwise around `totalPits`, **skips the opponent's store** (`if (pos == (player == 0 ? totalPits - 1 : numberOfPits)) continue`), returns "extra turn" when the last seed lands in your own store, and captures when it lands in an empty own pit with a non-empty opposite (`opposite = totalPits - 2 - pos`, seeds + the landing seed into your store, opposite zeroed); `checkGameOver()` sums both sides, **sweeps the leftovers into the stores** and `endGame()` declares a win or a tie by comparing the two store values; the board is configurable up to a **`MAX_SEEDS = 999`** total with per-state validation messages ("Invalid input. Please enter a positive integer between 1 and N."). Caveats, honestly: **there is no AI and no single-player path** — `player` simply flips `(player + 1) % 2` on every non-extra turn, so it needs two humans passing one phone; **choosing an empty pit silently grants another turn** because `makeMove()` returns `1` ("type move again") for an empty pit and the caller reads `1` as "same player continues"; the empty-submit guard `if (input == "")` **compares String references**, so a blank Done press never hits it and instead falls through to `parseInt` — reproduced on the emulator as two "Invalid input. Please enter a pit number…" lines from empty submits; `sign.sh` hardcodes `/home/willie/willie-release-key.jks` and needs `$ALIAS`/`$PASSWD`, so releases can only be signed by the author; **there is no persistence** (the board is a `List<Integer>` field with no `onSaveInstanceState`, so a rotation loses the game), no undo, no move history, no statistics, no animation, and no haptics. Clone is **4.0 MB, single commit `e7ad5d5`, 2026-05-31 "Update sign.sh"**. Breakdown: buildability **2/2** (verified here — committed wrapper jar, pinned AGP/SDK, `assembleDebug` in 27 s producing a 5.7 MiB debug APK, and an F-Droid recipe that pins both released commits), runs on phone **2.5/3** (adb-installed and played on the emulator with live moves and turn switching, minSdk 21 down to Lollipop, phone-only packaging with adaptive icons at every density — docked because the input surface is a **typed command line through the IME** with a monospace number dump instead of a touch board, which is clumsy on a handset), depth **1/3** (correct sow/capture/extra-turn/sweep rules and a configurable board are a real game — docked because it is **hotseat-only with no AI, no difficulty, no variants, no progression, no stats and no saves**, so replay value is entirely "find another person"), polish **1/2** (a tight 314-line single-file implementation, clean validation messages, an adaptive icon set, day/night template theming and valid F-Droid metadata — docked for **65 hardcoded strings in one language, zero audio, zero art beyond the launcher, zero tests, a dead blank-input check, an empty-pit extra turn, an unused theme resource, and no state saved across rotation**). **Recommendation: keep as a rules-correct reference implementation of Mancala, not as a phone game to show off** — it is the cheapest real build in this roster (27 s, 5.7 MiB) and a clean base for a touch board, but as shipped it is a CLI in an Android shell with no opponent. |
| 129 | **Matrix Puzzle** (`Matrix-Puzzle`) | Logic-grid deduction puzzle (the "Einstein / zebra" genre) — a procedurally generated matrix of **3–10 floors × 3 categories** where you assign one item per cell from the clue list; 10 themed category triples (Name+Profession+Nationality, Name+Pet+Hobby, Name+Drink+Color, …) drawn from a 9-category pool, with a **20-item name list and 10 items in each of the other eight** (`_categoryPool` in `matrix_puzzle_engine.dart`). Nine constraint kinds — `fixedFloor`, `sameFloor`, `negativeSameFloor`, `immediateAbove`, `somewhereAbove`, `adjacentFloor`, `distanceAbove`, `betweenFloors`, `parityFloor` — unlocked by difficulty band, and every generated puzzle is **verified unique** by a bitmask constraint-propagation solver before it is served. Four modes: a 20-level-per-page **level ladder**, a **date-seeded daily challenge**, free **practice** (configurable floor count, default 5), plus hint / undo / cross-off-clue / free-text notes as play aids | *_6.5 — desk audit_* (buildability 1 · phone target 2 · depth/replay 2 · polish 1.5) | **Flutter (Dart) — Android + web from one codebase**, package **`matrixpuzzle` v1.0.3+4**, Dart SDK **^3.12.2**, Flutter stable (`6a19cca5` per `.metadata`); **Riverpod 2.6.1** (+`riverpod_annotation`, `riverpod_generator`) for state, **Hive 2.2.3 / hive_flutter** with committed adapters for persistence, `url_launcher 6.3.2`, `flutter_localizations` + `intl`, `flutter_lints 6` and `build_runner`; Android `namespace`/`applicationId` **`com.sidhant.matrixpuzzle`**, Java/JVM **17**, release signing read from `android/key.properties`, `dependenciesInfo` disabled in both APK and bundle, **adaptive launcher icons** (`mipmap-anydpi-v26` + background/foreground/**monochrome** at five densities); `AndroidManifest.xml` declares **zero `uses-permission` entries** — no INTERNET — so the game is offline by construction, with `<queries>` for `https`/`http` VIEW (the Ko-fi and F-Droid links) and a single exported `singleTop` `MainActivity`; a **web target ships in-tree** (`web/index.html`, `manifest.json`, 192/512 masked icons) and the README points at the live build `matrix.fossgames.online`; **GPL-3.0** (`LICENSE`, full GNU GPL v3 verbatim); no iOS directory at all | **Desk audit — no build run: this host has no Flutter/Dart SDK installed** (`which flutter dart` → both *not found*), so `flutter pub get && flutter build apk` cannot even start, and the **README is 19 lines** — a one-line description, a Ko-fi button, an online-play link, four feature bullets and the licence — with **no build, install or run command anywhere**. Nothing was compiled, nothing was adb-installed and **no frame and no APK are claimed**. Content counted from the tree rather than trusting the pack's meaningless `maps/levels=0` (literal and correct — the puzzles are generated, not stored): the real content is **code + constraint vocabulary**. **18 Dart files / 6,008 lines**, of which **3,234 are hand-written** once the **2,463 generated localisation lines** and **311 `.g.dart` codegen lines** are excluded; the brain is **`lib/domain/models/matrix_puzzle_engine.dart` at 1,024 lines** — `_FastMatrixSolver` (≈230 lines) does domain-mask propagation over `categories × floors` variables, then `countSolutions(..., maxCount: 2)` and only accepts a clue set that yields **exactly one** solution, which is the single hardest thing to get right in this genre and it is genuinely implemented. Difficulty is gated by level rather than by a random dial: **anchors** (`fixedFloor`) taper from 2 per puzzle at ≤15 down to 0 above 70, `parityFloor` and `negativeSameFloor` unlock at **≥16**, `distanceAbove`/`betweenFloors` at **≥41**; floor count steps **3 (L1–5) → 4 (≤15) → 5 (≤25) → 6 (≤40) → 7 (≤55) → 8 (≤70) → 9 (≤85) → 10**, capped by `maxFloors = 10`. Levels are **deterministically seeded** (`seed = level * 1000 + 123`) so a level number always yields the same puzzle, and the daily challenge is seeded from its date string — i.e. everyone gets the same puzzle that day with no server. Level ladder shows **20 tiles, +12 for every 12 cleared**, with unlock/completion state persisted. UI counted: **7 screens** (`home` 222 lines, `game` 764, `how_to_play` 231, `levels` 101, `daily_challenge` 80, `settings` 68, `practice` 66) and a **269-line Riverpod `GameNotifier`** that owns a real undo stack (`_history`), `applyHint` (writes the stored `actualSolution` value into the cell), per-clue cross-off with a *move-crossed-clues-to-bottom* setting, and persisted notes; storage splits into a typed **Hive box** of `GameStateModel` plus a `game_progress_box` (`current_level`, `completed_levels`). Localisation is a genuine three-locale job via ARB + `l10n.yaml` codegen: **`app_en.arb` 244 keys, `app_pt.arb` 241, `app_pt_BR.arb` 241** (three keys short of the template), generated into `app_localizations.dart` (1,592 lines) + `app_localizations_en.dart` (871). Store kit: **fastlane for `en-US` and `pt-BR` only**, each with title, short + full description, icon and **3 phone screenshots**. CI is two `workflow_dispatch` workflows — `build.yml` (Zulu JDK 17, Flutter `stable`, keystore from secrets, version-tag release) and `test.yml` — but `test.yml` is named *"Manual Build Test"* and **runs no `flutter test`**, consistent with the tree: **zero `*_test.dart` files**, so the uniqueness solver has no regression coverage at all. Art is UI-native, not asset-driven: **32 PNGs total = 20 launcher mipmaps + 8 fastlane store images + 4 web icons**, plus `favicon.ico` and 3 vector/XML launcher + splash files; **zero audio files** and no tilesets, sprites or models. Caveats, honestly: **no tests**, **no in-app statistics or streaks** despite the daily-challenge hook (grep for `streak`/`stats`/`bestTime` across `lib/` returns nothing, so the daily mode records no streak, history or completion count — a missed retention loop for a daily puzzle), a **dead** `MatrixSolver` class at the bottom of the engine whose `hasUniqueSolution()` simply `return true`s and is never called, a stale template `pubspec.yaml` description still reading *"A new Flutter project."*, **`.g.dart` files committed** alongside their sources (codegen needed after any model change), the three-key l10n gap, and only two store locales. runs on phone **2/3** (Android-first single-activity Flutter app with **no INTERNET permission**, adaptive + monochrome icons at every density, `singleTop` launch, a web build published alongside, and a two-locale F-Droid-style fastlane listing — docked because nothing was installed here and the touch interaction on a 10-floor × 3-category grid on a handset is exactly the thing a screenshot would have to prove); depth **2/3** (a **real** logic-deduction engine with a uniqueness solver and nine clue types gated by difficulty, infinite seeded levels with stable numbering, a date-seeded daily, practice mode at any size, and hint/undo/notes/cross-off aids — docked because the content pool is thin and fixed (9 categories, 10 ten-item sets, 10 theme triples, and the same 20 names recycled), there is no campaign, no statistics, no streaks and no scoring, so long-term pull rests entirely on the puzzle generator); Rules are genuinely implemented, not stubbed: `model/MemoGame.java` (205 lines) owns the deck, the two-card selection state, `setFound`, `getNextPlayer` (`ONE_PLAYER` / `DUO_PLAYER`), `isFinished`, and delegates scoring to `MemoGameHighscore`, whose formula is `baseScore − (time × tries)` floored at 0 with **base 3000 / 9000 / 50000** for Easy/Moderate/Hard — a real speed-versus-accuracy trade-off rather than a bare timer; `MemoGameTimer` is a 1 Hz `java.util.Timer` gated by an `AtomicBoolean` so a paused game accrues no time; `MemoGameDeck` shuffles with `Collections.shuffle` and builds a **`Set<Uri>` custom deck** from gallery picks, persisted via `SharedPreferences` (`MemoGameCustomImages`), so a custom deck survives restart. Code measured: **28 Java files / 3,078 lines in `app/src/main/java`** plus **4 Kotlin files / 170 lines** (`BackupRestorer.kt` 83, `BackupCreator.kt` 43, `PFMemory.kt` 40, `PFABackupService.kt` 4) — the game itself is Java; biggest units `ui/MemoActivity.java` **434** (grid build, card flip, turn hand-off), `ui/navigation/StatisticsActivity.java` **285**, `ui/navigation/DeckChoiceActivity.java` **284**, `ui/MainActivity.java` 269, `model/MemoGame.java` 205, `ui/navigation/HighscoreActivity.java` 191, `model/MemoGameDefaultImages.java` 123, `common/MemoGameLayoutProvider.java` 107. Presentation: **14 layouts** (game board, win screens for solo **and** duo, statistics fragments, welcome dialog, nav drawer) plus **`values-w820dp`** and **`values-xlarge`** for tablets. Localisation is real but uneven: base **`values` 75 strings**, then **`values-de` / `-fr` / `-ja` / `-ru` / `-zh-rCN` at 69 each**, **`values-tr` at 12**, and **`values-el` / `values-in` are 63-byte stubs containing zero strings** — six languages actually ship, two are empty placeholders. Tests are real rather than template: **3 JUnit files / 115 lines** covering deck construction (`MemoGameDeckTest` asserts `images × 2 == deck.size()`), the default-image sets and the game model. Caveats, honestly: **no audio and no haptics** anywhere in `app/src/main` (zero `SoundPool` / `MediaPlayer` / `Vibrator` references), the single biggest miss for a card-flip game; **no animations** beyond whatever `MemoActivity` does inline; the README's own header image still carries `alt="Privacy Friendly 2048 Icon"` and the manifest registers **`org.secuso.privacyfriendly2048.backup.PFABackupService`** — a copy-paste from the sibling 2048 app whose package never exists in this tree (the real class is `org.secuso.privacyfriendlymemory.backup.PFABackupService`, which `PFMemory.kt` exports), so the advertised Backup-app integration is **very likely broken as shipped**; README says targetSdk 33 while the build says 34; highscores are per-difficulty with no global board. Last commit `4b6b702`, **2026-05-10**, "Merge pull request #53 from weblate/weblate-pfa-memo-game-android-app". Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (conventional two-module Android project with a committed wrapper jar and pinned SDK levels — docked because the README documents no build command, the offline build failed in 4 s on uncached AGP 7.4.2 and Kotlin 1.7.20, and the required `:backup-api` **submodule is empty**, so nothing was assembled here); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android-native and handset-first by construction: minSdk 17 down to Jelly Bean, **zero permissions** so it is fully offline and privacy-clean, a pair-matching grid that is pure tap input, tablet/landscape-aware resources, an F-Droid listing and a companion backup app — docked because nothing was adb-installed and the backup service package mismatch would have to be confirmed on a device); depth **1.5/3** (three difficulty sizes, solo-timer and duo-hotseat modes, gallery custom decks, a real time×tries score, per-difficulty highscores and a statistics screen is a complete little game loop — docked because memo is one rule deep: no AI opponent, no variants, no progression ladder, no unlocks and no online play); polish **1.5/2** (64 hand-drawn card faces across two licensed decks, custom-deck-from-gallery, portrait and tablet layouts, win screens for both modes, a nav drawer with about/help, six real locales at 69 strings each, **zero permissions**, and genuine unit tests over deck and image sets — docked for **zero audio or haptics**, **no animations**, two empty locale stubs, the 2048 copy-paste in manifest and README, and an unminified 2022-era toolchain). **Recommendation: keep as the cleanest offline memo in the roster and a five-minute phone demo** — the fix list is short and concrete: correct `PFABackupService` to the `privacyfriendlymemory` package, fill or delete `values-el`/`values-in`, add card-flip haptics and a match sound, bump AGP/Kotlin to cached lines, then `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` and an adb play-through of one Hard board. |

Type `make`."* — and it was followed against upstream source: `make -j8` dies in `src/levelblit.c` with **`too many arguments to function 'CanGetArtifact'; expected 0, have 1`**, i.e. GCC 15's **C23 default** (where `int f()` declares *no* parameters) rejecting the tree's K&R prototypes; the actual fix, `make -j8 CFLAGS='-O2 -std=gnu17'`, was retried and then died on **`fatal error: SDL_mixer.h: No such file or directory`** — this host has **SDL 1.2.68** and **sdl2_mixer 2.8.2** but no SDL 1.2 mixer dev headers, so nothing was compiled to a binary, installed or launched, and **no frame and no APK are claimed**. The bigger finding is the entry itself: `git submodule status` reads **`-33f7deba8565f40c487f563bfe151207e98b7b81 project/jni/application/meritous`** (leading `-` = never initialised), so `project/jni/application/meritous/` is an **empty directory** — **zero game source ships in this clone** — while `project/jni/application/src` is an **8-byte plain file containing the string `meritous`** instead of the symlink the readme prescribes, `AndroidAppSettings.cfg` is a 50-byte path string, and **`app-release.apk` is a 45-byte text stub** holding `project/app/build/outputs/apk/app-release.apk`. Content was counted from the fetched upstream (`--depth 1`, `b2153880`) rather than trusting the pack's meaningless `maps/levels=0` — which is *correct here, because there is no level data: the dungeon is generated* by `src/mapgen.c` from one params file. Code measured: **10,019 lines of C across 24 `.c`/`.h` files**, of which `levelblit.c` **2,749** (rendering, PSI/artifact pickup logic), `demon.c` **2,607** (monsters, AI, projectiles), `boss.c` **1,829** (three bosses), `mapgen.c` **972** (cavern generation), `ending.c` **700**, `gamemap.c` 342, `audio.c` 266. Data pool measured: **72 files in `dat/`** = **41 PNGs** (`dat/i/` tiles, sprites, UI, font), **21 WAV** sound effects (`dat/a/`), **9 data tables** (`dat/d/` — monster/PSI/artifact/item definitions) and **1 mapgen params file** (`dat/m/`), so art and sound are complete and in-tree rather than downloaded; localisation is **a single `.po`**, i.e. one language, no multi-locale surface. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built or run here**, so no fps, control feel or phone behaviour is claimed; the wrapper repo is **8 years stale** (`bd3b5386`, 2018) and its own readme warns the bundled submodules *"may be outdated and won't compile, some contain only patch file and no sources"* — this one is simply **absent**; SDL 1.2 is EOL, the renderer is a **640×480 software blit** (`levelblit.c`), and upstream has no touch-native input layer — the Android path depends entirely on the wrapper's SDL-1.2 touch shim, which needs **`ndk-build`** on `PATH` plus Android SDK, **OpenJDK + `ant`** (the wrapper's 2018 recipe), none of which is configured for that tree here. Upstream activity is Debian/Fedora packager maintenance (`Sylvain Beucler`, `Pascal Audoux` credited in the README), not gameplay work. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (README documents `make` and every dependency, `dat/` is complete and in-tree, and one `-std=gnu17` flag stands between this source and a compile on this host — docked because as-shipped `make` **fails** on GCC 15, **SDL_mixer 1.2 dev headers are missing**, and the `games/` clone holds **no game source whatsoever**: uninitialised submodule, path-string `src`, 45-byte fake APK); runs on phone **2/3** (an SDL 1.2 Android port genuinely exists — `sdl_android` ships `AndroidData/`, `changeAppSettings.sh`, 14 build/sign scripts and ~30 working ports — and the game's top-down twin-stick shape maps to touch, but **no APK has ever been produced here**, the wrapper is a 2018 `ndk-build`+`ant`/OpenJDK-8 tree, and SDL 1.2's touch shim is exactly the layer that breaks on Android 10+); depth **2.5/3** (a genuinely deep single-player action-RPG for its era: procedural multi-layer caverns, separate monster/boss/artifact systems in 10 k lines of hand-written C, PSI circuits that gate abilities, artifacts, three bosses, saves and a real ending — docked because there is no meta-progression, one character, one mode, and run variety rests entirely on generation RNG); polish **1/2** (complete 41-image/21-sound asset set, minimap and help screens, i18n scaffolding, a save system, and it is packaged in Debian/Fedora — docked for a 640×480 software renderer, **no mobile-native UI**, one locale, no settings beyond Wuss mode, and a wrapper repo with no buildable artifact). **Recommendation: keep — it is the strongest *desktop* action-RPG in the roster and a real mobile candidate, but re-point the entry**: clone `https://gitlab.com/meritous/meritous.git` (10 k LOC, GPL-3.0, assets complete) as the judged source instead of the `sdl_android` wrapper, and treat Android as a port task — `pacman -S sdl_mixer sdl_image && make CFLAGS='-O2 -std=gnu17'` should produce a runnable binary on this host in seconds, then `git submodule update --init` in the wrapper (or a modern SDL2/NDK port) for the handset build. |

Localisation counted: base **`values` 34 strings**, then **`values-cs` 33, `values-es` 33, `values-kk` 32, `values-ru` 32** — five in-app locales at near-parity; store metadata is far wider — **18 fastlane locales** (`cs-CZ de en-US es et fr he kk-KZ pl pt pt-BR pt-PT ro ru tr uk vi zh-CN`) with **6 phone screenshots each for `en-US`, `cs-CZ` and `kk-KZ`**, per-locale 10-inch screenshots, and 56 metadata PNGs. The mechanism is visible in one interface: `PokemonGOListener` declares only `onStart()`, `onStop()` and `isGoRunning()` behind `REFRESH_INTERVAL = 1000`, i.e. once a second it asks whether GO is in the foreground and shows or hides the overlay — there is **no parsing of GO's notifications and no game state**, so it is a launcher/watchdog rather than even a client. Presentation has one genuinely strong metric: **localisation is exceptional for a 2017 hobby app — 29 locales at exact parity** (`values` 72 strings; `af-rZA ar-rSA ca-rES cs-rCZ da-rDK de-rDE el-rGR en-rUS es-rES fi-rFI fr-rFR hu-rHU it-rIT iw-rIL ja-rJP ko-rKR nl-rNL no-rNO pl-rPL pt-rBR pt-rPT ro-rRO ru-rRU sr-rSP sv-rSE tr-rTR uk-rUA vi-rVN zh-rCN zh-rTW`, **49 translated strings each**, with `MissingTranslation` lint disabled to permit the drift), against only **5 XML layouts** and **68 resource files** total. Tests: the sole `src/androidTest` file is the empty generated `ApplicationTest` stub — **zero real tests**. Dead on arrival for a second reason as well: Pokémon GO stopped exposing the log/notification hooks this app hunts during 2016–2017, so even a working build would have nothing left to watch. History: **one squashed commit** (`d7b515f`, **2017-01-26**, "Translations"), **12 MB** on disk, unmaintained since; `promotion_codes.csv` and the `translation-migrate.sh` / `update-translation.sh` scripts are the only non-app leftovers. The rules engine is the one substantial and genuinely correct piece: **7 Java files / 819 lines**, with `Board.java` 217 implementing the real Mū tōrere legality rule — for each of the eight ring nodes it rejects a move when both angular neighbours hold the mover's colour (`validMoveAdjacencyCheck`), otherwise requires the destination to be one of those neighbours or the centre, with a blanket `from == 9` escape for the putahi — and `isWin()` declaring the loss when the side to move has no `isValidMove`. `Pieces.java` is 192 lines of the same eight-node adjacency tables plus selection; `GameActivity.java` 216 wires nine buttons, a computer-vs-human toggle, who-goes-first prompt, restart dialog and win toast. The AI is where depth collapses: **`Ai.java` is 85 lines and picks uniformly at random from the legal-move list** (`rand.nextInt(moves.size())`, unguarded — an empty list throws `IllegalArgumentException`), the constructor's `difficulty` parameter is dispatched through a `switch` with only a `default:` arm and the doc comment says so outright ("Only difficulty is 0 for random number gen ai"), there is dead code building an unprinted `StringBuilder`, and the piece lookup uses the fragile array-of-five with `count` starting at 0 so `l[0]` is never populated and every caller loops `for (int i = 1; i < l.length; i++)`. No undo, no score, no move history, no variant, no difficulty, no sound, no haptics. Presentation: **2 locales** — base `values` (**18 strings**, 4 marked `translatable="false"` internal piece-state tokens, so **14 user-facing**) and a complete **`values-zh-rHK`** (13 strings) — a `values-night` theme that swaps parent to `Theme.AppCompat.DayNight` and lightens two brand colours, and nothing else (no adaptive icon, no tablet layout, no content descriptions). The settings screen is a **dead end, not a feature**: the gear button in `MainMenu.java` starts `settingsActivity`, whose 13-line `onCreate` inflates an **8-line `activity_settings.xml` containing an empty `ConstraintLayout` and no children** — tapping it opens a blank white page. Tests are only the two generated stubs (**16 + 25 lines, one `assertEquals(4, 2+2)`**). Store metadata is `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US` only — short and long descriptions, icon, **2 phone screenshots** and **one** changelog (`1.txt`, first release). History: **one commit** (`3240c4c`, **2021-07-29**, "Merge branch 'options-page' into main"), **1.4 MB** on disk, unmaintained since; the fastlane long description is a single unpunctuated line plus six bullet fragments and still says "OPTM". Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (tiny, coherent single-module project with a real wrapper, a CI workflow that does state `./gradlew build`, and a dependency set of three libraries that still resolve from Maven Central — docked because the README documents no build command, **AGP 4.2.2 cannot run on the only JDK here (27)**, **buildTools 30.0.3 and android-30 are not installed**, and `jcenter()` is declared for a 2021 build whose wrapper is the one piece that is cached); runs on phone **1.5/3** (a genuine Android-native touch game — minSdk 20, portrait-locked, nine tappable nodes, a Material menu and two theme variants — docked hard because nothing was built or adb-installed, there is **no APK evidence and no store listing to corroborate**, a targetSdk 30 payload is below current Play requirements, one launcher density, and a reachable screen that renders empty); depth **1/3** (the Mū tōrere rule — the both-sides block and putahi freedom — is implemented correctly and the game is genuinely winnable by driving a piece into a corner, but it is one 9-node board with a uniformly **random** opponent, a single mode, no undo, no score, no progression and no difficulty beyond the enum arm that does not exist); polish **1/2** (small and honest — 1.4 MB, custom drawables for both piece states, a night theme, one complete second locale and fastlane metadata where most 2021 hobby ports have none — docked for zero audio, no haptics, no animations, a settings screen that is a blank stub, a 10-line README, generated-only tests, and store text that is one run-on line in a single locale). **Recommendation: keep only as a tiny rules reference, low priority** — it is the catalog's only Mū tōrere implementation and its `isValidMove` adjacency logic is about 20 useful lines, but as a game it is a one-board demo with a random bot; The pack's `android signals: manifest=no` is **wrong** — `app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` exists and registers **9 activities** | **Desk audit — no build run; the conventional probe died in 2 s on a JDK mismatch, not a code error.** The README (**77 lines**) is entirely player-facing — about, permissions rationale, current status, screenshots, contributor roll-call, third-party credits — and documents **no build command at all**, so the standard recipe was tried: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17 ANDROID_HOME=~/Android/Sdk timeout 70 sh ./gradlew --offline -q :app:assembleDebug` → **`BUILD FAILED in 2s`**, `Could not compile settings file 'settings.gradle'` → **`Unsupported class file major version 61`** (Groovy's ASM in Gradle 6.7.1 cannot parse JDK 17 class files; AGP 4.2.0 targets JDK 8/11 and this host has only JDK 17-adoptium and JDK 27). Two further blockers sit behind that, confirmed rather than assumed: **AGP 4.2.0 is absent from the Gradle cache** (8.2.2 → 9.4.1 are present) and **platform android-30 / buildTools 30.0.3 are not installed** (35, 36, 37 are), so even with a JDK 11 the configure step would need network. Nothing was compiled, adb-installed or launched; **no frame and no APK are claimed**. Content counted from the tree rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` — literal and meaningless for a chess variant whose board is code: the substance is **28 Java files / 12,497 lines**, led by **`Mover.java` at 4,950 lines** — a hand-written move validator/animation engine with per-piece paths (`movePawn`, `pieceHasEnemies`, `pawnHasEnemies`, `movePiece(..., boolean bloodThirsty)`, both single- and multi-game animation paths) — then `MultiPlayerBoard.java` **1,501**, `SinglePlayerBoard.java` **1,227**, `MainActivity.java` **613**, `SettingsActivity.java` **450** (twelve colour pickers plus the gameplay toggles), `GameplaySettingsManager.java` 296, `AchievementHandler.java` 274, `MultiGame.java` 259, `MultiPlayerService.java` 254 (the Bluetooth RFCOMM service), `GameConnectionHandler.java` 224, `SecretActivity.java` 224 (the Konami-style easter egg), `Square.java` 201. Depth is genuinely above a one-mechanic toy: **18 achievement definitions** (`STARTED_GAME`, `WON_A_GAME`, `UNTOUCHABLE`, `PLAYED_100_GAMES`, `WON_50_GAMES`, …) with a local stats/persistence handler and an achievements UI, four distinct rule variants, a real two-player surface over Bluetooth with a host/client handshake, and a colour theming system covering board, pieces, texts, bars and selection persisted to files. Presentation counted: **11 PNGs in `res/`** (10 launcher mipmaps — no adaptive icon — plus one drawable) plus **1 app-level PNG** and **3 `icons/`** marketing images and **7 fastlane images** (5 phone screenshots + feature graphic + icon) — so the pack's `images=40` is store art plus density variants, not game art; **12 layouts**, **52 XML** drawables/resources, `values-large`/`values-sw600dp`/`values-w820dp`/`values-xlarge` tablet qualifiers, and **seven in-tree locales** at a full **70 strings each** — `values` (en), `ar`, `de`, `es`, `id`, `in` (legacy Indonesian duplicate), `ru`, `tr`; labels are deliberately jokey ("cant_move_that_bruh", "not_your_turn_stupid"). Gaps, honestly: **zero audio and no haptics** (grep finds no `MediaPlayer`/`SoundPool`/`Vibrator`), **no `src/test` or `src/androidTest` at all**, `.github/workflows` holds only an issue-response bot (no CI build), the release path relies on a `jcenter()` that has been sunset, and the clone has been untouched since **`52dd1f2`, 2023-07-13**. Breakdown: buildability **1.0/2** (small, coherent single-module project with a committed wrapper, pinned AGP, a plain dependency set and no native/NDK step — docked because the README documents no build command, the offline probe failed in 2 s with `Unsupported class file major version 61` under this host's only JDKs, AGP 4.2.0 is uncached, SDK 30 is not installed, and `jcenter()` cannot be reached); runs on phone **2.5/3** (a genuine first-party Android game: minSdk 16, portrait-locked board, tap-to-select-then-random-destination interaction that suits a single thumb, no `INTERNET` permission so the whole loop works offline, and a live F-Droid listing — docked because nothing was installed or launched here, there is no APK in the tree, and Bluetooth play needs a filled-in location permission plus paired devices to test); depth **2.0/3** (a complete, non-trivial rules core at 12.5 k lines with four variants, two AI personalities, achievements with persisted stats, a theming system and real two-human Bluetooth play — well past most board rows here — docked because the central mechanic is deliberately RNG-decided, so strategy is reduced to piece selection, and there is no campaign, online play, stats screen beyond achievements or difficulty ladder); polish **1.5/2** (deep user-facing theming, seven full locales, tablet layouts, piece-move animation, an achievements UI, an easter-egg activity and an honest F-Droid release; docked for **zero audio/haptics**, no adaptive icon, zero tests, no CI build, and a 2023-frozen codebase on a sunset repository). **Recommendation: keep** — it is the catalog's only playable chess variant and the largest hand-written rules engine outside the Ludo row, but treat it as a Java reference rather than a shipping title until the toolchain is modernised (AGP ≥8, compileSdk ≥34, JDK 17 + Gradle 8) and sound is added; to turn the audit into a run: switch the wrapper/AGP to the 8.x pair already cached here, install SDK 30 or bump `compileSdk`, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, adb-install, play one solo game with bloodthirst on, then host/join a Bluetooth match between two emulators and confirm an achievement unlocks. |

one `MainActivity` plus a non-exported `GameFetchService` that only refreshes the local library; **no iOS, desktop or web target** and **no APK in the tree** | **Desk audit — no build run; the offline configure probe failed at 1 min 15 s on an uncached AGP plugin marker, so no frame and no APK are claimed.** The README documents **no command line at all**: the whole "Development Setup" section is four GUI steps — *"Open the project in Android Studio → Synchronize the project with Gradle files → Deploy the `app` module"* — under requirements of *"Android Studio Ladybug (or newer), Android SDK 37, min Android 6.0 (API 23)"*. The conventional CLI probe was still attempted under the 90 s rule: `JAVA_HOME=~/.gradle/jdks/jetbrains_s_r_o_-25 ANDROID_HOME=~/Android/Sdk timeout 80 ./gradlew --offline :app:assembleDebug` → **BUILD FAILED in 1m15s**, `Plugin [id: 'com.android.application', version: '9.2.1'] was not found` ("could not resolve plugin artifact `com.android.application:com.android.application.gradle.plugin:9.2.1`" across Google / MavenRepo / Plugin Portal). Inputs were checked rather than assumed: the **gradle-9.6.0-bin wrapper distribution, AGP 9.2.0/9.2.1/9.3.1/9.4.1, JDK 17/25 and SDK platforms 35/36/37.0 are all present on this host**, so the failure is a missing *plugin marker* in the offline Gradle cache, not a broken repo — one networked `./gradlew assembleDebug` would very likely go through. Content counted directly rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal and correct: a launcher ships no content of its own — the "content" is whatever the user has installed): **117 Kotlin files / 13,035 lines**, split **59 files under `features/`** (`games_launcher`, `game_details`, `app_picker`, `analytics`, `settings`, `licenses`) and **50 under `core/`** (`data`, `domain`, `ui`, `util`) plus one `di` module and the Application class — a genuinely feature-modularised codebase, not a demo. Weight sits in **`SettingsScreen.kt` 48.5 KB**, `core/ui/components/FieldHeight.kt` 29.2 KB, `LicenseComponents.kt` 27.9 KB, `ArcadeButton.kt` 19.0 KB, `LaunchOverlay.kt` 18.0 KB, `GamesLauncherScreen.kt` 15.5 KB and `GamesLauncherViewModel.kt` 13.4 KB, with `GameRepositoryImpl.kt` 10.5 KB and a **Room** persistence layer behind it. Presentation is **UI-native and hand-built**: **51 PNG/XML resources** in total — five-density `mipmap-*` launcher sets plus an adaptive `mipmap-anydpi-v26` icon, custom Compose components instead of stock Material widgets, a `HeroBannerPager`, `FilterChipsRow`, Palette-derived dynamic theming and a launch overlay; **0 audio, 0 models, 0 sprites** (there is nothing to draw a game with — it draws your library). **One locale only** (`values/strings.xml`, **83 user-facing strings**, no `values-xx` translations) and `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US` only (title, short + full description, icon, **2 phone screenshots**, changelogs `1.txt`/`3.txt`). Tests are the generated stub alone (**`app/src/test/.../ExampleUnitTest.kt`** — no real suite, no hand-written `androidTest`). History is a **single squashed commit** (`d3a99cd`, **2026-07-04**, "feat(ui): add reduce animations setting and game launch overlay"), version 1.0.2, **2.0 MB** on disk. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (clean modern single-module Compose project with a committed wrapper, pinned AGP/Kotlin/Compose versions, a versioned dependency catalogue, R8 and configuration-cache enabled, an honest CHANGELOG — docked because the README gives only Android-Studio clicks and **no CLI build command**, and the offline configure probe died on an uncached `com.android.application:9.2.1` plugin marker, so nothing was compiled here); runs on phone **2.5/3** (it *is* the phone target: Android-only, minSdk 23 covering Android 6, Material-3 Compose UI, adaptive icon, play-time through the sanctioned UsageStats API, GitHub + F-Droid release flavours and a working fastlane listing — docked only because nothing was adb-installed or launched in this pass, and `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` is a Play-policy-sensitive permission for a launcher); depth/replay **0.5/3** (as software it is substantial — categories, tag management, play-time analytics, per-game theming, license attribution — but as a *game* it has **no game loop, no objective, no score, no opponent and no content of its own**, so there is nothing to replay; the rubric measures play value, and this is the shell around other games); polish **1.5/2** (13 k lines of feature-modular Kotlin, a hand-built component library, dynamic palette theming, a reduce-animations accessibility setting, a generated licenses screen, a system splash screen, offline by construction with no INTERNET permission, and clean MIT/F-Droid packaging — docked for **one locale**, **zero real tests**, no CI workflow, a single-commit history, and only two store screenshots). **Recommendation: drop it from `GAMES.queue`** — same call as Oblique Strategies, MHGU-Database and Nounours: it entered via the F-Droid *Games* category but it is a **launcher**, not a game. It is however the cleanest modern Compose reference in this catalog (feature-module layout, Koin DI, Voyager navigation, Room + KSP, Palette theming) if the collection ever needs an offline front-end for its own APKs — in which case the honest next step is one networked `./gradlew assembleDebug`, adb-install on `pm_pixel`, grant UsageStats access and confirm play-time tracking populates. |
| 145 | **OpenDungeons** (`OpenDungeons`) | Real-time strategy / dungeon management — the Dungeon Keeper formula rebuilt open-source: dig and build an underground dungeon from a couple of dozen room types, lure and satisfy creatures so they work your rooms, then fight rival keepers with hand-cast spells, traps and indirectly ordered minions; singleplayer skirmish over 10 hand-designed levels, 11 multiplayer levels including free-for-all and alliance setups, plus a shipped in-game level editor | *_5.0 — desk audit_* (buildability 0.5 · phone target 0.5 · depth 2.5 · polish 1.5) | **Desktop only — no mobile target of any kind** — **C++11 + CMake + OGRE 3D 1.9+ + CEGUI 0.8+ + OIS + SFML 2.x + Boost + Threads**, all required through `find_package(OGRE REQUIRED CONFIG)` / `CEGUI` / `OIS` / `SFML 2` with hard version floors (`CMakeLists.txt` 509–532); **GPL-3.0-or-later** for source per `LICENSE.md` (art/audio carry separate terms under `licenses/`); commit `a9efe49`, 2025-10-04; **294 MB** on disk; last real release **0.7.1, 24 Aug 2016** per `RELEASE-NOTES.md`; **zero Android signals** — `grep -ril android` across gradle/manifest files returns nothing, no `android/` directory, no APK, and no iOS or web shell; packaging is `snapcraft.yaml`, `dist/`, `appveyor.yml` and `scripts/{unix,win32}` | **Desk audit — no build run; the CMake configure was attempted and died before compiling a single file, so no binary, frame or gameplay is claimed.** Two real failures captured rather than assumed: (1) plain `cmake -S . -B …` → **`Compatibility with CMake < 3.5 has been removed`** at `CMakeLists.txt:1`, i.e. the project's `cmake_minimum_required` predates this host's CMake and must be overridden with `-DCMAKE_POLICY_VERSION_MINIMUM=3.5` just to start; (2) with that override the configure gets only as far as **`Package 'OIS' not found` → `Required library OIS not found!`** (`cmake/modules/FindOIS.cmake:82`), the first link in a chain this host cannot satisfy: `pacman -Qq` and `/usr/include` show **no OGRE, no CEGUI and no OIS** — only `sfml` — and the Arch/CachyOS repos ship **ogre 14.6.3**, an API-incompatible major line against code that requires `OGRE_VERSION >= 1.9.0` plus CEGUI 0.8, with neither CEGUI nor OpenInputSystem packaged for the distro at all. The 90 s window was never a viable path and **nothing was compiled, installed or launched**. The README (about 100 lines) documents **no command line** — its "Build instructions" section lists the five required libraries and then defers the actual recipe to a GitHub wiki page — while the repo does carry `scripts/unix/` and `scripts/win32/` helpers. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (levels are `.level` files, invisible to that glob): **22 level files = 10 `levels/skirmish/*.level` + 11 `levels/multiplayer/*.level` + 1 `levels/template.level` editor template**. Systems counted from `config/*.cfg`, which is where this game's depth lives: **34 `[Creature]` blocks** across the 3,105-line `config/creatures.cfg` (Kobold, DwarfWorker, Rat, Orc, LavaSpawn, LizardMan among the first) and **50 equipment records** in `config/equipments.cfg`, against **20 room classes** in `source/rooms/`, **7 trap classes** in `source/traps/`, **13 spell classes** in `source/spells/` and **11 creature-skill classes** in `source/creatureskill/` — creatures, rooms, traps and spells are first-class moddable data plus code, not hard-coded set pieces. Art and audio counted: **238 model files / 452 material scripts** (105 MB of materials alone — the OGRE material and particle pipeline), **106 sound files**, **3 music tracks** (10 MB) and **60 GUI files** (2.2 MB) across `gui/`, `particles/` and `config/`. Code measured: **246 `.cpp` + 243 `.h` files / 71,523 C++ lines** in 4.1 MB of `source/`, the largest subdirectories being `modes/` (24), `entities/` (24), `creatureaction/` (24), `rooms/` (20), `renderscene/` (19) and `utils/` (15). Honest caveats: the project is explicitly **archived** — the README's first section says activity stopped years ago and points at the `tomluchowski/OpenDungeonsPlus` fork as the live continuation; the host cannot build it without vendoring three 2016-era libraries; and **nothing was played**, so creature AI quality, pathfinding and multiplayer stability are unverified. Breakdown: buildability **0.5/2** (a real, coherent multi-target CMake project with committed unix/win32 build scripts, a Snap recipe and AppVeyor CI, and a README that at least names every dependency — docked hard because the hosted configure cannot run: modern CMake rejects the `cmake_minimum_required` line, OIS/CEGUI/OGRE-1.9 are absent from the distro entirely, the packaged OGRE is a decade ahead of the required API, and no build command is documented in the tree); runs on phone **0.5/3** (desktop-only in the strictest sense — no Android project, no manifest, no touch input path, no mobile build target, and the OGRE/CEGUI keyboard-and-mouse loop needs a port that does not exist; the half point is only for being a title that could in principle be re-targeted); depth **2.5/3** (genuinely deep: 22 level files across skirmish and multiplayer, 34 creatures over a happiness/needs simulation, 20 buildable rooms, 7 traps, 13 spells, 11 skills, 50 equipment records, factions, and an in-game level editor — real Dungeon Keeper-grade systems design, docked because singleplayer content is 10 levels and the AI and multiplayer layers are unverified); polish **1.5/2** (238 models and 452 materials, 106 sound effects, three music tracks, voice-overs on game events, an animated scripted main menu, a minimap, full GUI skinning, and nine years of issue-tracked release notes — docked for a 2016 code freeze and total abandonment). **Recommendation: keep as a desktop reference donor, not a phone title** — it is the catalog's deepest dungeon-management simulation, and its `config/*.cfg` creature/room/spell tables plus 238 OGRE models are a strong donor pool for an isometric dungeon game, but it scores low here because this index measures phone targets and OpenDungeons has no mobile path whatsoever. To turn the audit into a run: vendor OGRE 1.9 + CEGUI 0.8 + OIS into a container (or take the published Snap), configure with `-DOD_USE_SFML_WINDOW=ON`, build, and play one skirmish level to the rival keeper's fall. |

Content counted directly rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal — this is a card game, all content is imagery): **246 PNGs**, and the substance is **four complete decks at 53 files each — `rus` (A. Charlemagne 19th-century Russian, public domain), `fra` (David Bellot, LGPL-3), `int` (Chris Aguilar, LGPL-3), `psu`** — i.e. **52 card faces plus a back per deck, 212 card images**, against 4 suit glyphs, 6 logo/badge screens, 2 UI plates, 2 holiday splash images and 2 backgrounds. Code measured: **16 Kotlin files / 2,675 lines** in `core` — `GameScreen.kt` **896** (the whole table layout, card animation, throw-in/beat/pass/take state machine and per-player card scaling), `Player.kt` **408** (hand sorting, thrown/beaten/passed/done/take events), `PlayerTesting.kt` **311** (a *headless* duplicate of the rules engine with no transitions, which `DesktopLauncher` drives via a real `--test-players <players> <games>` batch mode over all four deck sizes — the tidiest thing in the repo), `SettingsScreen.kt` **309**, `MainMenuScreen.kt` 161, `ResultScreen.kt` 130, `NewGameScreen.kt` 125, plus `Card`/`Deck`/`Rank`/`Suit`/`RuleSet`/`Holiday` models; `RuleSet` carries exactly five knobs (`deuceBeatsAce`, `loweredFirstDiscardLimit`, `allowPass`, `teamPlay`, `cardCount`) exposed as real checkboxes/select boxes on the new-game screen. The honest gap is opponents: grepping `core` for `AI|bot|computer|intelligence|thinking|auto.?play` returns **nothing but a `cardScaleAI` layout constant**, and the i18n bundle has no AI/computer string, so this is **hot-seat pass-the-phone for 2–5 humans** — the README's "Planned → player names (both AI and human)" confirms the bot was never written. Presentation: **5 locales at 54 keys each** (`OpenFool.properties` base plus `_en`, `_cs`, `_de`, `_ru`, complete parity), four hand-picked licensed card decks selectable in-game, portrait-locked, and a holiday splash. Gaps: no audio at all, no tests, no stats or persistence, no online play, no AI, and a source tree untouched since January 2020. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a coherent four-module Gradle project with a committed wrapper, a README that states the run command, and a self-contained rules harness you can execute without a display — docked because the exact wrapper distribution is uncached here, compileSdk 28/buildTools 28.0.3 are not installed, all libGDX deps would need network, and there is no LICENSE file behind the MIT claim); runs on phone **2.5/3** (the Android module is a first-class target — minSdk 14, portrait-locked, touch card table, shipped on F-Droid as `ru.hyst329.openfool` — docked only because nothing was built or adb-installed here and no APK is in the tree); depth **1.5/3** (a complete, correctly-structured Durak with the real throw-in rules, partnership 2v2, four deck sizes and three rule switches is a genuine game rather than a toy, but it is one card game with **no AI opponent**, no progression, no scoring record and no online play, so a solo player has nobody to play against); polish **1.5/2** (four fully licensed art decks with attribution in the README, five complete locales, a clean menu/new-game/table/result screen flow with animated card movement, and a headless rules simulator — docked for zero audio, no haptics, no tests, no persistence and a 2020 freeze). **Recommendation: keep as a rules-deck donor, low gameplay priority** — the 212 card faces across four licensed decks plus `RuleSet`/`PlayerTesting` are directly reusable for any card-game prototype, but as a phone title it needs an AI first. To turn the audit into a run: install buildTools 28.0.3 + platform 28 (or bump to 34/AGP 8.x), `./gradlew :android:assembleDebug`, adb-install, play one 2-player game through to a loser and one 2v2 partnership game, and confirm the three rule toggles change play. |

The full build then finished **inside an 85 s `timeout` at `-j12`**: `[100%] Built target jagamex86_64`, and the tree produced **`openjk_sp.x86_64` (1,311,528 B)**, **`openjk.x86_64` (2,041,776 B)**, `openjkded.x86_64`, the `compact_glsl` shader preprocessor, and the whole gamecode set — `code/game/jagamex86_64.so`, `code/rd-vanilla/rdsp-vanilla_x86_64.so`, `codemp/game/jampgamex86_64.so`, `codemp/cgame/cgamex86_64.so`, `codemp/ui/uix86_64.so`, `codemp/rd-vanilla/rd-vanilla_x86_64.so`, `codemp/rd-rend2/rd-rend2_x86_64.so` — with only C/C++ ABI notices on `q_shared.h:2349` (a C++ `sg_export` member inside an `extern "C"` struct) and **no errors**; full log at `evidence/openjk-build-run-2026-11-07.log`. Running the SP binary under headless Xvfb printed `OpenJK: latest linux-x86_64` / `Initialising zone memory` / `----- FS_Startup -----` / `0 files in pk3 files` / **`Couldn't load default.cfg`** and dropped to an error dialog — i.e. **the engine works and the game does not exist in this clone**, exactly the Tier B contract (source free, data retail): the tree has **0 `.pk3` files** and no `base/assets*.pk3`, so the 26 images the pack counted are **`shared/icons/PNG/j{ao,mp,sp}{16,32,48,64,128,256,512}.png` launcher/window icons**, not content. Code counted rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0`: **1,197 C/C++ files / 1,216,348 lines** across **`code/` 423,081** (SP engine + shared gamecode, 434 files), **`codemp/` 597,230** (MP engine, botlib, rend2 — 531 files), **`codeJK2/` 185,057** (198 files) and **`shared/` 10,980**; the Jedi-Academy-specific substance is **`code/game` 191,335 lines**, `code/cgame` 51,572, `code/ui` 21,077, with `codemp/botlib` **36,437** of Q3-derived navigation/bot AI and `codeJK2/game` **127,896**; the largest single files are exactly the systems this engine is famous for — **`bg_pmove.cpp` 15,144** (movement/physics prediction), **`wp_saber.cpp` 14,452** (per-blade saber tracing, bolt deflection, disarm), `ui_shared.cpp` 11,551, `Q3_Interface.cpp` 11,401 (Icarus scripting bridge), `cg_players.cpp` 8,458 (Ghoul2 skeletal animation), **`AI_Jedi.cpp` 7,669** (saber-aware NPC AI), `bg_panimate.cpp` 7,258, `g_combat.cpp` 6,981. Docs are real engineering notes for **884 lines across 5 files** (`renderer-architecture.md`, `save games.md`, `libraries.md`, `sound-code-declutter.md`, `language strings.md`), and `codemp/rd-rend2` carries a full modern renderer (`tr_backend`, `tr_animation`, `G2_*` Ghoul2 skinning, vendored `MikkTSpace`, `stb_image`, `glext.h`) with its own `glsl/` shader set. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was played**; the README's build instructions are **two wiki links** (`Compilation guide`, `Debugging`) with **no in-tree command**, so the successful invocation above was mine, not the project's; there are **no assets, no menus, no maps and no sound** here to judge presentation from; the player install path assumes you already own Jedi Academy; `git tag` in this shallow clone resolves oddly (`Git tag is latest`); and `BuildJK2SPEngine` defaults off, matching the "needs attention" verdict for JK2. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (configures in 0.9 s and compiles end to end in under 85 s with `-j12` against stock distro libraries, with meaningful option switches and a CMake project clearly kept alive by CI — docked because the README documents no command line, `BuildTests` needs Boost and is off, and the build is only half a deliverable without retail assets); runs on phone **0.5/3** (strictly desktop: no Android/iOS target, no touch input path, no mobile build files anywhere; the half point is for SDL2-abstracted platform code and shipped ARM / Apple-Silicon support, which makes a port plausible rather than present); depth **2.5/3** (AAA-grade simulation: 15 k lines of movement physics, 14 k of saber combat with per-blade traces and deflection, Ghoul2 per-vertex skeletal deformation, Icarus scripting, force powers, saber-aware NPC AI, a full singleplayer campaign and MP with bots plus a dedicated server — docked because **none of that content is in the clone**, and JK2 MP is unsupported); polish **1.5/2** (a decade of maintenance aimed at stability and portability, an SDL2 layer replacing the original Win32/DirectX code, a PBR `rend2` renderer with MikkTSpace tangents, five design docs, GitHub Actions CI and Coverity scanning, and honest README scoping — docked for shipping zero art/audio/UI in-repo and no committed test suite). **Recommendation: keep as the catalog's flagship Tier B engine donor, not as a phone title** — it is the deepest codebase in this catalog (1.2 M lines, real physics and skeletal animation, a buildable dedicated server) and the natural donor for any third-person action prototype, but it is legally and practically a *shell*: to turn the audit into a run, install retail `assets*.pk3` into `base/`, then `/devmap` one campaign map and confirm saber combat, Ghoul2 animation and force powers. |

ON)`, so the two clones in this collection are the two halves of one product | **Desk audit — no build run; there is no game to run and the packaging tool is absent, so nothing was compiled, packaged or launched.** The README is **five lines plus a CI badge** — it names the repo's purpose and links `OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2` for the codebase, and documents **no command of any kind**. Packaging was read rather than assumed: `tools/scripts/build.mjs` copies `objects/` to `artifacts/`, rewrites each object's image array, then shells out to **`gxc build <lgxFilename> images.json`** to compile multi-sprite `.lgx` blobs and finally to `zip`/`7z` to produce `.parkobj` + `.zip` — and on this host `command -v gxc` and `command -v dotnet` both return nothing (only `/usr/sbin/7z` exists), so the 90 s window had no viable path: `gxc` ships with the engine's own image tooling, and CI builds it via **`dotnet restore/build/publish -r linux-x64`** under `.NET 6` for both `objexport` and the tooling. Content was counted directly instead of trusting the pack's meaningless `maps/levels=0  images=2188` — the PNG figure is right but the level figure is a category error for an asset pack. **422 `object.json` files**, and their declared `objectType` distribution is the honest measure of the pack: **180 `scenario_meta`** (the RCT1/2 campaign scenario descriptors — park name, objective, loan, date, guests, research), **79 `ride`**, **38 `footpath_surface`**, **35 `scenery_wall`**, **25 `scenery_small`**, **19 `terrain_edge`**, **15 `footpath_railings`**, **14 `station`**, **8 `terrain_surface`**, **7 `scenery_large`**, **1 `scenery_group`**, **1 `footpath_item`**. Provenance is recorded per object in `sourceGame`: **187 objects declare an array** (multi-game), and the singletons split **62 `official`** (OpenRCT2's own new content), **45 `rct1`**, **43 `rct2`**, **33 `rct1aa`** (Added Attractions), **22 `rct1ll`** (Loopy Landscapes), **7 `rct2ww`** (Wacky Worlds), **5 `rct2tt`** (Time Twister) — i.e. the pack is a complete licence-clean reimplementation of the retail object set, not a sample. Art counted: **2,188 PNGs across 349 objects that carry their own `images/` tree** (ride art alone is **740 PNGs**, `terrain_edge` **328**, `terrain_surface` **119**, `scenario_meta` **74** park previews, `scenery_small` 47, `scenery_wall` 42), while the remaining objects intentionally ship **no bitmaps** — **35 `object.json` files index the engine's original sprite base via `$G1`-style references** and **318 carry the retail `originalId`** (`00000000|#ALPINEC|00000000` in the alpine-coaster example) so an object can be matched to or emitted as its retail counterpart. Metadata is deep rather than decorative: the sample ride declares `minCarsPerTrain`/`maxCarsPerTrain`/`numEmptyCars`/`tabCar`/`defaultCar`/`buildMenuPriority`/`noCollisionCrashes`/`riderControlsSpeed` plus a four-way `carColours` matrix, and each ride object carries a full `cars` array of per-vehicle sprite groups and peep positions. Localisation is the pack's quietest achievement: **all 422 objects carry a `strings` block**, with **937 `en-GB` string records and 18 locales at real coverage** — `en-GB` 937, `ca-ES` 879, `pl-PL` 873, `zh-CN` 866, `pt-BR` 862, `fi-FI` 849, `fr-FR` 848, `ko-KR` 773, `hu-HU` 757, `es-ES` 669, `de-DE` 637, `ru-RU` 620, `ja-JP` 591, `it-IT` 575, `nl-NL` 571, `sv-SE` 557, `cs-CZ` 557, `tr-TR` 156 — i.e. object names and descriptions are translated in-tree, which is why OpenRCT2 speaks 18 languages without the retail object files. Honest caveats: **it is not a game** — there is no loop, no objective, no score, and nothing to run; it is inert without the `openrct2` engine clone (which in turn still needs retail **RCT2 `Data/g1.dat`** to boot, so this pack does not remove the Tier-B data dependency); the **README documents no build or install command**; packaging needs `gxc` + `dotnet` + `zip`, none of which is on this host; and the clone is **one squashed commit** (`978f596`, "Autoformat objects"), so history is not auditable. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a coherent, small toolchain — an F# objexport with a .NET 6 project and an `sl`n, a Node packaging script with documented flags `--no-zip|--parallel|--verbose`, a green CI badge and a publish path for linux-x64/win-x64 — docked because the README gives **no command**, the packaging chain needs `gxc` (absent here) and `dotnet` (absent), and "build" for a data pack is only ever a repackage of already-committed PNG+JSON); runs on phone **0.5/3** (nothing executes; the consumer engine `games/openrct2` is desktop-only with no Android manifest, APK or touch path, so the half point is only for the pack being the platform-independent half of a product that could in principle be re-targeted); depth/replay **1/3** (as *content* it is the deepest object set here — 79 rides with per-car vehicle definitions and colour matrices, 180 scenario descriptors, 26 terrain primitives, 15 footpath railings and 25 scenery groups, all with retail provenance — but as *software* there is no game loop, no progression and no opponent, so nothing is replayable on its own); polish **1.5/2** (all 422 objects localised into 18 languages, 2,188 committed PNGs, licence-clean CC-BY-4.0 with per-object author attribution, `originalId` mapping for every retail object, JSON formatted to a committed prettifier, and it is the asset layer of a shipping GPL engine — docked for **zero README documentation, a missing packaging toolchain, no tests beyond CI's objexport self-check, and a single-commit history**). **Recommendation: keep, reclassified** — this is not a queue-eligible *game* and should be marked as the **content half of the `openrct2` entry** rather than scored as a title; it is however a first-class donor pool (2,188 isometric sprites at a consistent scale plus 422 typed JSON records with 18 locales) for any isometric park/tycoon prototype. To turn the audit into a run: build `gxc` from the engine's image tooling, `node tools/scripts/build.mjs --no-zip`, then drop the resulting `.parkobj` set into an OpenRCT2 install that has retail `g1.dat` and start a scenario from one of the 180 `scenario_meta` objects. |

Art and audio counted: **164 PNGs**, **116 `.spr` sprite-compilation definitions** across `core` (camera, water, profiler), **11 enemy types** (`marmot`, `marmotred`, `wolfey`, `harrier`, `mosquito`, `lady_bugsy`, `crococopter`, `fish`, `jumping_fish`, `salamander`, `springfling`), **3 bosses** (`giant_wolf`, `hydra`, `salamander_boss`) and **20+ item/entity types** (`pipes`, `bridges`, `bubbles`, `bumpers`, `capsule`, `collectible`, `conveyor_belt`, `door`, `elevator`, `goal`, `layers`, `checkpoint`, `spikes`, `powerups`, `audio_source`, `event_trigger`, …), plus **15 `themes/` backdrops with separate `.bg`/`.brk` layers and a `waterworks_indoors_fg.bg` foreground**, **22 music tracks (24 MB)** and **91 sound samples (8.1 MB)** — the changelog's 0.6.1.3 notes a reworked Audio System (global pause/resume, underwater muffler formula, drowning-music fix). Scripting is the real depth: **170 `.ss` SurgeScript files (1.3 MB)** split across `functions` 36, `items` 29, `debug_mode` 27, `setup` 17, `players` 14, `enemies` 11, `core` 10, `ui` 7, `misc` 9, `behaviors` 4, `bosses` 3, `surge_the_rabbit` 2 — i.e. the whole game loop, 5 characters (`surge`, `tux`, `neon`, `charge`, `none`) and its UI are scripts, which is what "engine" means here. Code: **241 C/C++ files**, **90,702 lines of `.c`** under `src/{core,entities,physics,scenes,scripting,util,misc,third_party}` (a physics dir, a scene/state machine and a SurgeScript binding layer — not one big `main.c`). Localisation: **13 `.lng` files** (English, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Portuguese, Polish, Russian, Ukrainian, Lithuanian, Indonesian, Esperanto, plus an `extends` include dir). Caveats, honestly: **never built or played here**, so collision feel, the level editor and `--game-folder` MOD loading are unverified; README's install step assumes `sudo`; the Android build is a custom Makefile rather than a committed Gradle/CI project, so no APK is reproducible without downloading its whole dependency chain; the shallow clone pins one commit of an engine whose *games* (MODs) live on the website, not in-tree. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (README documents the exact three-command CMake flow and an Android pipeline exists with pinned versions and F-Droid packaging — docked because Allegro, SurgeScript and PhysicsFS are all absent from this host so configure aborted at `allegro.h` and nothing was compiled); runs on phone **2.5/3** (genuinely mobile-targeted: `WANT_GLES` OpenGL ES path, a real NDK Makefile demanding arm64-v8a + armeabi-v7a, an F-Droid listing at 6.1.3.0 / versionCode 30650, 11 locales of store metadata, gamepad auto-detection and a touch-friendly menu-script UI — docked because nothing was installed and no frame exists); depth **2.5/3** (27 authored levels / 30 k lines of tile data, 8 quests, 11 enemy types, 3 bosses, 20+ interactive entity types, 5 playable characters, a built-in level editor and 170 SurgeScript files that make the whole game moddable — docked because the campaign is a short arcade run rather than a 100-hour sandbox, and mods must be sourced from the site); polish **1.5/2** (22 tracks, 91 samples, 15 themed parallax backdrops with foreground layers, 13 languages, a decade of releases with a detailed 2026 changelog, F-Droid + repology packaging and a permissive MIT licence on the game content — docked for zero automated tests in the tree, a `sudo make install` README assumption and no CI for the Android APK). **Recommendation: keep as the catalog's best engine-grade 2D platformer** — it is the real alternative to shipping an engine-only clone, since code, 164 sprites, 22 tracks and a working level/enemy/item script set all arrive in one MIT-content tree; to turn the audit into a run: `pacman -S allegro physfs`, build SurgeScript 0.6.1 from source, then `cmake .. && make -j12` and play `quests/surge.qst` under Xvfb, or run `make -C src/misc/android apk` with the NDK for the phone target. |

Code counted: **20 Kotlin files / 3,232 lines** (3,052 under `app/src/main/kotlin`) — `World.kt` 630 (libGDX-free simulation: fixed 1/60 sub-steps, two flat owner/trail arrays, BFS capture, collision priorities), `GameScreen.kt` 464, `BotNet.kt` 459 (ONNX/Kotlin forward + `encodeAt` as a 1:1 port of the gym's `encode_all`), `WorldRenderer.kt` 429 (smoothstep coverage-texture shader, miter ribbon trails), `SoundFx.kt` 146 (**audio synthesised at runtime** into a WAV buffer — the only bundled assets are two TTFs and the two bot nets). Beside it, a real **PyTorch self-play gym**: **12 Python files / 2,302 lines** with a Numba batched rule kernel mirrored 1:1 from `World.kt`, parity tests (`gym/tests/test_env.py`), `train.py`/`tournament.py`/`export.py` and a `run_pipeline.sh` league pipeline, plus a documented reward lesson (pure Δarea makes agents turtle; a dense territory-hold term fixes it). Tests are real: **10 `@Test` cases** in `SimTest.kt` driving `World` headless (capture grows area, incremental area matches the grid, wall and self-cross kill, 40 s 8-bot stability, spawn never lands on turf). Caveats, honestly: nothing was played here; **depth is one arcade mode** — no campaign, no unlock progression, no online/hot-seat multiplayer (a genuine single-player-only design), so replay rests on bots + best-score chasing; the repo is a **shallow one-commit clone** (`20f15fe`, 2026-08-02) and the neural nets are 2.1 MB binary blobs with no training checkpoint in-tree (retraining needs an NVIDIA GPU); the 91.8 MB debug APK carries all four ABIs and both nets, so per-ABI release splits are the only sane distribution size. | 153 | **PipePanic** (`PipePanic`) | Puzzle — timed pipe-connection (plumber): random pieces arrive from a 3-piece preview bar and you place them on an **11×11 board** to join the green start pipe to the red end pipe before the timer expires; at zero (or on tapping **Fill Pipes**) the non-connecting "dead" pieces are cleared and the network is flooded, scoring the leak-free area. Placed pipes **age and randomly break** (`pipebreakprob`), so the board you built decays under you; difficulty is an unbounded breakage/aging scale, and seeded **battle mode** boards make scores comparable | *_6.0 — desk audit_* (buildability 1 · phone target 2.5 · depth/replay 1.5 · polish 1) | **Android-only Java: a 282-line WebView shell around a browser game** — `org.olgsoft.apipepanic` (namespace and package agree), **minSdk 14 / compileSdk 30 / targetSdk 30**, **AGP 8.4.1** on a committed **Gradle 8.6-bin** wrapper resolving from `google()` + `mavenCentral()`; the whole app is one activity plus **`app/src/main/res` = 11 files** (1 layout, 1 menu, five mipmap launchers, `values/{dimens,strings,styles}` and a `values-sw720dp-land/dimens` tablet bucket, **no locale variants**); `WebSettings` enables `setJavaScriptEnabled` / `setDomStorageEnabled` / `setDatabaseEnabled` and a wide viewport with zoom controls suppressed, plus an immersive-fullscreen helper, `onConsoleMessage → Log.d("PipePanic")`, pause-on-leave and back-button handling; **the game itself is vendored JS**: `assets/jspp/jspp.js` **1,347 lines**, `mersenne-twister.js` **208** (seeded PRNG), `index.html` **481**, `assets/jspp/` **340 KB**; licences split cleanly — **MIT** for the port (README/LICENSE) and the original JS by Thunor, **Free Art License 1.3** for the art (`assets/jspp/COPYING-ARTWORK`); shipped on **F-Droid** (`org.olgsoft.apipepanic`, badge + `screenshot.png` + launcher icon in the tree); **no APK committed** | **Desk audit — no build run: nothing was compiled, installed or launched, and no frame and no APK are claimed.** The 90 s window was not entered because three pinned inputs are missing from this host: wrapper **Gradle 8.6-bin** is not in `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists` (5.4.1 → 9.8.2 are, minus 8.6), **platform `android-30` for `compileSdkVersion 30`** is not installed (`~/Android/Sdk/platforms` = 35, 36, 37.0 only), and the README — 97 lines, but a play guide rather than a build doc — documents **no Gradle command at all** (it offers only the F-Droid link and an `htmlpreview` link to the raw `index.html`). Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's meaningless `maps/levels=0`: this is a procedural grid puzzle with **no level files by design**, and the art pool is real — **45 animated `.gif` pipe sprites** plus **6 `.png`** in `assets/jspp/images` (51 image files) and 5 more PNGs as launcher/store art, i.e. every pipe piece, the preview bar, the water flood and the HUD digits are animated GIFs. Mechanics read out of the code, not the README: `pprows = ppcolumns = 11` with the in-source note *"no value different from 11 will work for now"* (**121 squares**), a 3-slot `pppreviewarray`, dead-pipe clearing from `ppcleardeadpipesy = pprows - 1`, and a genuine decay rule — `pipebreakprob = pipebreakprob_base * ppdifficulty`, `pppipebreakagestep = pppipebreakagestep_base * (ppdifficulty + 1)`, with a broken piece reset to `ppnullpipeval`; difficulty is a continuous scale rendered as `(H)`, `(VH)`, `(D<n>)` and an `(∞)` mode, and **high scores persist per difficulty and per battle-mode seed through `window.localStorage` (`"pphighscore" + item`)**, which is why the wrapper sets `setDomStorageEnabled`/`setDatabaseEnabled` — an 8-reference `ppbattlemodeseed` path plus the bundled Mersenne Twister means identical, shareable boards, the one modern idea in a 2013-era script. Audio is **zero**: not one `.wav`, `.ogg` or `.mp3` anywhere in the tree, so the flood and the flash-highscore animation are silent. Tests: **none** — no `src/test`, no `src/androidTest`, no CI config. Toolchain honesty: `compileSdk 30` + `targetSdk 30` under **AGP 8.4.1** is a half-modernised pairing (a 2024 plugin pointed at a 2020 SDK); touch input is entirely the WebView's default DOM click handling — `MainActivity` has **no `MotionEvent`, `GestureDetector` or `setOnTouchListener`** — so the wrapper adds a launcher icon, offline assets, fullscreen and lifecycle pauses, and nothing else. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a complete, internally consistent one-module Gradle project with a real wrapper, manifest, launcher icons and a vendored game that runs in any browser — docked because the README documents no build command, and **Gradle 8.6 and platform android-30** are both absent here, so nothing was assembled); runs on phone **2.5/3** (a genuine handset product, not a shim: F-Droid release under `org.olgsoft.apipepanic`, minSdk 14, offline assets with no INTERNET need, immersive fullscreen, zoom controls suppressed for touch, tablet dimens bucket, and a fixed 11×11 grid that suits a portrait phone — docked because nothing was adb-installed and the WebView path was never exercised); depth/replay **1.5/3** (a real loop with a failure mode the README never mentions: pipe breakage scales with difficulty, so skill is board maintenance, not just laying tiles; seeded battle boards and per-seed high scores add replay — docked for one mode on one fixed-size board, no campaign, no unlocks, no multiplayer and no audio); polish **1/2** (45 hand-animated GIF tiles give the board actual character, the high-score flash and per-difficulty/per-seed persistence are thoughtful, licences are split properly between code and art, and it has a decade of F-Droid releases — docked for **zero sound**, one Java file with no tests, no localisation, `targetSdk 30` far behind Play policy, and a README that never says how to build it). **Recommendation: keep, low priority** — it is a well-formed, offline, F-Droid-shipped arcade puzzle and the cheapest possible donor for a touch grid-puzzle prototype (1,347 lines of dependency-free JS, 45 animated tiles, seeded determinism), but it is a 2013 browser game in a thin wrapper, and its one thoughtful mechanic (aging/breaking pipes) is undocumented upstream. To turn the audit into a run: install platform android-30 (or bump `compileSdk`/`targetSdk` to 35), `./gradlew assembleDebug`, adb-install on `pm_pixel`, tap the preview bar to place a few pieces, let the timer run out and read back the saved high score. |

Audio counted: **17 `.wav` effects** (`bonus`, `countdown1/2`, `drifting`, `explosion`, …) plus **2 `.mp3` tracks** (`menu`, `victory`) in `musics/`, alongside `particles/`, `ui/`, `fonts/` and `screens/` asset dirs. Caveats, honestly: **nothing was built or played here**, so handling, drift feel, weapon balance and the 2-player split-screen are unverified; the assets in `android/assets` are **generated artifacts** built from the sparse `core/assets-src` tree (115 files: `fonts`, `maps`, `sounds`, `sprites`, `ui` + a `Makefile`), so a fresh clone cannot even reach `make` without ImageMagick and the Python requirements; the `ios/` module exists but was never inspected beyond its presence; and the clone is a single pinned commit with no upstream history to audit. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a cleanly modular, CI-backed Gradle project with a committed wrapper, a Docker build path, a `Makefile` asset pipeline and an explicit prerequisites list in `docs/building.md` — docked because the build cannot start on this host: Gradle 8.9 + JDK 27 is an unsupported pairing, JDK 11 is absent, and assets must be generated by ImageMagick/Make/Python before the game will run); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android is not an afterthought — minSdk 19, targetSdk 35, five staged ABIs including x86_64 for emulators, touch+HUD laid out for handsets, F-Droid/Play/itch releases, fastlane store metadata and mobile CI — docked because no APK was assembled, installed or seen); depth **2/3** (9 authored Tiled tracks with their own tilesets and rules sheets, 3 championships with three-tier time records giving a genuine skill ladder, 15 distinct vehicles with different handling profiles and a weapon/pickup layer is real arcade racing depth — docked because there is a single racing mode, no career progression beyond the championships, no online play, and 2-player split-screen is desktop-only); polish **2/2** (CC-BY-SA art with a deliberate three-licence split explained in the README, 16 complete translations, 17 effects plus menu/victory music, particle effects and skid/drift audio, a committed test module, CI with reproducible Docker builds, and a decade of shippable F-Droid/Play releases under the original author's hand). **Recommendation: keep — the catalog's best-built top-down racer and a clean CC-BY-SA car/track donor** for any arcade racer prototype; to turn the audit into a run: install JDK 11 (or 17 with a Gradle/AGP bump), `pip3 install -r requirements.txt`, `make assets && make packer`, then `./gradlew :android:assembleDebug`, adb-install on `pm_pixel`, and complete one championship race to confirm vehicle handling and weapon pickup behaviour. |

Localisation is genuine: **base 210 strings plus 7 translated locales** (de 205, es 204, it 202, pl 204, ro 205, id 190, tr 187). Caveats, honestly: **nothing was compiled, installed or played**, so plane placement, the deduction feel and the online round handshake are unverified; the Compose rewrite is the author's own “being now developed” work-in-progress; and 45 MB of the clone is duplicated Android sources (Kotlin + Compose + legacy Java) rather than content. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (three complete, internally consistent Gradle Android projects — one with a committed Gradle 9.6.1 wrapper, one on AGP 8.13.2 with kapt/Room wiring — and a 7-target CMake Qt desktop build with its own test project: a lot of real toolchain for a hobby game — docked because the README gives no build command, the legacy `kotlin/PlanesAndroid` module has **no wrapper at all**, and the one command that exists cannot resolve AGP 9.3.2 offline on this host); runs on phone **2.5/3** (Android is the primary target, not a port: F-Droid and Play Store releases under `com.planes.android`, minSdk 21 on the classic app and 26 on the Compose one, targetSdk 36/37 i.e. current, a touch-sized grid board with dedicated portrait and sw600dp tablet layouts, only networking permissions, four in-app tutorial videos — docked because no APK was assembled or adb-installed here and no APK is committed); depth/replay **1.5/3** (a clean deduction core — rotated-plane geometry, three AI hunt modes at a parameterised skill level, per-round statistics, plus real online multiplayer with accounts and chat is more than a toy, but it is one board game with one board size, no progression or unlocks, one opponent type, and the online half is dead without the author's server); polish **1.5/2** (8-language UI at ~200 strings each, a 12-chapter LaTeX technical book documenting the board, controller and computer strategy, 43 test files, four tutorial videos, three desktop front ends over one engine, MIT on code and content, and a decade-plus shipping record across desktop and two app stores — docked for zero audio, the unreferenced cat/dog photo dump, a test source set wired into `main`, and a README that never states a build command). **Recommendation: keep — the catalog's cleanest example of a *deduction-game* engine plus a genuinely mobile-first shipping discipline**; to turn the audit into a run: switch to JDK 17/21 (AGP 9.3.2 wants 17+ and Gradle 9 is happier away from JDK 27), `sh ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` in `kotlin/PlanesCompose`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, play one single-player round against the “find head” AI, then place a plane at 90° in board editing and confirm the rotated body is accepted. |
