**AGPL-3.0-only** (`package.json` `license` + `LICENSE`), author Julien Maulny, commit **`5750af4`, 2026-11-01** ("[Fixes #76] Allow cancel a mid-game by mutual agreement"); **30 MB** on disk (5.6 MB `assets/`, 12 MB `src/`); **no `android/` module, no APK, no iOS shell** — the phone lane is the PWA (`pwa-manifest.json`: `display: standalone`, `display_override: window-controls-overlay/minimal-ui`, theme `#DC3545`, `categories: [games, entertainment]`, IARC rating id, `related_applications: play → org.playhex.twa`) with a committed `service-worker.js` and VAPID web-push | **Desk audit — no build run; the documented chain cannot start inside the 90 s window on this host, so no frame, page load or finished game is claimed.** The README *does* document a real command line (`pnpm install` → `pnpm typeorm schema:sync` → `pnpm serve`, app on `:3000`), which is more than most rows here get, but every prerequisite was checked rather than assumed: **`pnpm` and `corepack` are both absent from PATH** (`which` finds neither; `corepack pnpm -v` → `No such file or directory`), the clone carries **zero `node_modules`**, so the tree needs a networked pnpm bootstrap plus the whole dependency graph before the first command, and the server will not boot without a **live MySQL/MariaDB >=12.1 or Postgres** (a `psql` client exists but no server is provisioned, and there is no `mysql`/`mariadb` binary at all) onto which **40 TypeORM migrations** run. That is a multi-minute install, not a 90 s build. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal — a Hex board is generated, never authored): the substance is engine, service and localisation, and it is large — **1,076 TypeScript/Vue files / 128,974 lines**: **`src/client` 51,903** (247 `.vue` single-file components), **`src/server` 39,791** (game server, ladder, tournaments, puzzles, ai-jobs, 40 migrations), **`src/shared` 27,108** (game engine `Board.ts`/`EngineGame.ts`, time-control including `ByoYomiChrono.ts`, the hex-game importer, SGF, swap-maps, position comparator) and **`src/packages` 10,172** (the reusable PIXI board renderer plus three published helper packages). Rules surface measured rather than guessed: **`MIN_BOARDSIZE = 1` … `MAX_BOARDSIZE = 53`** with **`BOARD_DEFAULT_SIZE = 11`** (`src/shared/game-engine/Board.ts:4`) and **`MOHEX_MAX_BOARDSIZE = 14`** for play/solve; **34 tournament + ladder source files** across `src/server/{tournaments,ladder}/`, **15 puzzle files**, and **309** `katahex / mohex / davies` references showing the AI lane is actually wired. Quality infrastructure is the best in this catalog's web tier: **88 unit-test files** (Mocha) and **31 Cypress e2e specs** — `play-game`, `watch-game`, `tournaments`, `tournament-series`, `ranked`, `puzzles`, `time-controls`, `clock-replay`, `chat`, `channel-chat`, `rematch`, `resign`, `pass`, `undo`, `challenge`, `lobby`, `myturn-notification`, `push-subscription-security` — over **21 locales** (ar, cs, da, de, en, es, fi, fr, hu, id, it, ja, ko, nl, pl, pt, ru, sv, ta, tr, zh-Hans) behind **1,682 English UI strings**, translated through Weblate. Audio and art: **48 sound files** (two alternative packs, `halloween/` and `lisp/`, each move/victory/berserk as mp3+ogg) and **39 images** (PWA logos at 192, maskable and monochrome, guide illustrations, external-service logos). Honest caveats: **nothing was installed, booted, adb-installed or played**; the PWA and Play-Store claim rests on the shipped manifest, service worker and Play listing referenced in code, not on a load here; and the TWA is a web wrapper, not a native Android app. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a coherent pnpm monorepo with a committed lockfile, versioned workspace packages, a documented three-command start, a real e2e harness and 40 migrations — docked hard because **`pnpm`/`corepack` are missing on this host, `node_modules` is empty**, and the schema step needs an external MySQL/MariaDB/Postgres server, so the tree cannot be stood up in a 90 s window); runs on phone **2.5/3** (mobile was the design target from day one: a responsive Vue/PIXI touch board, an installable standalone PWA with scope and launch handling, a service worker, **web push notifications**, and a published Google Play **TWA** under `org.playhex.twa` — docked for **no native Android/iOS project, no APK in-tree**, and no load or touch interaction exercised in this pass); depth/replay **2.5/3** (one game, but the deepest possible treatment of it: 1–53 board sizes, opening-balance swap rules, byo-yomi and absolute clocks, ranked ladder, tournaments *and* tournament series, puzzles, import from other servers, Mohex solving and Katahex analysis, replays, chat — docked because it is still a single abstract ruleset with no campaign, unlocks or content progression, and the strongest AI lanes need the separate `hex-ai-distributed` worker); polish **1.5/2** (129 k lines of typed, test-covered code, 31 e2e specs, 21 locales at ~1,700 strings, a Weblate pipeline, two sound packs, push, PWA chrome and a live production deployment — docked for **zero run verification here**, a heavy server footprint (DB + Redis + AI workers) for a board game, and an on-disk size that is code rather than content). **Recommendation: keep — the catalog's most complete web-service game and the only entry whose phone story is an installable PWA plus a Play-Store TWA rather than a port**; it is also the best free reference for a PIXI hex-grid renderer (`src/packages/pixi-board`) and for ranked-ladder and time-control design. To turn the audit into a run: `npm i -g pnpm`, provision MariaDB 12.1 or Postgres, `pnpm install && pnpm typeorm schema:sync && pnpm hex create-random-bots && pnpm serve`, open `localhost:3000` at a 390×844 viewport, start a bot game on 11×11 with 5+5 byo-yomi, play a move, and confirm the clock advances and the connection win is detected. |

Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a clean single-project Godot 4.7 tree that imported in 2.4 s and booted first try on a slightly newer engine than it declares, with six committed export presets, Flathub/Snap/Chocolatey manifests and fastlane metadata — docked because the README gives no build command and the Android lane needs editor-only templates plus a real package name, so nothing packaged); runs on phone **2.5/3** (mobile-first by construction: portrait-locked 1080×1920 viewport, `touch_primary` action, drag-back launch with a live trajectory line, arm32/arm64/x86 presets, immersive mode, ETC2/ASTC texture import, itch.io release — docked because no APK was assembled or adb-installed here and the preset's package id is still `com.game.$genname`); depth/replay **1.5/3** (a real skill loop — 11 merge tiers worth 0→5,120 points, elliptical central gravity that changes how shots bank, a 1.5 s grace window with a warning band, next-planet preview, persistent high score and mid-run save/resume — but it is **one mode, one board, no progression, unlocks, daily run, leaderboard or second ruleset**, so replay rests entirely on beating your own number); host is Node v26.12.1 / npm 11.20.0, and a networked `npm install` for an Expo 54 app plus `npx expo prebuild` is minutes, not seconds. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=33` (that was expo-router route files — there are only **4** — so the pack's level count is spurious): **72 TypeScript module files / 5,529 lines** across `pages` 24 files / 2,686, `entities` 16 / 1,344, `features` 10 / 644, `components` 9 / 460, `shared` 7 / 189 and `app` 4 route files / 93, the largest being **`pokemon-filter-bottom-sheet.tsx` 372**, `generation-bottom-sheet.tsx` 222, `evolution-page.tsx` 175, `stats-page.tsx` 172, `tcg-card-fullscreen-viewer.tsx` 167, `pokemon-list-context.tsx` 165, `pokemon-details-page.tsx` 164. **There is no local data at all**: the roster and stats come from a personal deployment, **`https://pokedex-mouzinho.leapcell.app`** (`entities/pokemon/api/pokemon-api.ts`), and cards from **`api.tcgdex.net/v2/en`** — a single-host backend, so the app is dead without that service; offline reads work only through react-query's AsyncStorage cache. Art is all UI, **78 PNG + 52 SVG = 130 images / 84 unique basenames**: an **18-type icon set drawn twice (PNG + SVG)**, **48 numbered files = 24 Pokémon sprite pairs** (the three starter lines plus a handful per generation), and a set of card patterns/gradients (card pattern, horizontal pattern, gradient pokeball, silhouette, search/sort/filter/heart icons) plus the adaptive launcher trio. Quality note that matters for reuse: the app bundles **5 `SF-Pro-Display` OTFs** (Bold/Light/Medium/Regular/Thin) required in `app/_layout.tsx`, i.e. **Apple's licensed typeface redistributed in a repo that claims MIT** — do not lift these into another product. Tests are real for what they are: **10 spec files** (pokemon-api, pokemon-mapper, pokemon-stats, tcg-card-api, three hook tests, one component plus snapshot) under a `jest-expo` preset; **`npm test` is `jest --watchAll`**, so it never exits in CI as written. Honest gaps: zero audio, zero gameplay, no localisation beyond `en-US`, no data-checked offline mode, and no `node_modules` or APK in the tree. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a modern, correctly-wired Expo 54 app with a committed lockfile, a documented start command and a real F-Droid CI workflow — docked because nothing installs offline here, `expo prebuild` plus keystore signing puts a run well past triage, and `jest --watchAll` is not a buildable test entry point); runs on phone **2.5/3** (genuinely phone-first, not a port: F-Droid release, portrait lock, edge-to-edge, adaptive plus monochrome icons, flash-list and expo-image for scroll performance, haptics, a touch-shaped bottom-sheet filter UI — docked for no APK assembled or adb-installed here and a runtime dependency on one author-hosted backend); depth/replay **0.5/3** (there is no game: no score, no AI, no rules, no progression — the entire interaction is browse, search, filter, read a stat block and page through a card image; the half point is only for the evolution-chain and stats presentation being structured rather than a text dump); polish **1.0/2** (clean FSD layering with public-API barrels, a coherent hand-drawn type-icon set in two formats, card patterns and gradients that read as a real design, 10 test files, fastlane screenshots and store metadata — docked for the **SF Pro licence defect**, `en-US`-only strings, no dark-mode-specific assets beyond the splash, no sound, and a README whose licence section contradicts its own `LICENSE` file). **Recommendation: not a candidate for the phone roster — keep only as a UI/asset donor and a good FSD-architecture reference.** It is honest work of the wrong kind: the reusable pieces are the 18-type two-format icon set, the card-pattern/gradient plates, the bottom-sheet filter interaction and the react-query persist setup; to turn the audit into a run it would need `npm install` on a networked host, `npx expo prebuild --platform android`, a Gradle `assembleRelease`, adb-install and a scroll through the list — which would still only prove a Pokédex opens. |

depth/replay **2/3** (six distinct modes with their own engines and score columns, 8×3 quiz matrix with 100 authored questions plus runtime-generated ones, XP/level leaderboard, persistent per-difficulty high scores, and a team builder with type-coverage math — well past a Pokédex; docked because each mode is a short arcade loop with **no campaign, unlock tree or daily/async competition**, the authored bank is only 100 questions, and the deepest mode (CardClash) sits behind the paid tier); polish **1.5/2** (a deliberate Pokéball-themed M3 design with glassmorphic cards, per-Pokémon particle effects driven by 4 Lottie files, 8 SFX including a bespoke "who's that Pokémon" sting, offline dex caching, animated transitions — docked for **placeholder-only tests**, 3 locales, a stale README badge link, and ads/subscription plumbing in the default store flavor). **Recommendation: keep as the strongest Pokémon-fan app in the roster and a reference for a multi-mode arcade hub** — the `GameScoreEntity` schema, the XP-event leaderboard and the flavor-split billing flags (`ENABLE_ADS`/`ENABLE_BILLING` off in `foss`) are directly liftable patterns; Mechanics confirmed in source, not from the README: the three powerup types are picked at random and re-rolled when the ball passes a spent pad (`GameStage:237`), the high score persists through libGDX `Preferences` (`GameStage:196-199`), and mute persists the same way (`MuteButton:70`). Breakdown: buildability **1/2** — a coherent three-module project with a committed wrapper, a real manifest, all five ABI native sets and a working desktop launcher written out, docked because the entire toolchain is eight years stale (jcenter, AGP 2.3.2, Gradle 4.4 against JDK 27, SDK 25 absent) and no build command is documented. Runs on phone **2.5/3** — a genuine handset product, not a shim: F-Droid release, portrait lock, touch-only input by design (the entire verb is a finger swipe), minSdk 8, multi-ABI natives, haptic feedback and a mute toggle; docked only because no APK was built or adb-installed here. Depth/replay **1.5/3** — a real skill loop with three powerup behaviours, cannons, obstacles, fans and a persisted best score, but one endless mode with no levels, unlocks, missions or authored difficulty curve. Polish **1.5/2** — hand-drawn art set with a custom bitmap font, one music track and nine distinct SFX, parallax sky, splash/tap/game-over screens, persistent mute and a 5.7 MB footprint; docked for zero tests, no optimisation notes and a licence (NPOSL-3.0) that is non-profit-oriented rather than plainly permissive. **Recommendation: keep as a compact reference for a one-swipe mobile physics arcade and as a small honest libGDX/Box2D sample — the line-as-trampoline contact routing in `MyContactListener` is the liftable part**; to turn the audit into a phone result: re-target the build to Gradle 8/9 + AGP 8 + `mavenCentral()` with `compileSdk 34`, `./gradlew android:assembleDebug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, then swipe one trampoline under the falling ball.

five piece types with genuinely different verbs — **President** ×1 (one step any direction, cannot capture anything, blocked by a Civil Servant's Aura, acts as a score multiplier), **Voter** ×4 (one step any direction), **Minister** ×4 (one step diagonally), **Delegate** ×4 (one step orthogonally), **Civil Servant** ×4 (immobile, radiates a one-square Aura that shuts the President out) — a win being 5 figures in an unbreakable row or a position the opponent can no longer answer, with scoring on the five valued squares (1, 22, 28, 82, 88) and the centre square **55** as an extra multiplier, max 100 points a board | *_5.5 — desk audit_* (buildability 0.5 · phone target 2 · depth/replay 2 · polish 1) | **Native Android, Java 11 + Gradle — a phone app by construction.** Single-module project, `applicationId/namespace eu.veldsoft.politrics`, `compileSdk 33 / minSdk 25 / targetSdk 33`, `versionCode 2 / versionName 1.0.1`, four activities (`SplashActivity` with a 5 s timeout redirect, landscape-locked `GameActivity`, `HelpActivity`, `AboutActivity`), only the `INTERNET` permission, deps appcompat 1.6.1 / material 1.9.0 / constraintlayout 2.1.4 plus JUnit 4.13.2 and Espresso 3.5.1; the wrapper cannot start at all on this clone, so no APK, frame or played move is claimed.** Probed rather than assumed: `sh gradlew --version` dies immediately with **`Error: Unable to access jarfile …/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar`** — the `gradlew` script shipped but the whole **`gradle/` directory did not**, so the wrapper has no jar and no `gradle-wrapper.properties` (distribution version therefore unknowable); there is **no system `gradle` on PATH**, the root `build.gradle` pins **AGP 9.2.1** (a Gradle 9 setup that would have to be fetched from the network anyway), and `compileSdk 33` is **not installed** — `~/Android/Sdk/platforms` holds only **android-35, 36, 37.0**. **The README documents no build command** — it is rules text end to end (play, capture and scoring examples with worked positions), which is the most complete ruleset text any row in this catalog has carried. Content counted directly rather than trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal — the board is a fixed 9×9 with no authored level files): the entire game is **14 Java files / 1,891 lines**, dominated by **`model/Board.java` 967** and **`GameActivity.java` 487**; `Board` is a real rules engine, not a stub — `SIZE = 9`, `PIECES = 17`, per-side pile/figure/aura/line-up matrices, `evaluate(i,j,dx,dy,five)` line scoring, `score(Enemy,five)`, `maxScoreWithFiveInRow`, `maxScoreWhithoutFiveInRow`, `hasMoreThanFour`, `hasAlivePresident`, `allFiguresBlocked`, `spreadAura`, and the move pipeline `figurePlaceClicked` / `figureMoveClicked` / `figureCaptureClicked` plus `winner()`. Figure verbs are real classes (`Figure` 127 lines as the base, `Voter` / `Minister` / `Delegate` / `President` / `Servant` as small subclasses), and `Enemy` is nothing but the two player identities plus the violet/orange palette — **there is no AI of any kind**: a grep for `computer|artificial|minimax|alphaBeta|bot` across `app/src/main/java` returns **zero hits**, and the menu is only `new_game`, `help`, `about`, so play is strictly **two-player hotseat**. Art is one hand-made set: **16 PNGs** — 12 board/figure PNGs at **`drawable-hdpi` only** (`board`, `aura`, and violet/orange pairs for president, voter, minister, delegate, servant) plus 4 `ic_launcher` densities — **zero audio** (no `.mp3`/`.ogg`/`.wav` anywhere), **no `res/raw`**, and the single asset is `assets/banner.html`. Localisation is effectively absent: **11 strings**, English-only, and the code carries a literal `// TODO Use string resources.` for the one toast it shows. Honest caveats: **nothing was compiled, installed or played**; the three fastlane screenshots are the **vendor's** captures, not ours — one shows a real 9×9 board mid-setup with both line-ups filled and the violet/orange pieces in place, one an About screen — so the game demonstrably *exists and renders on a handset*, but no interaction was exercised here, and the tests are the two empty JUnit/Espresso template stubs. Breakdown: buildability **0.5/2** (a small, coherent, current-ish Android module with a real rules engine and clean model/UI separation — docked hard because **the checked-in wrapper is broken: no `gradle/` directory at all**, no system Gradle, AGP 9.2.1 unreachable offline, SDK 33 missing, and no build command documented, so nothing could even begin compiling); runs on phone **2/3** (an honest native Android board game, not a port: landscape-locked board activity, tap-a-figure-then-tap-a-square input, minSdk 25, a splash/help/about shell, published store metadata and vendor screenshots of it running on a device — docked because **no APK was produced or adb-installed in this pass**, so the phone claim rests on their screenshots rather than ours); depth/replay **2/3** (genuinely deep for a 1.9 k-line project: asymmetric piece verbs, a placement phase before movement, a blocking Aura that makes the President's multiplier positional, jump-capture rules, a five-in-a-row / dead-President / all-figures-blocked win detection branch, and a multiplier scoring system with worked examples in the README — docked because it is **hotseat-only with no opponent AI, no undo, no hint, no difficulty, and no persistence**: the app never accumulates the multi-board total the README describes, and every session starts from `reset()`); polish **1/2** (a distinctive design choice worth crediting — heraldic figure art on geometric base shapes, colour-coded violet vs orange sets, a splash screen, in-game help and about screens, and a full store page — docked for **one art density only, zero sound, 11 unextracted strings with an in-code TODO, English-only, and a UI that is a fixed grid of ImageViews rather than an adaptive layout**). **Recommendation: keep as the catalog's cleanest example of a real rules engine behind a thin Android shell** — `Board.java` is a directly liftable reference for a placement-then-move abstract game with aura/blocking and multiplier scoring, and the README's scoring examples are worth keeping as a spec; but it is a two-player-only board, so it is a donor, not a phone-roster title. To turn the audit into a phone result: restore the wrapper (`gradle wrapper --gradle-version 9.3`, or drop in `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar` + properties), install `platforms;android-33` or bump `compileSdk` to 35, run `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, then place all 17 dark figures, answer with light, and make one `V33-35` capture to confirm the score readout updates. |

Depth is the honest limiter: `grep -niE 'minimax|alpha.?beta|computer|opponent|bot'` across `src/` returns **zero hits** — there is **no AI of any kind**, and the README states “Multiplayer is planned”, so play is **hotseat-only**; the win check exists four times over (rows, columns, both diagonals, `times >= win_condition - 1`) and turn order is `(player_to_move + 1) % (players_n + 1)` with `players_n_max = 4`, so the 2–4-player recursive boards are genuine. Fastlane carries **two locales (en-US, ru)**. The author is candid about the codebase — “contains many parts with code of questionable quality… I will rewrite it when I have time” — and the 1.3 changelog is exactly three items: the SDL3 port, the RmlUI replacement of the legacy GUI, and player colours. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a genuinely portable single-target CMake tree with SDL3 vendored in-tree and a real Android Gradle project — docked because the three SDL addon submodules are uninitialized in this clone, RmlUI is fetched from an unpinned `master`, and the compile is minutes of SDL/RmlUI/FreeType, so nothing was configured or linked); runs on phone **2.5/3** (not a port bolted on: a live F-Droid listing under `org.kirkezz.rttt`, the APK built from the in-tree SDL project with a real `applicationId`, a touch-sized RML menu → new-game → game → back flow, and three committed phone screenshots — docked because no APK was assembled or adb-installed in this pass, so the touch claim rests on the vendor's captures); depth/replay **1.5/3** (recursive sub-board play with per-game **width, height, win-condition and 2–4-player** configuration is a real rules engine, well past plain tic-tac-toe — docked hard because it is **hotseat-only: no AI, no online, no difficulty, no undo, no persistence, no scoring meta**, and the recursion is fixed at one extra level); polish **1/2** (a clean RmlUI front end with menu/settings/new-game/in-game screens, four distinct player marks plus a colour setting, an icon set, GPL-3.0 and bilingual F-Droid metadata — docked for **zero audio, no tests, English/Russian-only strings, a bare two-widget settings screen, and a dependency graph that cannot build without the network**). **Recommendation: keep as the catalog's smallest clean *recursive-rules* reference — `RTTT_Engine.hpp` is a directly liftable recursive-board model where sub-board ownership feeds the parent grid, and the vendored SDL3 + RmlUI layout is a compact template for shipping one C++ game to desktop and Android**; it is donor-grade rules code, not a roster title, until it grows an opponent. To turn the audit into a phone result: `git submodule update --init` (SDL_image/mixer/ttf), pin RmlUI to a tag instead of `master`, `cmake -S . -B build && cmake --build build -j16` to confirm the desktop loop, then `cd SDL/android-project && ./gradlew assembleDebug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, and play one 3×3 recursive game at 3 players, confirming that taking a sub-board claims its outer square. |

| 168 | **ReGeX** (`ReGeX`) | **Puzzle / code-golf brain-teaser — "write a regular expression that solves this"**: the board shows two columns of strings — one list that MUST all match your pattern and one that MUST all fail it — and you type a regex into a single-line box; each keystroke recompiles the pattern and live-marks every word as matching or not, and when the split is clean you advance and the difficulty ratchets up. Four modes share that core: Intro (7 hand-authored lessons), Simple Match (a randomly generated hidden regex must be reverse-engineered), Mutate Match (one string mutated by character swaps/substitutions, so you must find the invariant), and Word Match (the corpus is an 8,711-word English dictionary instead of generated strings) | *_6.5 — desk audit_* (buildability 1 · phone target 2.5 · depth/replay 1.5 · polish 1.5) | **Android-native Java, single module, hand-rolled Views — no engine, no game framework, 1,224 lines of game code in 16 files**: app id `com.phikal.regex`, **minSdk 16 / compileSdk 28 / targetSdk 28**, `buildToolsVersion "29.0.3"`, **AGP 4.0.1 resolved from `google()` + `jcenter()`** (jcenter is shut down) on a committed **`gradle-6.4.1-all`** wrapper, `versionCode 5 / versionName "1.3.1"`, Java 8 source/target, single dependency **`androidx.core:core:1.2.0`**; manifest declares **only `VIBRATE`** (no INTERNET — fully offline), two activities (`GameActivity` is the launcher, `SettingsActivity` an AppCompat dialog), `allowBackup=true`; **GPL-3.0** (`LICENSE`), author Philip Kaludercic, commit **`b496faa`, 2022-02-21, "Merge pull request #52 from andmizyk/master"**; **632 KB** on disk; shipped on **F-Droid** (`com.phikal.regex`) | **Desk audit — no build run; the wrapper build was attempted and never reached a compiler, so no APK, frame or solved regex is claimed.** `timeout 90 sh ./gradlew --offline :app:assembleDebug` ignored `--offline` for the distribution itself and spent the whole window **downloading `https://services.gradle.org/distributions/gradle-6.4.1-all.zip`**, reaching ~60% before `timeout` killed it (exit 124) — so **gradle-6.4.1 is not in `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists`** (5.4.1, 5.5, 6.1.1, 6.5, 6.7.1, 7.2 and 8.x→9.8.2 are). `gradlew` also arrives **without the exec bit** (plain `./gradlew` = Permission denied; must run via `sh`). Three further blockers stand behind that download: **platform `android-28`** and **build-tools `29.0.3`** are both absent (`~/Android/Sdk/platforms` = 35, 36, 37.0; `build-tools` = 34/35/36), and even with them **Gradle 6.4.1 cannot run on this host's JDK 27** (`/usr/lib/jvm` holds only `java-27-openjdk`; 6.4.1 predates JDK 16), with **AGP 4.0.1 reachable only via the retired jcenter** as well. **The README documents no build command at all** — 12 lines, three of them bare link definitions, pointing at the GitHub wiki, the F-Droid listing and the GPL text. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal — every board is generated at runtime): the substance is a **procedural regex grammar plus a real difficulty ladder**, and it is compact and honest. **RegularExpression.java (216 lines)** is a recursive generator with five node types — `LiteralRE`, `EmptyRE`, `ConcatRE`, `AlterRE`, `MultipleRE` (`MAX_REPET = 8`) — built by `produceRE(double diff)`, whose branching factor itself shrinks with skill (`rnd.nextInt(4 + (int) floor(4 * (1 - diff)))`), drawing literals from a shuffled 37-character alphabet (`_`, a–z, A, 0–9) over a **`SecureRandom`**; each node can also emit a near-miss string (`produceOther(diff)`), which is what makes Simple and Mutate solvable by elimination rather than luck. Difficulty is **persisted per mode and adaptive, not scripted**: `Progress` stores `difficulty` and `rounds` in `SharedPreferences` under `<MODE>_progress` / `<MODE>_count`, and each solve adds `factor * 0.01 * pow(1/1.01, rounds)` — a decaying-curve gain that makes round 1 a big jump and round 50 a small one, an unusually deliberate curve for a hobby puzzle app. The pattern box is capped by skill too: **max pattern length = `(0.8 * diff^1.5 + 0.2) * 24`** characters, so the hard levels are genuine code golf (a 24-char ceiling at diff 0). Validation is real `java.util.regex.Pattern.compile` per keystroke with `PatternSyntaxException` mapped to an error state, and the win test is a strict XOR over every word (`allMatch &= match ^ !w.match`). Content data: **`res/raw/words.gz` = 8,711 words / 26 KB gzip**, length-skewed toward 7–8 characters (2,467 seven-letter, 2,493 eight-letter, 143 three-letter), loaded once and `Collections.shuffle`d so Word Match never repeats an order. **Intro mode is a real 7-lesson curriculum** read from source comments: basic matching → char classes and case → `?` → `.` → `*` → `[..]*` → alternation/groups, each with paired match and no-match sets and a stored solution. Structure and honesty gaps worth recording: `Game` exposes **4 modes but `strings.xml` names 6** (`reverse_match`, `reduced_match` are dead strings), **`games/extract/SimpleExtractTask.java` is an empty class body** — an extract-a-substring mode declared and never written — and `MultipleRE.produceOther` **returns `null`**, the obvious null-path bug in the near-miss generator. UI is four XML layouts (`main_layout`, `column_layout`, `word_layout`, `activity_settings`) driven by `ArrayAdapter`s over `Column`/`Word`, plus an optional on-screen `Util.CHARS` regex-symbol bar (`[ ] ( ) . * + ? ^ | { } - \\`) toggled in settings. Styling is the best thing here: a bundled **Solarized palette** (`solarized.xml`, all 16 base and accent colours, by the author) with `android:typeface="monospace"` on both themes — the app deliberately looks like a terminal. Feedback is **zero audio files** (0 `.mp3`/`.ogg`/`.wav` in the tree); a correct solve optionally plays the **system notification ringtone** plus a **250 ms vibration**, gated by a `notif` preference that defaults to **off**. Art is **9 PNGs, all launcher/settings icons** (4 mipmap densities plus `ic_launcher-web.png`), with one vector settings glyph. Localisation is real: **10 translated locales** (ca, da, de, eo, fr, hu, it, ja, pt, uk) against a **26-string** base — note `values-uk` is complete (26) while the others stop at 15–16, i.e. the shell was translated but later-added settings strings were not. Tests: **none** — no `src/test`, no `src/androidTest`, no `.github`, no CI. Breakdown: buildability **1/2** (a tiny, internally consistent one-module Gradle project with a committed wrapper and exactly one dependency, which would be a five-minute build with a period JDK — docked because nothing can start here: the 6.4.1 distribution must download and did not finish in 90 s, SDK 28 and build-tools 29.0.3 are missing, Gradle 6.4.1 + JDK 27 is an unsupported pairing, AGP 4.0.1 lives on dead jcenter, `gradlew` is not executable, and the README never states an assemble command); runs on phone **2.5/3** (a genuine handset product, not a port: live F-Droid listing, minSdk 16 so it installs almost anywhere, a one-thumb portrait layout of two word columns plus one text field, an optional symbol bar so a regex keyboard is never needed, and `configChanges="orientation|screenSize"` handling — docked because nothing was adb-installed, `targetSdk 28` is years behind policy, there is no tablet or landscape layout, and no in-tree screenshot exists); depth/replay **1.5/3** (a real generator with five composable node types, a persisted adaptive difficulty curve, per-keystroke live validation, the `^` XOR invariant test, and a 7-lesson onboarding ladder is more thought than most puzzle clones get — docked hard because all four modes are **the same match task with a different word source**, the extract mode is an empty file, two advertised modes are unimplemented strings, and there is **no score, no leaderboard, no streak, no daily puzzle** — progress is one ever-growing difficulty float per mode); polish **1/2** (the Solarized monospace theme, 10 locales, GPL-3.0 with the full text, and a 2022 commit history shipped on F-Droid make a coherent, tasteful package — docked for **zero sound**, icon-only art, no tests or CI, a dead-string/dead-class pair still in-tree, a `null`-returning near-miss generator, and a README that is a link list rather than documentation). **Recommendation: keep as a curiosity and a donor, not a roster title — it is the roster's only code-as-gameplay puzzle, and the liftable piece is `RegularExpression.java`: a 216-line recursive pattern generator with matched near-miss strings and a difficulty-scaled grammar that would port to any prototype teaching pattern or language rules.** To turn the audit into a phone result: install a JDK 11/17, `sdkmanager "platforms;android-28" "build-tools;29.0.3"`, bump AGP to 7.x and the repositories to `mavenCentral()` to drop jcenter, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, then solve one Simple Match and one Intro lesson and confirm the difficulty float survives a full app restart. |

Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0` (literal — every exercise is generated at runtime): **26 Java/Kotlin source files** across the activity/game packages, **23 XML layouts**, **6 translated locales** beyond base (`values-de/el/fr/in/ru/tr`, plus `values-v21` and `values-w820dp`), **89 base strings**, **53 PNGs** in `res/` (drawer/toolbar/material icon set plus the PFA logo). The generator is small but deliberate: `gameInstance.getUpperBound()`/`getLowerBound()` map `space` 0–3 onto 10/100/1,000/10,000 and their decade floors; `randomOperator()` picks from whichever operator flags the player left enabled (MainActivity refuses to disable the **last** one); addition draws `x` then `y` inside the remaining headroom, subtraction keeps `y ≤ x`, multiplication picks a factor then a second ≤ `bound/x` to avoid overflow, and division builds a divisor list from the real divisors of `x` then divides — so subtraction never goes negative and division never yields a fraction, the two things that break drill apps. Progression and persistence are real, not a bare quiz: highscores are stored per **`hsscore`+operator+space** with `rightadd`/`wrongadd` counters (ResultActivity), finished rounds are written as rows into a `SAVED_EXERCISES` SQLite table with `(operator1, operator2, operand, space)` and replayed by a pause/resume path read back through `exerciseInstance.solve()`, and the app ships a **screen-scale backup/restore** (`BackupCreator.kt` / `BackupRestorer.kt` / `PFABackupService.kt`) wired to the Privacy Friendly Backup app — unusual plumbing for an arithmetic trainer. Also present: `TutorialActivity` + `HelpActivity`/`HelpDataDump`, a `SplashActivity`, and a `ScoreActivity`/`ScoreFragment` history screen inside a Material drawer shell. Honest limits: **zero audio** (0 `.mp3`/`.ogg`/`.wav` in the tree), no art beyond icons, **no tests except two generated placeholders** (`ExampleUnitTest`, `ApplicationTest`), no CI, and the arithmetic is the whole game — 4 operators × 4 magnitudes, fixed 10 rounds, no adaptive difficulty, no campaign, no achievements beyond the high-score table. The manifest declares **no permissions at all**, so it is fully offline by construction. To turn the audit into a phone result: install a **JDK 17** (or bump AGP/Gradle to a pair that matches 27), `sdkmanager "platforms;android-34"`, `./gradlew assembleDebug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, then start a 0–100 round with + and × only and confirm one correct answer scores `(1 + (100−t)/100) × 1` and that the high score survives an app restart. |

That makes this a **tier-B-shaped row: free source, content elsewhere** — the pack's `maps/levels=0` and `images=11` are literal and correct here, not a counting miss. `bin/` is **not** a prebuilt game either: 27 MB `amd64` + 13 MB `x86` + 804 KB `arm64`, but the contents are **Windows DLLs** (`SDL2.dll`, `OpenAL32.dll`, `freetype.dll`, `libpng16-16.dll`, `libwebp-4.dll`, `discord-rpc.dll`, `libsteam_api.so`/`.dylib`) plus **17 third-party license texts** (`LICENSE.SDL`, `.openalsoft`, `.opus`, `.ogg-vorbis`, `.webp`, `.avif`, `.dav1d`, `.vpx`, `.tiff`, `.steam` …) — a vendor-SDK drop, with **no `redeclipse` executable in the tree**. Code counted directly (the substance that *is* here): **359 `.cpp`/`.h` files, 119,946 `.cpp` + 152,386 `.h` = 272,332 lines**, of which the gameplay module is **34,746 `.cpp` lines over 34 files** in `src/game/` and the engine another ~3.4 MB under `src/engine/` (octree world + `octaedit.cpp` cooperative editor, Tesseract renderer, `mcube`/`material`/`grass`/`dynlight`/`lensflare`/`lightning`), with **`enet` vendored in-tree** for networking. Gameplay depth is real and enumerable: **7 gamemode constants** — `G_DEATHMATCH`, `G_CAPTURE`, `G_DEFEND`, `G_BOMBER`, `G_SPEEDRUN`, `G_EDITING`, `G_DEMO` (plus a duel mutator in `duelmut.h`) — each with its own `.cpp` (`bomber.cpp`, `capture.cpp`, `defend.cpp`, `physics.cpp`, `weapons.cpp`, `waypoint.cpp`, `scoreboard.cpp`); **18 weapons** declared in one macro row in `weapdef.h` — `claw, pistol, sword, shotgun, smg, flamer, plasma, zapper, rifle, corroder, trident, grenade, mine, rocket, minigun, jetsaw, eclipse, melee` — and *every one* of them gets a generated CubeScript variable table (`WPVAR` for the single-tier set, `WPVARM` for the two-tier `##1`/`##2` set) covering damage/recoil/spread/speed/lifetime per weapon, which is why the tuning surface is thousands of live vars rather than a config file; plus **3,066 lines of bot AI** (`ai.cpp` 2,507 + `aiman.h` 559). Shipped UI/feel tuning is data-in-config, not code: **`config/usage.cfg` 1,926 lines**, **196 files under `config/ui/`**, **27 GLSL shaders**, **41 `config/fx/` particle configs**, 124 KB `comp/` and 28 KB `map/`, 2 announcer configs. Documentation is unusually good for this roster: **23 files in `doc/`** including `cubescript-reference.md` (403 lines), `engine-systems.md` (309), `shader-reference.md`, `movie-recording.md`, `scripted-input.md`, man pages, `readme_sauerbraten.txt`/`readme_tesseract.txt` inheritance notes, and the licence set. **Licence split, worth quoting:** code is **ZLIB** (`doc/license.txt`, Red Eclipse © 2009-2025 Reeves/Salzman/Błauciak on Tesseract + Cube Engine 2) while the art is **CC-BY-SA** (`doc/cc-by-sa.txt`, `doc/all-licenses.txt`) — so "open source" is true of the engine and true-but-attribution-bound for the assets; the pack's `license: none found` is a root-file artifact (no `LICENSE`/`COPYING` at top level; the licence lives in `doc/`). Build toolchain is a hand-written **`src/Makefile`** (no CMake for the game itself — `src/CMakeLists.txt` is the msvc/docker path), `CXXFLAGS -O3 -std=c++17 -fno-exceptions -fno-rtti`, `pkg-config sdl2 SDL2_image openal sndfile zlib gl x11`, building `redeclipse`, `redeclipse_server` and a standalone `ctfont`, with a `PLATFORM_BIN` switch that already resolves `arm64`. **No build was attempted and none is claimed**: the README documents **no assemble command at all** (it is player-facing — "download at redeclipse.net", "primarily distributed via Steam", one link to the web Install Guide), and a 272 k-line C++ engine plus 196 UI cfgs is a multi-minute compile, far outside the 90 s triage window. CI is absent: `.github/` carries only `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `CODEOWNERS`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `FUNDING.yml`, `ISSUE_TEMPLATE.md`, `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, `SUPPORT.md` — **no workflows**. Tests are three token units: `src/tests/{blendslice,numberformat,slotmanager}.cpp`. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a real, dependency-modest, self-contained Makefile with enet vendored, a server target, and an arm64 branch already present — docked because the README states no build command, 272 k C++ lines cannot be verified in triage, and **a successful build is still unplayable without the 39 asset submodules**); runs on phone **0.5/3** (docked to the floor: **no Android target of any kind** — no manifest, no gradle, no NDK path, no touch or landscape activity, and upstream's own platforms are Windows/Linux/Steam; the `bin/arm64` directory is a *Linux desktop* ARM build, not a phone port. The 0.5 recognises that the tree carries a clean portable SDL2/GL abstraction and an arm64 build branch, so a future port is not absurd); depth/replay **3.0/3** (the roster's only genuine AAA-scale FPS design, and it earns full marks on the code alone: 7 distinct objective modes incl. bomb-defusal, CTF and base defence; 18 weapons each with a complete two-tier stat table; 3,066 lines of bot AI for offline play; parkour movement shipped as core physics rather than a mod; a **speedrun mode**; and a **cooperative in-engine realtime map editor** that makes the game its own content pipeline — nothing else cloned here comes close); polish **1.5/2** (a 272 k-line engine with 27 shaders, 41 FX configs, 196 UI screens and documented CubeScript/shader/engine references is polished software — docked because the polish that makes it a *product* — assets, maps, audio — lives entirely outside this clone, and there is no CI and only 3 token unit tests). **Recommendation: keep as the catalog's FPS/engine anchor and as the tier-B example to read before promising a playable clone — `redeclipse/base` is source-only, and "clone Red Eclipse" means recursing 39 submodules and pulling multiple GB of CC-BY-SA art.** The liftable parts are `src/game/weapdef.h` (one macro that generates a whole per-weapon tuning table) and `ai.cpp`/`aiman.h` (3 k lines of waypoint-driven bot movement, usable in any 3D prototype). To turn the audit into a real result: `git submodule update --init --depth 1` the 39 data repos (GBs — put them on the shield, not the repo tree), install the `sdl2 SDL2_image openal sndfile` dev packages, `make -C src -j16`, then run `./redeclipse` under `xvfb-run` and confirm the main menu + one bot deathmatch; a phone result would additionally need an SDL3/GLES Android port and a touch HUD, neither of which exists upstream. |

| 172 | **Die Simulator** (`Die-Simulator`) | **Single-die roller — a one-button dice toy, not a game**: tap **Roll** and a blue square dice face with canvas-drawn white pips settles on a random 1–6; holding the button re-rolls every 0.1 s for as long as you press, so the only mechanic is "hold for suspense". No score, no history, no multi-dice, no colour options — the README and the F-Droid description both list those as *planned*, not shipped | *_5.0 — desk audit_* (buildability 1.5 · phone target 2 · depth/replay 0.5 · polish 1) | **Android — a real phone product built with Buildozer/python-for-android, not a port**: `buildozer.spec` targets `android.api = 33` for both **`arm64-v8a` and `armeabi-v7a`**, `orientation = portrait`, `android.numeric_version = 3`, `requirements = python3,kivy`, package `com.andreiapps.diesimu`; the manifest declares **no permissions at all** (the `android.permissions` line is commented out), so it is fully offline by construction. Shipped on **F-Droid** (`com.andreiapps.diesimu`) with fastlane metadata in-tree. Kivy is cross-platform, so `python main.py` also runs on desktop | **Desk audit — no build run; Kivy is not installed on this host, so nothing was rendered, rolled or adb-installed.** Probed rather than assumed: `python3 -c 'import kivy'` → **`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'kivy'`**, and the documented path needs a pip install of Kivy (plus SDL2 wheels) before `python main.py` can open a window — that install plus a windowed launch is outside the 90 s triage window, and `buildozer android debug` is a python-for-android NDK/cross-compile job measured in tens of minutes, so it was not attempted. **The README does document both commands** (`pip install kivy` → `python main.py`, and `buildozer android debug` / `buildozer ios debug`), which is more than most rows here carry. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0 images=2` (both literal, not a counting miss): the whole application is **one file, `main.py`, 74 lines / 3,052 bytes**, and the clone is **19 tracked files** — no `data/`, no `assets/`, **0 audio, 0 levels, 0 models, 0 tests, no `.github/`, no CI**. Each face is a 3×3 `0/1` matrix literal selected by a `match` statement over `random.randint(1, 6)` and drawn as a `Color`/`Rectangle` plus `Ellipse` pips into a single `Widget.canvas`, with the die hard-coded to `die_size = 400`, `dot_size = 80`, `dist = 40` **in raw pixels** — no `dp()` scaling, so on a high-DPI handset the board is a small square in the upper half rather than a full-bleed die. The roll animation is `Clock.schedule_interval(self.roll_step, 0.1)` that unschedules after **5 ticks** unless the button is still held — the entire state machine is `self.count`. It also calls `kivy.require(kivy.__version__)`, which pins nothing and will not stop a future Kivy major. Art/audio counted: **2 PNGs in the tree**, both the same `fastlane/.../icon.png` for en-US and ro-RO — no launcher asset is configured in the spec, so the icon and presplash fall back to Buildozer defaults. Localisation is metadata-only, **3 locales** (`en-US` full, `ro-RO` full, `ru-RU` full_description + short_description but **no changelogs**), and the in-app strings — the single `'Roll'` label and the window title — are **English-only**. Licence is the full **GPL-3.0** text (`LICENSE`), author AndreiApps, head **`bb4848e`, 2026-04-11, "Update README.md"**; the clone is shallow (1 commit), so history is not in the tree; **268 KB** on disk. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** — a genuinely minimal, single-module Buildozer project that any Python dev can read end-to-end in a minute, with both documented commands and both Android ABIs declared; docked because **Kivy is absent here so neither command was exercised**, `requirements` pins no Kivy or Python version, there is no pip lockfile or CI, and the Android lane is a python-for-android cross-compile that can never fit a triage window. Runs on phone **2/3** — this is phone-first rather than a desktop port (portrait orientation, API 33, both ABIs, an F-Droid listing under its own package id, zero permissions); docked because **no APK was assembled or installed in this pass**, and the fixed-pixel canvas means the layout does not actually adapt to the screen it was built for. Depth/replay **0.5/3** — one die, one button, one uniform random draw: there is no scoring, no streaks, no roll history, no statistics, no second die, no tabletop-helper output (e.g. 4d6-drop-lowest), no settings of any kind; the hold-to-roll delay is the only expressiveness, and the three features that would make it useful at a table are precisely the ones still listed as "planned". Polish **1/2** — the pips are drawn rather than blitted (the changelog's own improvement), the window is a 50/50 die-to-button split, GPL-3.0 ships in full and F-Droid metadata is translated twice over; docked for **zero audio and zero haptics** (the one place a die app wants a click), no `dp()` scaling, icon-only visuals with no custom launcher art configured, no tests, no CI, and in-app strings that stayed English while the store listing was translated. **Recommendation: do not keep as a roster title — it is the shallowest thing cloned so far and, at 74 lines, closer to a Kivy hello-world than a game; keep it only as the catalog's smallest complete *Buildozer* reference** (a real `buildozer.spec` with API 33, two ABIs, portrait and no permissions, plus the `Clock.schedule_interval` + `canvas` idiom). The liftable part is the pips-as-matrix draw: a 3×3 mask over a 6-case `match`, which generalises to any dice, domino or card-face renderer in a prototype. To turn the audit into a real result: `pip install kivy`, `python main.py` under `xvfb-run` and confirm 20 rolls produce all six faces, then `buildozer android debug`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, and hold the Roll button to confirm the animation runs past 5 ticks while pressed and stops on release. |
| 173 | **Ricochlime** (`Ricochlime`) | **Turn-based ricochet shooter — the "calm alternative" arcade game**: aim and fire a perfectly elastic projectile (physics `restitution: 1`) off the walls and monsters, then every shot costs a round and monsters advance a row — 8 slots per row, ~30 % occupancy with a guaranteed minimum, monster HP set to the current score, one monster per row drops a **bullet** and one drops a **coin** (`KillReward`), so your ammo pool is `1 + score − bullet-reward monsters`; new rows per round grows every 50 points (`getNumNewRowsEachRound`: 1 row until 50, then 2, then 3 …), the run ends when the wall of monsters reaches the player, and coins buy bullet shapes/colours in a shop between runs | *_7.5 — desk audit_* (buildability 1.5 · phone target 2.5 · depth/replay 2 · polish 1.5) | **Android + iOS + Web PWA + Linux/macOS/Windows + Flatpak + Snap — a genuine cross-platform Flutter release, phone-first**: `applicationId com.adilhanney.ricochlime`, Android `minSdk`/`targetSdk` from `flutter.minSdkVersion`/`flutter.targetSdkVersion` (no pins), shipped on **Google Play, F-Droid and the Amazon Appstore** (all three badges in the README, live links), an in-tree **PWA** build target and `flatpak/`, `snap/`, `installers/` packaging directories; the manifest declares only **`android.permission.INTERNET`** (IAP/web links) and no touch-specific hardware, and `lib/ads/_iap_foss.dart` lets FOSS builds stub out in-app purchase via `patches/foss.sh` | **Desk audit — no build run; Flutter is not installed on this host, so nothing was compiled, launched or adb-installed.** Probed rather than assumed: `command -v flutter` returns nothing, and the project demands **Flutter ≥ 3.38.0 / Dart ≥ 3.10.0**, so even a `flutter pub get` is impossible here without a multi-hundred-MB SDK install — well outside the 90 s triage window — and `flutter build apk` (the documented Android command) would need the Android toolchain *plus* that SDK. **The README does document the build**, in a separate `BUILD.md`: prerequisites (Flutter stable, Android SDK/JDK, platform dev packages), a **`patches/` step** (a `foss.sh` variant that disables ads/IAP for F-Droid), and the command itself — `flutter build linux` / `flutter build apk`. Content counted directly instead of trusting the pack's `maps/levels=0 images=116 code=24` (levels are literal — the level *is* the procedurally spawned monster grid): **43 Dart files under `lib/`, 5,847 lines**, 55 Dart files repo-wide with **6 real test files** in `test/` — `new_row_test.dart`, `slime_priority_test.dart`, `bullet_speeds_test.dart`, `bg_skulls_test.dart`, `game_golden_test.dart` (golden-image regression), `version_test.dart` — and **8 CI workflows** (`.github/workflows/`: android, ios, linux, macos, web, windows, test, plus `check-flutter-submodule.yml`). Structure is a clean Flame/Forge2D game: `lib/flame/` (game loop, `ricochlime_game.dart`, `game_data.dart` save/restore), `flame/components/` (player, bullet, monster, walls, health bar, aim guide, tiled background), `lib/pages/` **8 screens** (home, play, shop, settings, tutorial, game_over, restart_game, loading_page), `lib/nes/` **6 files** theming the whole UI as an **NES console** (custom palette, icon font, coin counter, bouncing icon), plus `i18n/`, `utils/`, `ads/`. Localisation is real, not nominal: `slang` YAML sources in **4 languages — en, es, kk (Kazakh), ru — 59 keys each** (236 lines total) with generated `strings_*.g.dart` per locale. Shop economy counted: **20 purchasable items** — 12 bullet colours (white and black free, 10 at **200 coins** each) and 8 bullet shapes (circle free, 7 at **1,000 coins** each), with ownership persisted through `shared_preferences` (`stow_plain`/`stow_codecs`) and a negative price meaning "owned by default". Art/audio counted honestly: **41 files under `assets/`**, **27 images** (icon, monster/skull sprites, world and props atlases, `bullet_shapes.png`), **2 audio files** — one BGM (`Ludum_Dare_32_Track_4.wav`) and one hit SFX (`759825__noisyredfox__hitwood.wav`) — played through `flutter_soloud`, with `google_fonts` fetching NES faces at runtime. Repo is **7.2 MB**, head **`c851732`, 2026-08-07, "ci: publish pubspec-fdroid.lock"**, licence **AGPL-3.0** (`LICENSE.md`). Honest limits: the clone carries a **`flutter` git submodule** (`submodules/flutter`, stable branch) that is not initialised, art volume is modest (27 images), audio is a single loop plus one hit sound, and the game is deliberately shallow-loop arcade with no campaign or meta-progression beyond coins. Breakdown: buildability **1.5/2** (a textbook-modern Flutter project — committed `pubspec.lock`, a `BUILD.md` with prerequisites, patches and the exact `flutter build apk` line, 8 platform CI workflows and a golden-image test suite — docked because **no build ran here**: the SDK is absent, the `flutter` submodule is uninitialised, and `flutter ≥ 3.38` is a hard floor); phone target **2.5/3** (phone-first rather than a port: three store listings including F-Droid, an Android/IOS app id under the author's domain, a touch `aim_guide` + drag-to-shoot input layer, portrait-friendly single-screen UI, and only the INTERNET permission — docked because no APK was assembled or installed in this pass and the SDK/API levels are inherited rather than pinned); depth/replay **2/3** (the loop is genuinely clever — perfectly elastic ricochets, ammo as a resource that only *increases* when you kill the marked monsters, HP that scales with score, extra rows per round every 50 points, 20 cosmetics to spend coins on and a save/restore of the full monster grid mid-run — but it is one mechanic, one enemy family, no modes, no daily run, no achievements beyond a high score); polish **1.5/2** (unusually thorough for this roster: slang-generated localisation across 4 locales including Kazakh, an NES theme with a hand-built palette and icon font, 6 unit/golden tests, 8 CI workflows, a FOSS-patch lane that strips IAP, tutorial + settings + game-over screens, and multi-platform packaging directories — docked for the modest art set, single-track audio, and a README that markets the game rather than documents it). **Recommendation: the roster's cleanest example of an *arcade* title that is genuinely maintained — cite it for how a one-mechanic game is polished into a store-quality product (slang i18n, golden tests, FOSS patch lane, 8-platform CI); the liftable parts are `ricochlime_game.dart`'s `createNewRow`/`getNumNewRowsEachRound` difficulty curve and the `restitution: 1` + `aim_guide` shooting model, both small enough to port into a single-file prototype.** Its reported happiness is not evidence — **it was not played in this pass**. To turn the audit into a real result: install Flutter ≥ 3.38 stable, `git submodule update --init --depth 1 submodules/flutter`, `flutter pub get`, `flutter build apk --release`, adb-install on the `pm_pixel` AVD, then confirm one shot ricochets at least twice off walls, that killing the bullet-reward monster adds exactly one shot, and that a shop colour survives a restart. |
